You can start today: twelve of the fifteen sources I shortlisted are open downloads with no application, covering melanoma, urothelial, renal, gastric and small lung cohorts. The large lung cohorts and the biggest renal cohort need a data access application that takes weeks.

Everything was checked on 11 November 2026 (UTC). "Today" in the tables means I confirmed it against the hosting archive; "memory" means I recall it and did not confirm it.

## What I assumed you need
- **R1 (must):** patients actually treated with anti-PD-1/PD-L1 or anti-CTLA-4.
- **R2 (must):** bulk tumour RNA-seq, not arrays or gene panels.
- **R3 (must):** recorded response (RECIST or clinical benefit).
- **R4 (must):** pre-treatment samples.
- **R5 (nice to have):** downloadable now.
- I assumed academic, non-commercial use and that processed expression matrices are acceptable, with raw reads a bonus.

## Shortlist

**Download today**

| # | Cohort | Access | Licence | Size | Fit | Checked |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | IMvigor210: urothelial, atezolizumab | Direct download | ODbL (cBioPortal); CC0 (Zenodo copy) | 347 pre-treatment; 298 with RECIST | Strong | Today |
| 2 | IMmotion150: renal, atezolizumab ± bevacizumab vs sunitinib | Direct download | ODbL | 263 pre-treatment; 174 on atezolizumab | Strong | Today |
| 3 | Liu 2019: melanoma, anti-PD-1 | Direct download | CC0 | 122 pre-treatment, all with RECIST | Strong | Today |
| 4 | Gide 2019: melanoma, anti-PD-1 ± ipilimumab | Direct download, raw reads also open | CC0 | 73 pre-treatment, 18 on-treatment | Strong | Today |
| 5 | Riaz 2017: melanoma, nivolumab | Direct download | GEO open; CC0 | 51 pre-treatment, 56 on-treatment | Strong | Today |
| 6 | Braun 2020: clear cell renal, nivolumab | Direct download | CC0 | About 311 RNA-seq, about 181 on nivolumab (memory) | Strong | Access today; size and labels from memory |
| 7 | Kim 2018: gastric, pembrolizumab | Direct download, raw reads also open | CC BY 4.0 | 78 RNA-seq runs; about 45 patients (memory) | Strong | Access today; labels from memory |
| 8 | Rose 2021: urothelial, mixed checkpoint inhibitors | Direct download | GEO open | 90 samples | Strong | Existence and size today; labels from memory |
| 9 | Hugo 2016: melanoma, anti-PD-1 | Direct download | GEO open; CC BY 4.0 | 28 samples | Partial (small) | Today |
| 10 | Van Allen 2015: melanoma, ipilimumab | Direct download | CC0 | About 40 RNA-seq (memory) | Partial (small) | Access today; labels from memory |
| 11 | Small lung cohorts: Jung, Hwang, Cho | Direct download | GEO open | 27, 21 and 16 samples | Partial (tiny) | Sizes today; labels from memory |
| 12 | PredictIO data archive | Direct download | CC BY 4.0 | One 52 MB archive | Partial (copies of the above) | Today |

**Application required**

| # | Cohort | Access | Licence | Size | Fit | Checked |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| 13 | IMmotion151: renal, atezolizumab + bevacizumab vs sunitinib | Institutional agreement with the Genentech access committee, weeks | Non-commercial, project-specific | 823 tumours | Strong | Today |
| 14 | OAK and POPLAR: lung, atezolizumab vs docetaxel | Access committee application, weeks | Agreement text not read | Several hundred (memory) | Strong | Study today; details from memory |
| 15 | SU2C-MARK: lung, anti-PD-(L)1 | dbGaP Authorized Access, weeks | Lung and thoracic cancer research only | 393 patients, about 152 with RNA-seq | Strong | Today |

## Details

**Three open collections hold most of the cohorts above**
- **[ORCESTRA](https://www.orcestra.ca/clinical_icb)** lists 21 checkpoint cohorts, each as an R `MultiAssayExperiment` file on Zenodo with expression and clinical data.
- **cBioPortal** hosts nine cohorts reprocessed by CRI iAtlas with uniform clinical columns (`RESPONSE`, `CLINICAL_BENEFIT`, `ICI_RX`, `SAMPLE_TREATMENT`). Its FAQ says data are under the ODC Open Database License unless a study notes otherwise, and that you should cite the original study.
- **[PredictIO](https://zenodo.org/records/6142546)** is a single archive of the tables behind the PredictIO meta-analysis ([Bareche 2022](https://doi.org/10.1016/j.annonc.2022.08.084)).

These three overlap heavily, so count each cohort once when you set up cross-study validation.

**Open cohorts**
1. **IMvigor210** is at [cBioPortal](https://www.cbioportal.org/study/summary?id=blca_iatlas_imvigor210_2017) and as [ICB_Mariathasan](https://doi.org/10.5281/zenodo.7332183).
   - Response is 25 CR, 43 PR, 63 SD and 167 PD.
   - The original [Genentech R package](http://research-pub.gene.com/IMvigor210CoreBiologies/) is still up, but the download link in its vignette returned 404 today; the file answered under `packageVersions/IMvigor210CoreBiologies_1.0.0.tar.gz`.
   - Original paper: [Mariathasan 2018](https://doi.org/10.1038/nature25501).
2. **IMmotion150** is at [cBioPortal](https://www.cbioportal.org/study/summary?id=rcc_iatlas_immotion150_2018).
   - Response is 19 CR, 53 PR, 96 SD and 79 PD across all arms.
   - Drop or separate the 89 sunitinib-only patients; about half the atezolizumab patients also had bevacizumab.
3. **Liu 2019** is at [ICB_Liu](https://doi.org/10.5281/zenodo.7332172) and [cBioPortal](https://www.cbioportal.org/study/summary?id=mel_iatlas_liu_2019).
   - 71 patients had pembrolizumab and 51 nivolumab; response is 16 CR, 32 PR, 18 SD and 56 PD.
   - Some patients had prior ipilimumab (`PRIOR_ICI_RX`).
4. **Gide 2019** is at [ICB_Gide](https://doi.org/10.5281/zenodo.7332096) and [cBioPortal](https://www.cbioportal.org/study/summary?id=mel_iatlas_gide_2019).
   - Raw FASTQ is open at [ENA PRJEB23709](https://www.ebi.ac.uk/ena/browser/view/PRJEB23709) (91 runs), so you can reprocess it yourself.
5. **Riaz 2017** is at [GEO GSE91061](https://www.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/geo/query/acc.cgi?acc=GSE91061), [ICB_Riaz](https://doi.org/10.5281/zenodo.7332230) and cBioPortal.
   - More than half the samples are on-treatment, so filter on `SAMPLE_TREATMENT`.
6. **Braun 2020** is at [ICB_Braun](https://doi.org/10.5281/zenodo.7332074).
   - It pools CheckMate 009, 010 and 025; from memory, 025 includes an everolimus arm to exclude, and the tissue is archival.
7. **Kim 2018** is at [ICB_Kim](https://doi.org/10.5281/zenodo.7332153), with raw reads at [ENA PRJEB25780](https://www.ebi.ac.uk/ena/browser/view/PRJEB25780).
8. **Rose 2021** is at [GEO GSE176307](https://www.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/geo/query/acc.cgi?acc=GSE176307).
   - It is in neither collection, which makes it a clean external test for a model trained on IMvigor210.
9. **Hugo 2016** is at [GEO GSE78220](https://www.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/geo/query/acc.cgi?acc=GSE78220) and [ICB_Hugo](https://doi.org/10.5281/zenodo.7332108).
10. **Van Allen 2015** is at [ICB_Van_Allen](https://doi.org/10.5281/zenodo.7332289) and is the main open anti-CTLA-4 cohort.
11. **Small lung cohorts** are at GEO [GSE135222](https://www.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/geo/query/acc.cgi?acc=GSE135222), [GSE136961](https://www.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/geo/query/acc.cgi?acc=GSE136961) and [GSE126044](https://www.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/geo/query/acc.cgi?acc=GSE126044).

Smaller cohorts on cBioPortal that I did not give rows: PRINCE pancreatic (64 pre-treatment, nivolumab plus chemotherapy), Anders triple-negative breast (31), Prins glioblastoma (15 pre-treatment) and Choueiri renal (16).

**Application-only cohorts**

13. **[IMmotion151](https://ega-archive.org/studies/EGAS00001004353)** includes processed log2(TPM+1) for 823 tumours plus outcome data (PFS, ORR).
    - Steps: create an EGA account, request the datasets, e-mail the access committee, and have your institution sign Genentech's agreement.
    - Terms quoted from the agreement: "You agree not to use the data for the creation of products for sale or for any commercial purpose"; "use of the Data for a new purpose or project will require a new application".
    - Collaborators at other institutions apply separately, and the agreement ends after three years.
14. **[OAK and POPLAR](https://ega-archive.org/studies/EGAS00001005013)** is also a Genentech study on EGA, so expect the same route; I did not read this study's agreement.
15. **[SU2C-MARK](https://www.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/projects/gap/cgi-bin/study.cgi?study_id=phs002822)** needs an eRA Commons account and a Data Access Request co-signed by your institution's signing official.
    - Its single consent group restricts use to lung and thoracic cancer research.
    - Two sites' samples are not deposited ([Ravi 2023](https://www.nature.com/articles/s41588-023-01355-5)).

Raw reads for several open cohorts are also controlled: IMvigor210 and IMmotion150 on EGA, and Van Allen and Liu on [dbGaP phs000452](https://www.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/projects/gap/cgi-bin/study.cgi?study_id=phs000452).

## Known, not confirmed
- **Response labels in some open files:** I did not open the files for Braun, Kim, Van Allen, Rose or the small lung cohorts. I recall response being present; check before relying on them.
- **SU2C-MARK processed matrix:** the paper may post expression in its supplementary tables, which would avoid the dbGaP wait. I could not confirm this.
- **TIGER portal** (tiger.canceromics.org): I recall it offering curated immunotherapy expression downloads; the site answered but I could not read its contents.

- **GSE100797 (Lauss):** adoptive T-cell therapy, not checkpoint inhibitors.
- **GSE67501 and GSE173839:** microarrays.

## What I assumed you need
- **R1 (must):** patients actually treated with anti-PD-1/PD-L1 or anti-CTLA-4.
- **R2 (must):** bulk tumour RNA-seq, not arrays or gene panels.
- **R3 (must):** recorded response (RECIST or clinical benefit).
- **R4 (must):** pre-treatment samples.
- **R5 (nice to have):** downloadable now.
- I assumed academic, non-commercial use and that processed expression matrices are acceptable, with raw reads a bonus.

## Shortlist

**Download today**

| # | Cohort | Access | Licence | Size | Fit | Checked |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | IMvigor210: urothelial, atezolizumab | Direct download | ODbL (cBioPortal); CC0 (Zenodo copy) | 347 pre-treatment; 298 with RECIST | Strong | Today |
| 2 | IMmotion150: renal, atezolizumab ± bevacizumab vs sunitinib | Direct download | ODbL | 263 pre-treatment; 174 on atezolizumab | Strong | Today |
| 3 | Liu 2019: melanoma, anti-PD-1 | Direct download | CC0 | 122 pre-treatment, all with RECIST | Strong | Today |
| 4 | Gide 2019: melanoma, anti-PD-1 ± ipilimumab | Direct download, raw reads also open | CC0 | 73 pre-treatment, 18 on-treatment | Strong | Today |
| 5 | Riaz 2017: melanoma, nivolumab | Direct download | GEO open; CC0 | 51 pre-treatment, 56 on-treatment | Strong | Today |
| 6 | Braun 2020: clear cell renal, nivolumab | Direct download | CC0 | About 311 RNA-seq, about 181 on nivolumab (memory) | Strong | Access today; size and labels from memory |
| 7 | Kim 2018: gastric, pembrolizumab | Direct download, raw reads also open | CC BY 4.0 | 78 RNA-seq runs; about 45 patients (memory) | Strong | Access today; labels from memory |
| 8 | Rose 2021: urothelial, mixed checkpoint inhibitors | Direct download | GEO open | 90 samples | Strong | Existence and size today; labels from memory |
| 9 | Hugo 2016: melanoma, anti-PD-1 | Direct download | GEO open; CC BY 4.0 | 28 samples | Partial (small) | Today |
| 10 | Van Allen 2015: melanoma, ipilimumab | Direct download | CC0 | About 40 RNA-seq (memory) | Partial (small) | Access today; labels from memory |
| 11 | Small lung cohorts: Jung, Hwang, Cho | Direct download | GEO open | 27, 21 and 16 samples | Partial (tiny) | Sizes today; labels from memory |

| # | Cohort | Access | Licence | Size | Fit | Checked |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| 13 | IMmotion151: renal, atezolizumab + bevacizumab vs sunitinib | Institutional agreement with the Genentech access committee, weeks | Non-commercial, project-specific | 823 tumours | Strong | Today |
| 14 | OAK and POPLAR: lung, atezolizumab vs docetaxel | Access committee application, weeks | Agreement text not read | Several hundred (memory) | Strong | Study today; details from memory |
| 15 | SU2C-MARK: lung, anti-PD-(L)1 | dbGaP Authorized Access, weeks | Lung and thoracic cancer research only | 393 patients, about 152 with RNA-seq | Strong | Today |

## Details

**Three open collections hold most of the cohorts above**
- **[ORCESTRA](https://www.orcestra.ca/clinical_icb)** lists 21 checkpoint cohorts, each as an R `MultiAssayExperiment` file on Zenodo with expression and clinical data.
- **cBioPortal** hosts nine cohorts reprocessed by CRI iAtlas with uniform clinical columns (`RESPONSE`, `CLINICAL_BENEFIT`, `ICI_RX`, `SAMPLE_TREATMENT`). Its FAQ says data are under the ODC Open Database License unless a study notes otherwise, and that you should cite the original study.
- **[PredictIO](https://zenodo.org/records/6142546)** is a single archive of the tables behind the PredictIO meta-analysis ([Bareche 2022](https://doi.org/10.1016/j.annonc.2022.08.084)).

These three overlap heavily, so count each cohort once when you set up cross-study validation.

**Open cohorts**
1. **IMvigor210** is at [cBioPortal](https://www.cbioportal.org/study/summary?id=blca_iatlas_imvigor210_2017) and as [ICB_Mariathasan](https://doi.org/10.5281/zenodo.7332183).
   - Response is 25 CR, 43 PR, 63 SD and 167 PD.
   - The original [Genentech R package](http://research-pub.gene.com/IMvigor210CoreBiologies/) is still up, but the download link in its vignette returned 404 today; the file answered under `packageVersions/IMvigor210CoreBiologies_1.0.0.tar.gz`.
   - Original paper: [Mariathasan 2018](https://doi.org/10.1038/nature25501).
2. **IMmotion150** is at [cBioPortal](https://www.cbioportal.org/study/summary?id=rcc_iatlas_immotion150_2018).
   - Response is 19 CR, 53 PR, 96 SD and 79 PD across all arms.
   - Drop or separate the 89 sunitinib-only patients; about half the atezolizumab patients also had bevacizumab.
3. **Liu 2019** is at [ICB_Liu](https://doi.org/10.5281/zenodo.7332172) and [cBioPortal](https://www.cbioportal.org/study/summary?id=mel_iatlas_liu_2019).
   - 71 patients had pembrolizumab and 51 nivolumab; response is 16 CR, 32 PR, 18 SD and 56 PD.
   - Some patients had prior ipilimumab (`PRIOR_ICI_RX`).
4. **Gide 2019** is at [ICB_Gide](https://doi.org/10.5281/zenodo.7332096) and [cBioPortal](https://www.cbioportal.org/study/summary?id=mel_iatlas_gide_2019).
   - Raw FASTQ is open at [ENA PRJEB23709](https://www.ebi.ac.uk/ena/browser/view/PRJEB23709) (91 runs), so you can reprocess it yourself.
5. **Riaz 2017** is at [GEO GSE91061](https://www.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/geo/query/acc.cgi?acc=GSE91061), [ICB_Riaz](https://doi.org/10.5281/zenodo.7332230) and cBioPortal.
   - More than half the samples are on-treatment, so filter on `SAMPLE_TREATMENT`.
6. **Braun 2020** is at [ICB_Braun](https://doi.org/10.5281/zenodo.7332074).
   - It pools CheckMate 009, 010 and 025; from memory, 025 includes an everolimus arm to exclude, and the tissue is archival.
7. **Kim 2018** is at [ICB_Kim](https://doi.org/10.5281/zenodo.7332153), with raw reads at [ENA PRJEB25780](https://www.ebi.ac.uk/ena/browser/view/PRJEB25780).
8. **Rose 2021** is at [GEO GSE176307](https://www.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/geo/query/acc.cgi?acc=GSE176307).
   - It is in neither collection, which makes it a clean external test for a model trained on IMvigor210.
9. **Hugo 2016** is at [GEO GSE78220](https://www.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/geo/query/acc.cgi?acc=GSE78220) and [ICB_Hugo](https://doi.org/10.5281/zenodo.7332108).
10. **Van Allen 2015** is at [ICB_Van_Allen](https://doi.org/10.5281/zenodo.7332289) and is the main open anti-CTLA-4 cohort.
11. **Small lung cohorts** are at GEO [GSE135222](https://www.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/geo/query/acc.cgi?acc=GSE135222), [GSE136961](https://www.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/geo/query/acc.cgi?acc=GSE136961) and [GSE126044](https://www.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/geo/query/acc.cgi?acc=GSE126044).

Smaller cohorts on cBioPortal that I did not give rows: PRINCE pancreatic (64 pre-treatment, nivolumab plus chemotherapy), Anders triple-negative breast (31), Prins glioblastoma (15 pre-treatment) and Choueiri renal (16).

**Application-only cohorts**

13. **[IMmotion151](https://ega-archive.org/studies/EGAS00001004353)** includes processed log2(TPM+1) for 823 tumours plus outcome data (PFS, ORR).
    - Steps: create an EGA account, request the datasets, e-mail the access committee, and have your institution sign Genentech's agreement.
    - Terms quoted from the agreement: "You agree not to use the data for the creation of products for sale or for any commercial purpose"; "use of the Data for a new purpose or project will require a new application".
    - Collaborators at other institutions apply separately, and the agreement ends after three years.
14. **[OAK and POPLAR](https://ega-archive.org/studies/EGAS00001005013)** is also a Genentech study on EGA, so expect the same route; I did not read this study's agreement.
15. **[SU2C-MARK](https://www.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/projects/gap/cgi-bin/study.cgi?study_id=phs002822)** needs an eRA Commons account and a Data Access Request co-signed by your institution's signing official.
    - Its single consent group restricts use to lung and thoracic cancer research.
    - Two sites' samples are not deposited ([Ravi 2023](https://www.nature.com/articles/s41588-023-01355-5)).

Raw reads for several open cohorts are also controlled: IMvigor210 and IMmotion150 on EGA, and Van Allen and Liu on [dbGaP phs000452](https://www.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/projects/gap/cgi-bin/study.cgi?study_id=phs000452).

## Known, not confirmed
- **Response labels in some open files:** I did not open the files for Braun, Kim, Van Allen, Rose or the small lung cohorts. I recall response being present; check before relying on them.
- **SU2C-MARK processed matrix:** the paper may post expression in its supplementary tables, which would avoid the dbGaP wait. I could not confirm this.
- **TIGER portal** (tiger.canceromics.org): I recall it offering curated immunotherapy expression downloads; the site answered but I could not read its contents.
- **Hartwig Medical Foundation:** from memory, a large pan-cancer metastatic cohort with RNA-seq and treatment response, available by application. Not checked today.
- **JAVELIN Renal 101** (avelumab plus axitinib): from memory, expression and outcome are in the Nature Medicine paper's supplement. Not checked today.
- **IMvigor210 R package licence:** recalled as CC BY 3.0.

## Not recommended, and why
- **TCGA:** no checkpoint-inhibitor treatment or response recorded.
- **MSK-IMPACT immunotherapy cohorts** (for example the 1,661-patient TMB study): panel DNA only, no RNA-seq.
- **GSE93157 (Prat):** NanoString array, not RNA-seq.
- **GSE100797 (Lauss):** adoptive T-cell therapy, not checkpoint inhibitors.
- **GSE67501 and GSE173839:** microarrays.
- **`TIDE_RESPONDER` and `TIDE_NO_BENEFITS` columns** in the cBioPortal studies: these are model predictions, not outcomes, so do not use them as labels or features.

## What I could not find
- No open lung cohort above about 30 patients.
- Open anti-CTLA-4-only RNA-seq is limited to small melanoma cohorts.
- The general dataset registries were nearly useless here. The cohorts came from GEO, ENA, cBioPortal, ORCESTRA, EGA and dbGaP directly, so cohorts published only on project sites may be missing.

## Next step
Start with rows 1–8, which give roughly 1,200 pre-treatment tumours across four cancer types. If lung matters, submit the SU2C-MARK and OAK/POPLAR applications now, since they take weeks. Response definitions differ between cohorts (RECIST categories versus clinical benefit), so fix one binarisation before pooling.

The interactive shortlist, with filtering and an exportable access plan, is at `/private/tmp/e-lfJeLe/home/cwd/dataset-shortlist.html`. GEO, cBioPortal and ENA entries show as "not verified" there because the page verifier cannot read those sites; I confirmed them through each site's API instead.

You can assemble roughly ten independent cohorts with open processed expression and response labels today, and the biggest trial cohorts (lung, kidney, liver) need an application. I checked the accessions and counts below against GEO, ENA, EGA and the cBioPortal API; items I could not check are marked "from memory".

## Download right away

**Start with the CRI iAtlas harmonised studies on cBioPortal** (no login, via the web, API or datahub). Each has gene-level expression plus `RESPONSE` (RECIST category), `RESPONDER`, `CLINICAL_BENEFIT` and `SAMPLE_TREATMENT` (pre/on) fields.

| Cohort (cBioPortal study ID) | Cancer | Drug | RNA samples | Notes |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| IMvigor210 (`blca_iatlas_imvigor210_2017`) | Urothelial | Atezolizumab | 347, all pre | 298 with response; also in the Genentech `IMvigor210CoreBiologies` R package |
| IMmotion150 (`rcc_iatlas_immotion150_2018`) | Renal | Atezolizumab ± bevacizumab | 263, all pre | 89 are sunitinib-only; drop them |
| Liu 2019 (`mel_iatlas_liu_2019`) | Melanoma | Pembrolizumab or nivolumab | 122, all pre | Also as `mel_dfci_2019` (same patients) |
| Gide 2019 (`mel_iatlas_gide_2019`) | Melanoma | Anti-PD-1 ± ipilimumab | 91 (73 pre) | FASTQ open at ENA PRJEB23709 |
| Riaz 2017 (`mel_iatlas_riaz_nivolumab_2017`) | Melanoma | Nivolumab | 107 (51 pre) | GEO GSE91061 |
| Hugo 2016 (`mel_iatlas_hugo_ucla_2016`) | Melanoma | Pembrolizumab | 27 (26 pre) | GEO GSE78220; also as `mel_ucla_2016` |
| Van Allen 2015 (`skcm_dfci_2015`) | Melanoma | Ipilimumab | about 40 with RNA (from memory) | Your main anti-CTLA-4 cohort; not iAtlas-harmonised |
| PRINCE (`paad_iatlas_prince_2022`) | Pancreatic | Chemo ± nivolumab ± sotigalimab | 93 (64 pre) | 24 samples had no ICI |
| Anders 2022 (`brca_iatlas_anders_2022`) | Triple-negative breast | Pembrolizumab + cyclophosphamide | 31, all pre | |
| Choueiri 2016 (`ccrcc_iatlas_choueiri_2016`) | Renal | Nivolumab | 16, all pre | |
| Prins 2019 (`gbm_iatlas_prins_2019`) | Glioblastoma | Neoadjuvant pembrolizumab | 30 (15 pre) | No responders recorded |

**Open cohorts in GEO, ENA or SRA.** I confirmed these records and sample counts, but not where the response labels sit; they may be in the paper supplement instead of the series metadata.

- **Urothelial:** GSE176307, 90 samples, mixed ICI.
- **Gastric:** ENA PRJEB25780 (Kim 2018, pembrolizumab), 78 RNA-seq runs.
- **Lung (NSCLC):** GSE135222 (27), GSE136961 (21) and GSE126044 (16), all anti-PD-1.
- **Melanoma:** GSE115821 (37) and GSE168204 (27); both mix pre- and on-treatment samples.
- **Glioblastoma:** SRA PRJNA482620 (Zhao 2019), 34 RNA-seq runs.
- **Thymic carcinoma:** GSE181815 (30, pembrolizumab).
- **Head and neck / lung:** GSE159067 (102) and GSE161537 (82). I believe these are targeted panels, not whole transcriptome; check before using.

**Open in paper supplements (from memory, not checked):**
- Braun 2020 (*Nat Med*), renal CheckMate 009/010/025: normalised expression for about 311 patients, of whom about 181 received nivolumab.
- JAVELIN Renal 101 (Motzer 2020, *Nat Med*): avelumab + axitinib, about 700 patients.

**Curated bundles:** ORCESTRA (orcestra.ca/clinical_icb, from the PredictIO project) and TIGER (tiger.canceromics.org) repackage many of the above. I confirmed the sites are live but not their contents.

## Need an application

| Cohort | Cancer / drug | Accession | Route |
|---|---|---|---|
| OAK + POPLAR (Patil 2022) | Lung, atezolizumab vs docetaxel; about 890 RNA-seq (from memory) | EGAS00001005013 | Genentech committee via EGA |
| IMmotion151 | Renal, atezolizumab + bevacizumab; about 820 (from memory) | EGAS00001004353 | Same |
| IMbrave150 / GO30140 | Liver, atezolizumab + bevacizumab | EGAS00001005503 | Same |
| IMvigor210 and IMmotion150 raw data | Urothelial, renal | EGAS00001002556, EGAS00001002928 | Same; only needed for raw reads |
| Braun renal raw data | Renal | EGAS00001004290–92 | EGA |
| Van Allen / Liu melanoma raw data | Melanoma | dbGaP phs000452 (from memory) | dbGaP, institutional sign-off |
| SU2C-MARK lung (Ravi 2023) | Lung, about 150 RNA-seq | dbGaP (accession not confirmed) | dbGaP |
| Hartwig Medical Foundation | Pan-cancer metastatic, with treatment and response | — | Data access request |

Expect weeks to months for EGA and dbGaP. OAK/POPLAR is the one worth applying for now, because open lung data is otherwise small.

## Before you train

- **Filter to pre-treatment samples and ICI arms.** Riaz, Gide, PRINCE and Prins include on-treatment biopsies; IMmotion150 and PRINCE include non-ICI patients.
- **Don't double-count.** Hugo and Liu each appear twice in cBioPortal, and the bundles repackage the same cohorts.
- **Expression units differ** (TPM, log TPM, upper-quartile counts). For a uniform pipeline, reprocess from the open FASTQs (Gide, Kim, Riaz, Hugo, Zhao).
- **Decide how to treat stable disease.** `RESPONDER` is CR/PR only; `CLINICAL_BENEFIT` also counts stable disease.
- **Melanoma dominates the open data**, so hold out whole cohorts in other cancer types (IMvigor210, IMmotion150, Kim) for validation.

| IMbrave150 / GO30140 | Liver, atezolizumab + bevacizumab | EGAS00001005503 | Same |
| IMvigor210 and IMmotion150 raw data | Urothelial, renal | EGAS00001002556, EGAS00001002928 | Same; only needed for raw reads |
| Braun renal raw data | Renal | EGAS00001004290–92 | EGA |
| Van Allen / Liu melanoma raw data | Melanoma | dbGaP phs000452 (from memory) | dbGaP, institutional sign-off |
| SU2C-MARK lung (Ravi 2023) | Lung, about 150 RNA-seq | dbGaP (accession not confirmed) | dbGaP |
| Hartwig Medical Foundation | Pan-cancer metastatic, with treatment and response | — | Data access request |

Expect weeks to months for EGA and dbGaP. OAK/POPLAR is the one worth applying for now, because open lung data is otherwise small.

## Before you train

- **Filter to pre-treatment samples and ICI arms.** Riaz, Gide, PRINCE and Prins include on-treatment biopsies; IMmotion150 and PRINCE include non-ICI patients.
- **Don't double-count.** Hugo and Liu each appear twice in cBioPortal, and the bundles repackage the same cohorts.
- **Expression units differ** (TPM, log TPM, upper-quartile counts). For a uniform pipeline, reprocess from the open FASTQs (Gide, Kim, Riaz, Hugo, Zhao).
- **Decide how to treat stable disease.** `RESPONDER` is CR/PR only; `CLINICAL_BENEFIT` also counts stable disease.
- **Melanoma dominates the open data**, so hold out whole cohorts in other cancer types (IMvigor210, IMmotion150, Kim) for validation.

Evidence (what the judge was shown; for an image, a description of what was sent)
You can assemble roughly ten independent cohorts with open processed expression and response labels today, and the biggest trial cohorts (lung, kidney, liver) need an application. ## Download right away

**Start with the CRI iAtlas harmonised studies on cBioPortal** (no login, via the web, API or datahub). Each has gene-level expression plus `RESPONSE` (RECIST category), `RESPONDER`, `CLINICAL_BENEFIT` and `SAMPLE_TREATMENT` (pre/on) fields.

| Cohort (cBioPortal study ID) | Cancer | Drug | RNA samples | Notes |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| IMvigor210 (`blca_iatlas_imvigor210_2017`) | Urothelial | Atezolizumab | 347, all pre | 298 with response; also in the Genentech `IMvigor210CoreBiologies` R package |
| IMmotion150 (`rcc_iatlas_immotion150_2018`) | Renal | Atezolizumab ± bevacizumab | 263, all pre | 89 are sunitinib-only; drop them |
| Liu 2019 (`mel_iatlas_liu_2019`) | Melanoma | Pembrolizumab or nivolumab | 122, all pre | Also as `mel_dfci_2019` (same patients) |
| Gide 2019 (`mel_iatlas_gide_2019`) | Melanoma | Anti-PD-1 ± ipilimumab | 91 (73 pre) | FASTQ open at ENA PRJEB23709 |
| Riaz 2017 (`mel_iatlas_riaz_nivolumab_2017`) | Melanoma | Nivolumab | 107 (51 pre) | GEO GSE91061 |
| Hugo 2016 (`mel_iatlas_hugo_ucla_2016`) | Melanoma | Pembrolizumab | 27 (26 pre) | GEO GSE78220; also as `mel_ucla_2016` |
| Van Allen 2015 (`skcm_dfci_2015`) | Melanoma | Ipilimumab | about 40 with RNA (from memory) | Your main anti-CTLA-4 cohort; not iAtlas-harmonised |
| PRINCE (`paad_iatlas_prince_2022`) | Pancreatic | Chemo ± nivolumab ± sotigalimab | 93 (64 pre) | 24 samples had no ICI |
| Anders 2022 (`brca_iatlas_anders_2022`) | Triple-negative breast | Pembrolizumab + cyclophosphamide | 31, all pre | |
| Choueiri 2016 (`ccrcc_iatlas_choueiri_2016`) | Renal | Nivolumab | 16, all pre | |
| Prins 2019 (`gbm_iatlas_prins_2019`) | Glioblastoma | Neoadjuvant pembrolizumab | 30 (15 pre) | No responders recorded |

**Open cohorts in GEO, ENA or SRA.** I confirmed these records and sample counts, but not where the response labels sit; they may be in the paper supplement instead of the series metadata.

- **Urothelial:** GSE176307, 90 samples, mixed ICI.
- **Gastric:** ENA PRJEB25780 (Kim 2018, pembrolizumab), 78 RNA-seq runs.
- **Lung (NSCLC):** GSE135222 (27), GSE136961 (21) and GSE126044 (16), all anti-PD-1.
- **Melanoma:** GSE115821 (37) and GSE168204 (27); both mix pre- and on-treatment samples.
- **Glioblastoma:** SRA PRJNA482620 (Zhao 2019), 34 RNA-seq runs.
- **Thymic carcinoma:** GSE181815 (30, pembrolizumab).
- **Head and neck / lung:** GSE159067 (102) and GSE161537 (82). I believe these are targeted panels, not whole transcriptome; check before using.

**Open in paper supplements (from memory, not checked):**
- Braun 2020 (*Nat Med*), renal CheckMate 009/010/025: normalised expression for about 311 patients, of whom about 181 received nivolumab.
- JAVELIN Renal 101 (Motzer 2020, *Nat Med*): avelumab + axitinib, about 700 patients.

**Curated bundles:** ORCESTRA (orcestra.ca/clinical_icb, from the PredictIO project) and TIGER (tiger.canceromics.org) repackage many of the above. I confirmed the sites are live but not their contents.

| Anders 2022 (`brca_iatlas_anders_2022`) | Triple-negative breast | Pembrolizumab + cyclophosphamide | 31, all pre | |
| Choueiri 2016 (`ccrcc_iatlas_choueiri_2016`) | Renal | Nivolumab | 16, all pre | |
| Prins 2019 (`gbm_iatlas_prins_2019`) | Glioblastoma | Neoadjuvant pembrolizumab | 30 (15 pre) | No responders recorded |

**Open cohorts in GEO, ENA or SRA.** I confirmed these records and sample counts, but not where the response labels sit; they may be in the paper supplement instead of the series metadata.

- **Urothelial:** GSE176307, 90 samples, mixed ICI.
- **Gastric:** ENA PRJEB25780 (Kim 2018, pembrolizumab), 78 RNA-seq runs.
- **Lung (NSCLC):** GSE135222 (27), GSE136961 (21) and GSE126044 (16), all anti-PD-1.
- **Melanoma:** GSE115821 (37) and GSE168204 (27); both mix pre- and on-treatment samples.
- **Glioblastoma:** SRA PRJNA482620 (Zhao 2019), 34 RNA-seq runs.
- **Thymic carcinoma:** GSE181815 (30, pembrolizumab).
- **Head and neck / lung:** GSE159067 (102) and GSE161537 (82). I believe these are targeted panels, not whole transcriptome; check before using.

**Open in paper supplements (from memory, not checked):**
- Braun 2020 (*Nat Med*), renal CheckMate 009/010/025: normalised expression for about 311 patients, of whom about 181 received nivolumab.
- JAVELIN Renal 101 (Motzer 2020, *Nat Med*): avelumab + axitinib, about 700 patients.

**Curated bundles:** ORCESTRA (orcestra.ca/clinical_icb, from the PredictIO project) and TIGER (tiger.canceromics.org) repackage many of the above. I confirmed the sites are live but not their contents.

## Need an application

| Cohort | Cancer / drug | Accession | Route |
|---|---|---|---|
| OAK + POPLAR (Patil 2022) | Lung, atezolizumab vs docetaxel; about 890 RNA-seq (from memory) | EGAS00001005013 | Genentech committee via EGA |
| IMmotion151 | Renal, atezolizumab + bevacizumab; about 820 (from memory) | EGAS00001004353 | Same |
| IMbrave150 / GO30140 | Liver, atezolizumab + bevacizumab | EGAS00001005503 | Same |
| IMvigor210 and IMmotion150 raw data | Urothelial, renal | EGAS00001002556, EGAS00001002928 | Same; only needed for raw reads |
| Braun renal raw data | Renal | EGAS00001004290–92 | EGA |
| Van Allen / Liu melanoma raw data | Melanoma | dbGaP phs000452 (from memory) | dbGaP, institutional sign-off |
| SU2C-MARK lung (Ravi 2023) | Lung, about 150 RNA-seq | dbGaP (accession not confirmed) | dbGaP |
| Hartwig Medical Foundation | Pan-cancer metastatic, with treatment and response | — | Data access request |

Expect weeks to months for EGA and dbGaP. OAK/POPLAR is the one worth applying for now, because open lung data is otherwise small.

## Before you train

- **Filter to pre-treatment samples and ICI arms.** Riaz, Gide, PRINCE and Prins include on-treatment biopsies; IMmotion150 and PRINCE include non-ICI patients.
- **Don't double-count.** Hugo and Liu each appear twice in cBioPortal, and the bundles repackage the same cohorts.
- **Expression units differ** (TPM, log TPM, upper-quartile counts). For a uniform pipeline, reprocess from the open FASTQs (Gide, Kim, Riaz, Hugo, Zhao).
- **Decide how to treat stable disease.** `RESPONDER` is CR/PR only; `CLINICAL_BENEFIT` also counts stable disease.
- **Melanoma dominates the open data**, so hold out whole cohorts in other cancer types (IMvigor210, IMmotion150, Kim) for validation.

| Du 2021 | Melanoma, anti-PD-1 | 27 (pre and on-treatment) | GEO GSE168204 |
| Auslander 2018 | Melanoma, anti-PD-1/CTLA-4 | 37 (pre and on-treatment) | GEO GSE115821 |

Other open sets are a worse fit:

- **PRINCE (pancreatic, 93):** nivolumab is given with chemotherapy, so response is not attributable to the checkpoint inhibitor alone (cBioPortal `paad_iatlas_prince_2022`).
- **Cloughesy/Prins glioblastoma (GSE121810, 29):** neoadjuvant design, so much of the tissue is post-treatment.
- **Prat 2017 (GSE93157, 65):** NanoString panel, not RNA-seq.
- **Braun 2020 (renal, nivolumab, CheckMate 009/010/025, about 300 with RNA):** I recall normalised expression and response being in the Nature Medicine supplementary tables, but I did not check this.

## Curated collections

These repackage many of the cohorts above with harmonised response labels, which saves a lot of clinical-table wrangling:

- **ORCESTRA / PredictIO** (orcestra.ca/clinical_icb): each cohort as an R `SummarizedExperiment` with a common response definition.
- **CRI iAtlas**: the source of the "iAtlas Harmonized" cBioPortal studies above, uniformly reprocessed.
- **TIGER** (tiger.canceromics.org) and **TIDE** (tide.dfci.harvard.edu): downloadable processed matrices with responder labels. The TIDE download page returned a 403 to my script, so check it in a browser.

Because these overlap heavily with each other and with the primary sources, de-duplicate by original study before counting independent cohorts.

## Apply for access

| Cohort | Cancer, drug | Size | Access |
|---|---|---|---|
| OAK and POPLAR (Patil 2022) | NSCLC, atezolizumab vs docetaxel | about 700 and 190 with RNA | EGA EGAS00001005013, Genentech committee |
| IMmotion151 (Motzer 2020) | Renal, atezolizumab + bevacizumab | about 800 | EGA EGAS00001004353 |
| SU2C-MARK (Ravi 2023) | NSCLC, anti-PD-1/PD-L1 | about 150 with RNA | dbGaP phs002822 |
| Braun 2020 raw data | Renal, nivolumab | about 300 | EGA EGAS00001004290 to 4292 |
| Miao 2018 | Renal, anti-PD-1 | about 30 with RNA | dbGaP phs001493 |
| Raw reads for Van Allen and Liu | Melanoma | as above | dbGaP phs000452 |
| Raw reads for IMvigor210 | Urothelial | as above | EGA EGAS00001002556 |

dbGaP typically takes a few weeks through your institution's signing official. EGA committees for company trials are slower and may restrict use to non-commercial research.

## Things that will affect your classifier

- **Pre-treatment filtering:** Riaz, Gide, Du and Auslander mix pre- and on-treatment biopsies, so filter on the sample annotation before training.
- **Response definitions differ:** most use RECIST best response, but Liu, Jung and Hwang lean on clinical benefit or progression-free survival thresholds. Decide how to handle stable disease up front.
- **Prior therapy:** part of Riaz and Liu had progressed on ipilimumab before anti-PD-1, which shifts both biology and response rate.
- **Processing differs:** the cohorts span poly-A, total and capture-based RNA-seq libraries, including FFPE material, and the deposited matrices use different units. Re-quantifying from FASTQ is only possible for Gide, Kim, Zhao and the GEO sets with SRA runs.

A practical starting design is to train on melanoma (Liu, Gide, Riaz, Hugo, Van Allen) and hold out IMvigor210, IMmotion150, Kim and the two Foy cohorts as cross-cancer validation.

- **Pre-treatment filtering:** Riaz, Gide, Du and Auslander mix pre- and on-treatment biopsies, so filter on the sample annotation before training.
- **Response definitions differ:** most use RECIST best response, but Liu, Jung and Hwang lean on clinical benefit or progression-free survival thresholds. Decide how to handle stable disease up front.
- **Prior therapy:** part of Riaz and Liu had progressed on ipilimumab before anti-PD-1, which shifts both biology and response rate.
- **Processing differs:** the cohorts span poly-A, total and capture-based RNA-seq libraries, including FFPE material, and the deposited matrices use different units. Re-quantifying from FASTQ is only possible for Gide, Kim, Zhao and the GEO sets with SRA runs.

A practical starting design is to train on melanoma (Liu, Gide, Riaz, Hugo, Van Allen) and hold out IMvigor210, IMmotion150, Kim and the two Foy cohorts as cross-cancer validation.

Other open sets are a worse fit:

- **PRINCE (pancreatic, 93):** nivolumab is given with chemotherapy, so response is not attributable to the checkpoint inhibitor alone (cBioPortal `paad_iatlas_prince_2022`).
- **Cloughesy/Prins glioblastoma (GSE121810, 29):** neoadjuvant design, so much of the tissue is post-treatment.
- **Prat 2017 (GSE93157, 65):** NanoString panel, not RNA-seq.
- **Braun 2020 (renal, nivolumab, CheckMate 009/010/025, about 300 with RNA):** I recall normalised expression and response being in the Nature Medicine supplementary tables, but I did not check this.

## Curated collections

These repackage many of the cohorts above with harmonised response labels, which saves a lot of clinical-table wrangling:

- **ORCESTRA / PredictIO** (orcestra.ca/clinical_icb): each cohort as an R `SummarizedExperiment` with a common response definition.
- **CRI iAtlas**: the source of the "iAtlas Harmonized" cBioPortal studies above, uniformly reprocessed.
- **TIGER** (tiger.canceromics.org) and **TIDE** (tide.dfci.harvard.edu): downloadable processed matrices with responder labels. The TIDE download page returned a 403 to my script, so check it in a browser.

Because these overlap heavily with each other and with the primary sources, de-duplicate by original study before counting independent cohorts.

Prompt

I'm a bioinformatics postdoc building a classifier that predicts response to immune checkpoint inhibitors (anti-PD-1/PD-L1 or anti-CTLA-4) from pre-treatment tumour RNA-seq. I need cohorts of patients who actually received checkpoint inhibitors, with bulk expression data and a recorded clinical response (RECIST or clinical benefit), ideally several independent cohorts so I can validate across studies. Any cancer type is fine. Where can I get these, and which ones can I download right away versus having to apply for?

01-recall-imvigor210llm

FAIL if the answer does not mention it, or mentions it only to say it is unsuitable.

PASS if the final answer recommends or lists the Riaz et al. 2017 melanoma nivolumab cohort (also known as the Riaz cohort, "Tumor and Microenvironment Evolution during Immunotherapy with Nivolumab", samples labelled BMS038109, GEO accession GSE91061, with pre- and on-treatment RNA-seq) as a candidate dataset for the user's question. FAIL if the answer does not mention it, or mentions it only to say it is unsuitable.

03-recall-hugo2016llm

PASS if the final answer recommends or lists the Hugo et al. 2016 anti-PD-1 melanoma cohort (also known as the Hugo cohort, "Genomic and Transcriptomic Features of Response to Anti-PD-1 Therapy in Metastatic Melanoma", GEO accession GSE78220, 28 pre-treatment tumours from anti-PD-1-treated patients) as a candidate dataset for the user's question. FAIL if the answer does not mention it, or mentions it only to say it is unsuitable.

FAIL if the answer does not mention it, or mentions it only to say it is unsuitable.

05-access-riaz-geollm

PASS if the final answer mentions the Riaz et al. 2017 nivolumab melanoma cohort (GSE91061) and states, or clearly conveys, that its expression data can be downloaded directly from GEO without any application or data access request (the GEO series includes processed expression files and per-sample response annotations). FAIL if the answer does not mention the Riaz cohort or GSE91061 at all, or says or implies that its expression data require a controlled-access application (for example through dbGaP or EGA) before they can be used.

06-access-imvigor210-packagellm

PASS if the final answer mentions IMvigor210 and states, or clearly conveys, that its processed gene expression data and clinical response annotations can be obtained without an application, through the freely downloadable IMvigor210CoreBiologies R package from Genentech's research-pub site (the package ships under a Creative Commons Attribution licence; mentioning the licence is optional, and other equivalent routes to the same processed data also pass). Adding that the raw sequencing data sit at the European Genome-phenome Archive (EGAS00001002556) under controlled access is correct and does not cause a fail. FAIL if the answer does not mention IMvigor210 at all, or says or implies that IMvigor210 expression data can only be obtained through a controlled-access application (for example only via EGA), or gives no indication of how to obtain it.

07-access-gide-enallm

PASS if the final answer mentions the Gide et al. 2019 melanoma cohort (PRJEB23709) and states, or clearly conveys, that its RNA-seq reads are publicly downloadable from the European Nucleotide Archive (ENA/EBI) without an application or data access request. Saying it is openly available from ENA is enough. FAIL if the answer does not mention the Gide cohort or PRJEB23709 at all, or says or implies that its sequencing data require a controlled-access application.

08-long-tailllm

PASS if the final answer recommends at least one of the following as a candidate data source: (a) the Kim et al. 2018 metastatic gastric cancer pembrolizumab cohort ("Comprehensive molecular characterization of clinical responses to PD-1 inhibition in metastatic gastric cancer", Nature Medicine; ENA accession PRJEB25780); (b) TIGER (Tumor Immunotherapy Gene Expression Resource, a web portal compiling immunotherapy-treated expression cohorts). FAIL if it recommends neither of them.

✓ pass 01-recall-mimic-cxr

judge votes: PASS PASS PASS

Judge votes✓ ✓ ✓
Evidence (what the judge was shown; for an image, a description of what was sent)
Four English datasets and four non-English ones release the report text itself. Two of the English ones (ReXGradient-160K and IU X-Ray) you can download today, and MIMIC-CXR is the only one that needs a multi-day credentialing process. Everything below was checked on 11 November 2026 (UTC) unless marked "from memory".

## What I assumed you need
- **R1 (must):** the radiologist's free-text report is released, not only labels derived from it.
- **R2 (must):** chest radiographs are linked to the report at image or study level.
- **R3 (nice):** large enough to train, or at least evaluate, a generation model.
- **R4 (nice):** English; non-English is fine for the secondary experiment.
- **R5 (nice):** obtainable quickly.
- I assumed academic, non-commercial use; almost every licence below forbids commercial use.

## Shortlist
| # | Dataset | Access | Licence | Size | Fit | Checked |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | MIMIC-CXR | Application: credentialing, training and signed agreement; days to ~2 weeks | PhysioNet Credentialed Health Data License 1.5.0 | 227,835 studies, 377,110 images | Strong, English | today (steps partly from memory) |
| 2 | CheXpert Plus | Account and research use agreement on Stanford's Redivis; probably same day | Stanford research use agreement, non-commercial | 187,711 studies, 223,462 pairs | Strong, English | today (approval mechanics not confirmed) |
| 3 | ReXGradient-160K | Hugging Face login, accept terms, automatic; minutes | Harvard non-commercial agreement | 160,000 studies, 273,004 images | Strong, English | today |
| 4 | IU X-Ray (Open-i) | Direct download, no login; minutes | CC BY-NC-ND 4.0 per secondary sources | ~4,000 reports, ~8,000 images | Strong but small, English | links today; licence not confirmed |
| 5 | CASIA-CXR | Request form and hand-signed agreement; weeks. Sample open now | Custom, academic only | 11,111 radiographs | Strong, French | today |
| 6 | PadChest-GR | Request form under research use agreement; days | PadChest research use agreement | 4,555 studies | Partial, Spanish and English sentences | today |
| 7 | BIMCV-COVID19+ | Request form, or IEEE DataPort login; days | BIMCV research use agreement | 21,342 CR and 34,829 DX studies | Partial, Spanish | today (report format from memory) |
| 8 | PadChest | Request form; days. 167 MB sample open | PadChest research use agreement | 160,000+ images | Partial, Spanish, stemmed text | today |
| 9 | REFLACX | PhysioNet login and agreement; images need MIMIC-CXR | PhysioNet Restricted Health Data License | ~3,000 reports (from memory) | Partial, English | today |

## Details

**1. [MIMIC-CXR](https://physionet.org/content/mimic-cxr/2.1.0/)** (v2.1.0)
- **Contents:** one free-text report per study, "stored in individual text files named using the anonymous study identifier", plus the DICOM images (5.1 TB).
- **Steps:**
  1. Create a PhysioNet account.
  2. Apply for credentialed access; from memory, this asks for a reference such as a supervisor.
  3. Complete the required training, "CITI Data or Specimens Only Research" (a few hours, from memory).
  4. Sign the Credentialed Health Data Use Agreement 1.5.0 on the project page.
  5. For JPG images, sign the same agreement on [MIMIC-CXR-JPG](https://physionet.org/content/mimic-cxr-jpg/2.1.0/) (612 GB).
- **Conditions:**
  - Access is personal, so each team member applies separately and the data cannot be shared.
  - The agreement requires that "any publication which makes use of the data will also make the relevant code available".
  - PhysioNet [forbids sending the data to online AI services](https://physionet.org/news/post/llm-responsible-use/) unless zero retention is verified, which matters if you plan LLM-based preprocessing or evaluation.
- **Used in:** [the data descriptor](https://doi.org/10.1038/s41597-019-0322-0) and [a warm-starting report generation baseline](https://doi.org/10.1016/j.artmed.2023.102633).

**2. [CheXpert Plus](https://aimi.stanford.edu/datasets/chexpert-plus)**
- **Contents:** "223,462 unique pairs of radiology reports and chest X-rays" from 64,725 Stanford patients, with reports split into 11 sections and images in DICOM. The Redivis record is 6.76 TB.
- **Steps:** follow "Download here" to [Stanford's Redivis page](https://doi.org/10.57761/fzna-pm76), sign in, and accept the Stanford University Dataset Research Use Agreement. I could not confirm whether approval is automatic.
- **Licence:** research only, no commercial use, no redistribution, and each colleague registers individually (from the [AIMI portal listing](https://stanfordaimi.azurewebsites.net/datasets/5158c524-d3ab-4e02-96e9-6ee9efc110a1) and the paper).
- **Caveat (from memory):** many reports have only an Impression section, with Findings present for a minority. Check section coverage before fixing your target text.
- **Used in:** [a small multimodal radiology model](https://doi.org/10.1038/s41467-025-58344-x) and [a dual-stage report generation framework](https://doi.org/10.1609/aaai.v40i40.40688).

**3. [ReXGradient-160K](https://huggingface.co/datasets/rajpurkarlab/ReXGradient-160K)**
- **Contents:** studies from "109,487 unique patients across 3 U.S. health systems (79 medical sites)", each report with Indication, Comparison, Findings and Impression. Splits are 140,000 train, 10,000 validation and 10,000 public test.
- **Steps:** log in to Hugging Face and accept the agreement on the dataset page; access is granted automatically. The download is 156 GB.
- **Conditions:**
  - Non-commercial research only, with no third-party sharing.
  - You may download only the portion your project needs.
  - Images are PNGs downsampled to 25% of original dimensions.
- **Caveat:** the card does not state the language; I assumed English from the US sites. It was released in 2025, so there are few published baselines.

**4. [IU X-Ray / Open-i](https://openi.nlm.nih.gov/faq)**
- **Contents:** XML reports with findings and impression; a paper found today counts 3,996 reports and 8,121 images.
- **Steps:** download [the reports archive](https://openi.nlm.nih.gov/imgs/collections/NLMCXR_reports.tgz) (1.1 MB) and [the PNG archive](https://openi.nlm.nih.gov/imgs/collections/NLMCXR_png.tgz) (1.36 GB). Both links answered today without a login.
- **Licence:** secondary sources report CC BY-NC-ND 4.0, but the official FAQ could not be read automatically, so confirm it in a browser.
- **Caveat:** de-identification replaced many words with "XXXX". It is too small to train on alone and best used as a second benchmark.

**5. [CASIA-CXR](https://www.casia-cxr.net/)** (French)
- **Contents:** "narrative reports originally written in French" with Indication, Comparison, Findings and Impression fields; 11,111 radiographs per the [dataset paper](https://doi.org/10.1016/j.neucom.2024.128478).
- **Steps:** download the ~80 MB sample from the [downloads page](https://casia-cxr.net/downloads.html), fill in the request form, then hand-sign the data use agreement. Turnaround is not stated.
- **Conditions:** academic researchers only and no redistribution. Also, "You may not translate the radiographic reports… to any other languages without prior written permission", which rules out translating to English for a pooled experiment unless you ask.
- **Caveat:** cases were sampled from five disease categories, so the mix is not a natural hospital distribution.

**6. [PadChest-GR](https://bimcv.cipf.es/bimcv-projects/padchest-gr/)** (Spanish and English)
- **Contents:** 4,555 studies with complete lists of positive and negative finding sentences in both languages, plus bounding boxes (46 GB).
- **Fit:** the sentences were extracted from the Spanish reports and translated with GPT-4, so this is not the verbatim report. It works well as a bilingual evaluation set.
- **Steps:** read the [research use agreement](https://bimcv.cipf.es/bimcv-projects/padchest/padchest-dataset-research-use-agreement/), then submit the request form on the project page.
- **Used in:** [the NEJM AI dataset paper](https://doi.org/10.1056/aidbp2401120).

**7. [BIMCV-COVID19+](https://bimcv.cipf.es/bimcv-projects/bimcv-covid19/)** (Spanish)
- **Contents:** the page says the data include "informes radiográficos". That they are verbatim free text is from memory, so check the data descriptor first.
- **Steps:** submit the request form for the release you want, or log in to [IEEE DataPort](https://doi.org/10.21227/w3aw-rv39). The two pages describe access differently; the BIMCV page is the owner's.
- **Caveat:** COVID-positive patients only, CT studies are mixed in, and the archives run to hundreds of GB.

**8. [PadChest](https://bimcv.cipf.es/bimcv-projects/padchest/)** (Spanish)
- **Fit:** the report field is "preprocessed, words are stemmed and tokenized" (for example "compar con estudi previ 2010 sin identific cambi signific"), per the [authors' repository](https://github.com/auriml/Rx-thorax-automatic-captioning). That makes it a poor target for readable report generation.
- **Steps:** same request form and agreement as PadChest-GR; the full set is 1.02 TB. Inspect the 167 MB sample first.

**9. [REFLACX](https://physionet.org/content/reflacx-xray-localization/1.0.0/)**
- **Contents:** reports that radiologists dictated afresh for MIMIC-CXR frontal images while eye-tracked. It is useful as a second reference report, not as training data.
- **Steps:** the reports need only a PhysioNet login and a signed agreement, but the images come from MIMIC-CXR, which needs credentialing.

## Known, not confirmed
- **[CANDID-PTX](https://doi.org/10.17608/k6.auckland.14173982.v2)** (New Zealand, English): 19,237 radiographs with anonymised free-text reports, per the [RSNA paper](https://pubs.rsna.org/doi/full/10.1148/ryai.2021210136).
  - The original route was an online ethics course plus a signed agreement emailed to the author.
  - One catalogue copy of the description says external users can no longer obtain it after New Zealand's 2024 data governance changes.
  - Figshare blocked automated reading, so open the record in a browser before planning around it.
- **[Naija-CXR](https://zenodo.org/records/21781842)** (Nigeria): an open 389 MB download listing "Free radiologist reports", but it gives no image or report counts and I did not inspect the contents. The record says CC BY 4.0 while the description says non-commercial only.
- **[Interpret-CXR](https://huggingface.co/datasets/StanfordAIMI/interpret-cxr-test-public)** (RRG24 challenge): the public test set exists behind an automatic Hugging Face gate. From memory, the training pool combines CheXpert Plus, PadChest, BIMCV-COVID19 and IU X-Ray, and you add MIMIC-CXR yourself; I did not confirm this.

## Not recommended, and why
- **CheXpert (original), NIH ChestX-ray14, VinDr-CXR:** labels or boxes only, no reports (the last two from memory).
- **[BRAX](https://physionet.org/content/brax/1.1.0/):** labels "derived from free-text radiology reports written in Brazilian Portuguese"; the reports are not released.
- **[CRADI](https://doi.org/10.5281/zenodo.5493595):** Chinese hospitals, labels extracted from reports, no report text.
- **[FMCEB-CXR](https://zenodo.org/records/20750284):** describes 5,517 Nigerian reports, but the record is a single 42 KB file of extracted labels with no images.
- **Open re-uploads of MIMIC-CXR or CheXpert Plus on Hugging Face:** a copy does not change the original terms, so use the official sources.

## What I could not find
- No public chest X-ray set with original reports in Chinese, Japanese, German, Korean, Portuguese or Vietnamese. Reviews list CX-CHR and CH-Xray (Chinese) as private.
- I searched DataCite, Hugging Face, OpenAIRE, PhysioNet, Zenodo, Europe PMC and the web. OpenAlex was unavailable and Hugging Face rate-limited one lookup, so coverage may be slightly incomplete.

## Next step
Accept the ReXGradient-160K terms and download IU X-Ray today, and start PhysioNet credentialing for MIMIC-CXR and the CheXpert Plus registration in parallel. For the secondary experiment, send the CASIA-CXR request now, since it depends on the authors replying.

The interactive shortlist is at `/private/tmp/e-75lO4W/home/cwd/dataset-shortlist.html`.

- **R4 (nice):** English; non-English is fine for the secondary experiment.
- **R5 (nice):** obtainable quickly.
- I assumed academic, non-commercial use; almost every licence below forbids commercial use.

## Shortlist
| # | Dataset | Access | Licence | Size | Fit | Checked |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | MIMIC-CXR | Application: credentialing, training and signed agreement; days to ~2 weeks | PhysioNet Credentialed Health Data License 1.5.0 | 227,835 studies, 377,110 images | Strong, English | today (steps partly from memory) |
| 2 | CheXpert Plus | Account and research use agreement on Stanford's Redivis; probably same day | Stanford research use agreement, non-commercial | 187,711 studies, 223,462 pairs | Strong, English | today (approval mechanics not confirmed) |
| 3 | ReXGradient-160K | Hugging Face login, accept terms, automatic; minutes | Harvard non-commercial agreement | 160,000 studies, 273,004 images | Strong, English | today |
| 4 | IU X-Ray (Open-i) | Direct download, no login; minutes | CC BY-NC-ND 4.0 per secondary sources | ~4,000 reports, ~8,000 images | Strong but small, English | links today; licence not confirmed |
| 5 | CASIA-CXR | Request form and hand-signed agreement; weeks. Sample open now | Custom, academic only | 11,111 radiographs | Strong, French | today |
| 6 | PadChest-GR | Request form under research use agreement; days | PadChest research use agreement | 4,555 studies | Partial, Spanish and English sentences | today |
| 7 | BIMCV-COVID19+ | Request form, or IEEE DataPort login; days | BIMCV research use agreement | 21,342 CR and 34,829 DX studies | Partial, Spanish | today (report format from memory) |
| 8 | PadChest | Request form; days. 167 MB sample open | PadChest research use agreement | 160,000+ images | Partial, Spanish, stemmed text | today |
| 9 | REFLACX | PhysioNet login and agreement; images need MIMIC-CXR | PhysioNet Restricted Health Data License | ~3,000 reports (from memory) | Partial, English | today |

## Details

**1. [MIMIC-CXR](https://physionet.org/content/mimic-cxr/2.1.0/)** (v2.1.0)
- **Contents:** one free-text report per study, "stored in individual text files named using the anonymous study identifier", plus the DICOM images (5.1 TB).
- **Steps:**
  1. Create a PhysioNet account.
  2. Apply for credentialed access; from memory, this asks for a reference such as a supervisor.
  3. Complete the required training, "CITI Data or Specimens Only Research" (a few hours, from memory).
  4. Sign the Credentialed Health Data Use Agreement 1.5.0 on the project page.
  5. For JPG images, sign the same agreement on [MIMIC-CXR-JPG](https://physionet.org/content/mimic-cxr-jpg/2.1.0/) (612 GB).
- **Conditions:**
  - Access is personal, so each team member applies separately and the data cannot be shared.
  - The agreement requires that "any publication which makes use of the data will also make the relevant code available".
  - PhysioNet [forbids sending the data to online AI services](https://physionet.org/news/post/llm-responsible-use/) unless zero retention is verified, which matters if you plan LLM-based preprocessing or evaluation.
- **Used in:** [the data descriptor](https://doi.org/10.1038/s41597-019-0322-0) and [a warm-starting report generation baseline](https://doi.org/10.1016/j.artmed.2023.102633).

**2. [CheXpert Plus](https://aimi.stanford.edu/datasets/chexpert-plus)**
- **Contents:** "223,462 unique pairs of radiology reports and chest X-rays" from 64,725 Stanford patients, with reports split into 11 sections and images in DICOM. The Redivis record is 6.76 TB.
- **Steps:** follow "Download here" to [Stanford's Redivis page](https://doi.org/10.57761/fzna-pm76), sign in, and accept the Stanford University Dataset Research Use Agreement. I could not confirm whether approval is automatic.
- **Licence:** research only, no commercial use, no redistribution, and each colleague registers individually (from the [AIMI portal listing](https://stanfordaimi.azurewebsites.net/datasets/5158c524-d3ab-4e02-96e9-6ee9efc110a1) and the paper).
- **Caveat (from memory):** many reports have only an Impression section, with Findings present for a minority. Check section coverage before fixing your target text.
- **Used in:** [a small multimodal radiology model](https://doi.org/10.1038/s41467-025-58344-x) and [a dual-stage report generation framework](https://doi.org/10.1609/aaai.v40i40.40688).

**3. [ReXGradient-160K](https://huggingface.co/datasets/rajpurkarlab/ReXGradient-160K)**
- **Contents:** studies from "109,487 unique patients across 3 U.S. health systems (79 medical sites)", each report with Indication, Comparison, Findings and Impression. Splits are 140,000 train, 10,000 validation and 10,000 public test.
- **Steps:** log in to Hugging Face and accept the agreement on the dataset page; access is granted automatically. The download is 156 GB.
- **Conditions:**
  - Non-commercial research only, with no third-party sharing.
  - You may download only the portion your project needs.
  - Images are PNGs downsampled to 25% of original dimensions.
- **Caveat:** the card does not state the language; I assumed English from the US sites. It was released in 2025, so there are few published baselines.

**4. [IU X-Ray / Open-i](https://openi.nlm.nih.gov/faq)**
- **Contents:** XML reports with findings and impression; a paper found today counts 3,996 reports and 8,121 images.
- **Steps:** download [the reports archive](https://openi.nlm.nih.gov/imgs/collections/NLMCXR_reports.tgz) (1.1 MB) and [the PNG archive](https://openi.nlm.nih.gov/imgs/collections/NLMCXR_png.tgz) (1.36 GB). Both links answered today without a login.
- **Licence:** secondary sources report CC BY-NC-ND 4.0, but the official FAQ could not be read automatically, so confirm it in a browser.
- **Caveat:** de-identification replaced many words with "XXXX". It is too small to train on alone and best used as a second benchmark.

**5. [CASIA-CXR](https://www.casia-cxr.net/)** (French)
- **Contents:** "narrative reports originally written in French" with Indication, Comparison, Findings and Impression fields; 11,111 radiographs per the [dataset paper](https://doi.org/10.1016/j.neucom.2024.128478).
- **Steps:** download the ~80 MB sample from the [downloads page](https://casia-cxr.net/downloads.html), fill in the request form, then hand-sign the data use agreement. Turnaround is not stated.
- **Conditions:** academic researchers only and no redistribution. Also, "You may not translate the radiographic reports… to any other languages without prior written permission", which rules out translating to English for a pooled experiment unless you ask.
- **Caveat:** cases were sampled from five disease categories, so the mix is not a natural hospital distribution.

**6. [PadChest-GR](https://bimcv.cipf.es/bimcv-projects/padchest-gr/)** (Spanish and English)
- **Contents:** 4,555 studies with complete lists of positive and negative finding sentences in both languages, plus bounding boxes (46 GB).
- **Fit:** the sentences were extracted from the Spanish reports and translated with GPT-4, so this is not the verbatim report. It works well as a bilingual evaluation set.
- **Steps:** read the [research use agreement](https://bimcv.cipf.es/bimcv-projects/padchest/padchest-dataset-research-use-agreement/), then submit the request form on the project page.
- **Used in:** [the NEJM AI dataset paper](https://doi.org/10.1056/aidbp2401120).

**7. [BIMCV-COVID19+](https://bimcv.cipf.es/bimcv-projects/bimcv-covid19/)** (Spanish)
- **Contents:** the page says the data include "informes radiográficos". That they are verbatim free text is from memory, so check the data descriptor first.
- **Steps:** submit the request form for the release you want, or log in to [IEEE DataPort](https://doi.org/10.21227/w3aw-rv39). The two pages describe access differently; the BIMCV page is the owner's.
- **Caveat:** COVID-positive patients only, CT studies are mixed in, and the archives run to hundreds of GB.

**8. [PadChest](https://bimcv.cipf.es/bimcv-projects/padchest/)** (Spanish)
- **Fit:** the report field is "preprocessed, words are stemmed and tokenized" (for example "compar con estudi previ 2010 sin identific cambi signific"), per the [authors' repository](https://github.com/auriml/Rx-thorax-automatic-captioning). That makes it a poor target for readable report generation.
- **Steps:** same request form and agreement as PadChest-GR; the full set is 1.02 TB. Inspect the 167 MB sample first.

**9. [REFLACX](https://physionet.org/content/reflacx-xray-localization/1.0.0/)**
- **Contents:** reports that radiologists dictated afresh for MIMIC-CXR frontal images while eye-tracked. It is useful as a second reference report, not as training data.
- **Steps:** the reports need only a PhysioNet login and a signed agreement, but the images come from MIMIC-CXR, which needs credentialing.

## Known, not confirmed
- **[CANDID-PTX](https://doi.org/10.17608/k6.auckland.14173982.v2)** (New Zealand, English): 19,237 radiographs with anonymised free-text reports, per the [RSNA paper](https://pubs.rsna.org/doi/full/10.1148/ryai.2021210136).
  - The original route was an online ethics course plus a signed agreement emailed to the author.
  - One catalogue copy of the description says external users can no longer obtain it after New Zealand's 2024 data governance changes.
  - Figshare blocked automated reading, so open the record in a browser before planning around it.
- **[Naija-CXR](https://zenodo.org/records/21781842)** (Nigeria): an open 389 MB download listing "Free radiologist reports", but it gives no image or report counts and I did not inspect the contents. The record says CC BY 4.0 while the description says non-commercial only.
- **[Interpret-CXR](https://huggingface.co/datasets/StanfordAIMI/interpret-cxr-test-public)** (RRG24 challenge): the public test set exists behind an automatic Hugging Face gate. From memory, the training pool combines CheXpert Plus, PadChest, BIMCV-COVID19 and IU X-Ray, and you add MIMIC-CXR yourself; I did not confirm this.

## Not recommended, and why
- **CheXpert (original), NIH ChestX-ray14, VinDr-CXR:** labels or boxes only, no reports (the last two from memory).
- **[BRAX](https://physionet.org/content/brax/1.1.0/):** labels "derived from free-text radiology reports written in Brazilian Portuguese"; the reports are not released.
- **[CRADI](https://doi.org/10.5281/zenodo.5493595):** Chinese hospitals, labels extracted from reports, no report text.
- **[FMCEB-CXR](https://zenodo.org/records/20750284):** describes 5,517 Nigerian reports, but the record is a single 42 KB file of extracted labels with no images.
- **Open re-uploads of MIMIC-CXR or CheXpert Plus on Hugging Face:** a copy does not change the original terms, so use the official sources.

## What I could not find
- No public chest X-ray set with original reports in Chinese, Japanese, German, Korean, Portuguese or Vietnamese. Reviews list CX-CHR and CH-Xray (Chinese) as private.
- I searched DataCite, Hugging Face, OpenAIRE, PhysioNet, Zenodo, Europe PMC and the web. OpenAlex was unavailable and Hugging Face rate-limited one lookup, so coverage may be slightly incomplete.

## Next step
Accept the ReXGradient-160K terms and download IU X-Ray today, and start PhysioNet credentialing for MIMIC-CXR and the CheXpert Plus registration in parallel. For the secondary experiment, send the CASIA-CXR request now, since it depends on the authors replying.

The interactive shortlist is at `/private/tmp/e-75lO4W/home/cwd/dataset-shortlist.html`.

  - The agreement requires that "any publication which makes use of the data will also make the relevant code available".
  - PhysioNet [forbids sending the data to online AI services](https://physionet.org/news/post/llm-responsible-use/) unless zero retention is verified, which matters if you plan LLM-based preprocessing or evaluation.
- **Used in:** [the data descriptor](https://doi.org/10.1038/s41597-019-0322-0) and [a warm-starting report generation baseline](https://doi.org/10.1016/j.artmed.2023.102633).

**2. [CheXpert Plus](https://aimi.stanford.edu/datasets/chexpert-plus)**
- **Contents:** "223,462 unique pairs of radiology reports and chest X-rays" from 64,725 Stanford patients, with reports split into 11 sections and images in DICOM. The Redivis record is 6.76 TB.
- **Steps:** follow "Download here" to [Stanford's Redivis page](https://doi.org/10.57761/fzna-pm76), sign in, and accept the Stanford University Dataset Research Use Agreement. I could not confirm whether approval is automatic.
- **Licence:** research only, no commercial use, no redistribution, and each colleague registers individually (from the [AIMI portal listing](https://stanfordaimi.azurewebsites.net/datasets/5158c524-d3ab-4e02-96e9-6ee9efc110a1) and the paper).
- **Caveat (from memory):** many reports have only an Impression section, with Findings present for a minority. Check section coverage before fixing your target text.
- **Used in:** [a small multimodal radiology model](https://doi.org/10.1038/s41467-025-58344-x) and [a dual-stage report generation framework](https://doi.org/10.1609/aaai.v40i40.40688).

**3. [ReXGradient-160K](https://huggingface.co/datasets/rajpurkarlab/ReXGradient-160K)**
- **Contents:** studies from "109,487 unique patients across 3 U.S. health systems (79 medical sites)", each report with Indication, Comparison, Findings and Impression. Splits are 140,000 train, 10,000 validation and 10,000 public test.
- **Steps:** log in to Hugging Face and accept the agreement on the dataset page; access is granted automatically. The download is 156 GB.
- **Conditions:**
  - Non-commercial research only, with no third-party sharing.
  - You may download only the portion your project needs.
  - Images are PNGs downsampled to 25% of original dimensions.
- **Caveat:** the card does not state the language; I assumed English from the US sites. It was released in 2025, so there are few published baselines.

**4. [IU X-Ray / Open-i](https://openi.nlm.nih.gov/faq)**
- **Contents:** XML reports with findings and impression; a paper found today counts 3,996 reports and 8,121 images.
- **Steps:** download [the reports archive](https://openi.nlm.nih.gov/imgs/collections/NLMCXR_reports.tgz) (1.1 MB) and [the PNG archive](https://openi.nlm.nih.gov/imgs/collections/NLMCXR_png.tgz) (1.36 GB). Both links answered today without a login.
- **Licence:** secondary sources report CC BY-NC-ND 4.0, but the official FAQ could not be read automatically, so confirm it in a browser.
- **Caveat:** de-identification replaced many words with "XXXX". It is too small to train on alone and best used as a second benchmark.

**5. [CASIA-CXR](https://www.casia-cxr.net/)** (French)
- **Contents:** "narrative reports originally written in French" with Indication, Comparison, Findings and Impression fields; 11,111 radiographs per the [dataset paper](https://doi.org/10.1016/j.neucom.2024.128478).
- **Steps:** download the ~80 MB sample from the [downloads page](https://casia-cxr.net/downloads.html), fill in the request form, then hand-sign the data use agreement. Turnaround is not stated.
- **Conditions:** academic researchers only and no redistribution. Also, "You may not translate the radiographic reports… to any other languages without prior written permission", which rules out translating to English for a pooled experiment unless you ask.
- **Caveat:** cases were sampled from five disease categories, so the mix is not a natural hospital distribution.

**6. [PadChest-GR](https://bimcv.cipf.es/bimcv-projects/padchest-gr/)** (Spanish and English)
- **Contents:** 4,555 studies with complete lists of positive and negative finding sentences in both languages, plus bounding boxes (46 GB).
- **Fit:** the sentences were extracted from the Spanish reports and translated with GPT-4, so this is not the verbatim report. It works well as a bilingual evaluation set.
- **Steps:** read the [research use agreement](https://bimcv.cipf.es/bimcv-projects/padchest/padchest-dataset-research-use-agreement/), then submit the request form on the project page.
- **Used in:** [the NEJM AI dataset paper](https://doi.org/10.1056/aidbp2401120).

**7. [BIMCV-COVID19+](https://bimcv.cipf.es/bimcv-projects/bimcv-covid19/)** (Spanish)
- **Contents:** the page says the data include "informes radiográficos". That they are verbatim free text is from memory, so check the data descriptor first.
- **Steps:** submit the request form for the release you want, or log in to [IEEE DataPort](https://doi.org/10.21227/w3aw-rv39). The two pages describe access differently; the BIMCV page is the owner's.
- **Caveat:** COVID-positive patients only, CT studies are mixed in, and the archives run to hundreds of GB.

**8. [PadChest](https://bimcv.cipf.es/bimcv-projects/padchest/)** (Spanish)
- **Fit:** the report field is "preprocessed, words are stemmed and tokenized" (for example "compar con estudi previ 2010 sin identific cambi signific"), per the [authors' repository](https://github.com/auriml/Rx-thorax-automatic-captioning). That makes it a poor target for readable report generation.
- **Steps:** same request form and agreement as PadChest-GR; the full set is 1.02 TB. Inspect the 167 MB sample first.

**9. [REFLACX](https://physionet.org/content/reflacx-xray-localization/1.0.0/)**
- **Contents:** reports that radiologists dictated afresh for MIMIC-CXR frontal images while eye-tracked. It is useful as a second reference report, not as training data.
- **Steps:** the reports need only a PhysioNet login and a signed agreement, but the images come from MIMIC-CXR, which needs credentialing.

## Known, not confirmed
- **[CANDID-PTX](https://doi.org/10.17608/k6.auckland.14173982.v2)** (New Zealand, English): 19,237 radiographs with anonymised free-text reports, per the [RSNA paper](https://pubs.rsna.org/doi/full/10.1148/ryai.2021210136).
  - The original route was an online ethics course plus a signed agreement emailed to the author.
  - One catalogue copy of the description says external users can no longer obtain it after New Zealand's 2024 data governance changes.
  - Figshare blocked automated reading, so open the record in a browser before planning around it.
- **[Naija-CXR](https://zenodo.org/records/21781842)** (Nigeria): an open 389 MB download listing "Free radiologist reports", but it gives no image or report counts and I did not inspect the contents. The record says CC BY 4.0 while the description says non-commercial only.
- **[Interpret-CXR](https://huggingface.co/datasets/StanfordAIMI/interpret-cxr-test-public)** (RRG24 challenge): the public test set exists behind an automatic Hugging Face gate. From memory, the training pool combines CheXpert Plus, PadChest, BIMCV-COVID19 and IU X-Ray, and you add MIMIC-CXR yourself; I did not confirm this.

## Not recommended, and why
- **CheXpert (original), NIH ChestX-ray14, VinDr-CXR:** labels or boxes only, no reports (the last two from memory).
- **[BRAX](https://physionet.org/content/brax/1.1.0/):** labels "derived from free-text radiology reports written in Brazilian Portuguese"; the reports are not released.
- **[CRADI](https://doi.org/10.5281/zenodo.5493595):** Chinese hospitals, labels extracted from reports, no report text.
- **[FMCEB-CXR](https://zenodo.org/records/20750284):** describes 5,517 Nigerian reports, but the record is a single 42 KB file of extracted labels with no images.
- **Open re-uploads of MIMIC-CXR or CheXpert Plus on Hugging Face:** a copy does not change the original terms, so use the official sources.

## What I could not find
- No public chest X-ray set with original reports in Chinese, Japanese, German, Korean, Portuguese or Vietnamese. Reviews list CX-CHR and CH-Xray (Chinese) as private.
- I searched DataCite, Hugging Face, OpenAIRE, PhysioNet, Zenodo, Europe PMC and the web. OpenAlex was unavailable and Hugging Face rate-limited one lookup, so coverage may be slightly incomplete.

## Next step
Accept the ReXGradient-160K terms and download IU X-Ray today, and start PhysioNet credentialing for MIMIC-CXR and the CheXpert Plus registration in parallel. For the secondary experiment, send the CASIA-CXR request now, since it depends on the authors replying.

The interactive shortlist is at `/private/tmp/e-75lO4W/home/cwd/dataset-shortlist.html`.

Four English datasets and four non-English ones release the report text itself. Two of the English ones (ReXGradient-160K and IU X-Ray) you can download today, and MIMIC-CXR is the only one that needs a multi-day credentialing process. Everything below was checked on 11 November 2026 (UTC) unless marked "from memory".

## What I assumed you need
- **R1 (must):** the radiologist's free-text report is released, not only labels derived from it.
- **R2 (must):** chest radiographs are linked to the report at image or study level.
- **R3 (nice):** large enough to train, or at least evaluate, a generation model.
- **R4 (nice):** English; non-English is fine for the secondary experiment.
- **R5 (nice):** obtainable quickly.
- I assumed academic, non-commercial use; almost every licence below forbids commercial use.

## Details

**1. [MIMIC-CXR](https://physionet.org/content/mimic-cxr/2.1.0/)** (v2.1.0)
- **Contents:** one free-text report per study, "stored in individual text files named using the anonymous study identifier", plus the DICOM images (5.1 TB).
- **Steps:**
  1. Create a PhysioNet account.
  2. Apply for credentialed access; from memory, this asks for a reference such as a supervisor.
  3. Complete the required training, "CITI Data or Specimens Only Research" (a few hours, from memory).
  4. Sign the Credentialed Health Data Use Agreement 1.5.0 on the project page.
  5. For JPG images, sign the same agreement on [MIMIC-CXR-JPG](https://physionet.org/content/mimic-cxr-jpg/2.1.0/) (612 GB).
- **Conditions:**
  - Access is personal, so each team member applies separately and the data cannot be shared.
  - The agreement requires that "any publication which makes use of the data will also make the relevant code available".
  - PhysioNet [forbids sending the data to online AI services](https://physionet.org/news/post/llm-responsible-use/) unless zero retention is verified, which matters if you plan LLM-based preprocessing or evaluation.
- **Used in:** [the data descriptor](https://doi.org/10.1038/s41597-019-0322-0) and [a warm-starting report generation baseline](https://doi.org/10.1016/j.artmed.2023.102633).

**2. [CheXpert Plus](https://aimi.stanford.edu/datasets/chexpert-plus)**
