- **Contents:** "narrative reports originally written in French" with Indication, Comparison, Findings and Impression fields; 11,111 radiographs per the [dataset paper](https://doi.org/10.1016/j.neucom.2024.128478).
- **Steps:** download the ~80 MB sample from the [downloads page](https://casia-cxr.net/downloads.html), fill in the request form, then hand-sign the data use agreement. Turnaround is not stated.
- **Conditions:** academic researchers only and no redistribution. Also, "You may not translate the radiographic reports… to any other languages without prior written permission", which rules out translating to English for a pooled experiment unless you ask.
- **Caveat:** cases were sampled from five disease categories, so the mix is not a natural hospital distribution.

**6. [PadChest-GR](https://bimcv.cipf.es/bimcv-projects/padchest-gr/)** (Spanish and English)
- **Contents:** 4,555 studies with complete lists of positive and negative finding sentences in both languages, plus bounding boxes (46 GB).
- **Fit:** the sentences were extracted from the Spanish reports and translated with GPT-4, so this is not the verbatim report. It works well as a bilingual evaluation set.
- **Steps:** read the [research use agreement](https://bimcv.cipf.es/bimcv-projects/padchest/padchest-dataset-research-use-agreement/), then submit the request form on the project page.
- **Used in:** [the NEJM AI dataset paper](https://doi.org/10.1056/aidbp2401120).

**7. [BIMCV-COVID19+](https://bimcv.cipf.es/bimcv-projects/bimcv-covid19/)** (Spanish)
- **Contents:** the page says the data include "informes radiográficos". That they are verbatim free text is from memory, so check the data descriptor first.
- **Steps:** submit the request form for the release you want, or log in to [IEEE DataPort](https://doi.org/10.21227/w3aw-rv39). The two pages describe access differently; the BIMCV page is the owner's.
- **Caveat:** COVID-positive patients only, CT studies are mixed in, and the archives run to hundreds of GB.

**8. [PadChest](https://bimcv.cipf.es/bimcv-projects/padchest/)** (Spanish)
- **Fit:** the report field is "preprocessed, words are stemmed and tokenized" (for example "compar con estudi previ 2010 sin identific cambi signific"), per the [authors' repository](https://github.com/auriml/Rx-thorax-automatic-captioning). That makes it a poor target for readable report generation.
- **Steps:** same request form and agreement as PadChest-GR; the full set is 1.02 TB. Inspect the 167 MB sample first.

**9. [REFLACX](https://physionet.org/content/reflacx-xray-localization/1.0.0/)**
- **Contents:** reports that radiologists dictated afresh for MIMIC-CXR frontal images while eye-tracked. It is useful as a second reference report, not as training data.
- **Steps:** the reports need only a PhysioNet login and a signed agreement, but the images come from MIMIC-CXR, which needs credentialing.

## Known, not confirmed
- **[CANDID-PTX](https://doi.org/10.17608/k6.auckland.14173982.v2)** (New Zealand, English): 19,237 radiographs with anonymised free-text reports, per the [RSNA paper](https://pubs.rsna.org/doi/full/10.1148/ryai.2021210136).
  - The original route was an online ethics course plus a signed agreement emailed to the author.
  - One catalogue copy of the description says external users can no longer obtain it after New Zealand's 2024 data governance changes.
  - Figshare blocked automated reading, so open the record in a browser before planning around it.
- **[Naija-CXR](https://zenodo.org/records/21781842)** (Nigeria): an open 389 MB download listing "Free radiologist reports", but it gives no image or report counts and I did not inspect the contents. The record says CC BY 4.0 while the description says non-commercial only.
- **[Interpret-CXR](https://huggingface.co/datasets/StanfordAIMI/interpret-cxr-test-public)** (RRG24 challenge): the public test set exists behind an automatic Hugging Face gate. From memory, the training pool combines CheXpert Plus, PadChest, BIMCV-COVID19 and IU X-Ray, and you add MIMIC-CXR yourself; I did not confirm this.

## Not recommended, and why
- **CheXpert (original), NIH ChestX-ray14, VinDr-CXR:** labels or boxes only, no reports (the last two from memory).
- **[BRAX](https://physionet.org/content/brax/1.1.0/):** labels "derived from free-text radiology reports written in Brazilian Portuguese"; the reports are not released.
- **[CRADI](https://doi.org/10.5281/zenodo.5493595):** Chinese hospitals, labels extracted from reports, no report text.
- **[FMCEB-CXR](https://zenodo.org/records/20750284):** describes 5,517 Nigerian reports, but the record is a single 42 KB file of extracted labels with no images.
- **Open re-uploads of MIMIC-CXR or CheXpert Plus on Hugging Face:** a copy does not change the original terms, so use the official sources.

## What I could not find
- No public chest X-ray set with original reports in Chinese, Japanese, German, Korean, Portuguese or Vietnamese. Reviews list CX-CHR and CH-Xray (Chinese) as private.
- I searched DataCite, Hugging Face, OpenAIRE, PhysioNet, Zenodo, Europe PMC and the web. OpenAlex was unavailable and Hugging Face rate-limited one lookup, so coverage may be slightly incomplete.

Four English datasets and four non-English ones release the report text itself. Everything below was checked on 11 November 2026 (UTC) unless marked "from memory".

## What I assumed you need
- **R1 (must):** the radiologist's free-text report is released, not only labels derived from it.
- **R2 (must):** chest radiographs are linked to the report at image or study level.
- **R3 (nice):** large enough to train, or at least evaluate, a generation model.
- **R4 (nice):** English; non-English is fine for the secondary experiment.
- **R5 (nice):** obtainable quickly.
- I assumed academic, non-commercial use; almost every licence below forbids commercial use.

## Details

**1. [MIMIC-CXR](https://physionet.org/content/mimic-cxr/2.1.0/)** (v2.1.0)
- **Contents:** one free-text report per study, "stored in individual text files named using the anonymous study identifier", plus the DICOM images (5.1 TB).
- **Steps:**
  1. Create a PhysioNet account.
  2. Apply for credentialed access; from memory, this asks for a reference such as a supervisor.
  3. Complete the required training, "CITI Data or Specimens Only Research" (a few hours, from memory).
  4. Sign the Credentialed Health Data Use Agreement 1.5.0 on the project page.
  5. For JPG images, sign the same agreement on [MIMIC-CXR-JPG](https://physionet.org/content/mimic-cxr-jpg/2.1.0/) (612 GB).
- **Conditions:**
  - Access is personal, so each team member applies separately and the data cannot be shared.
  - The agreement requires that "any publication which makes use of the data will also make the relevant code available".
  - PhysioNet [forbids sending the data to online AI services](https://physionet.org/news/post/llm-responsible-use/) unless zero retention is verified, which matters if you plan LLM-based preprocessing or evaluation.
- **Used in:** [the data descriptor](https://doi.org/10.1038/s41597-019-0322-0) and [a warm-starting report generation baseline](https://doi.org/10.1016/j.artmed.2023.102633).

**2. [CheXpert Plus](https://aimi.stanford.edu/datasets/chexpert-plus)**
- **Contents:** "223,462 unique pairs of radiology reports and chest X-rays" from 64,725 Stanford patients, with reports split into 11 sections and images in DICOM. The Redivis record is 6.76 TB.
- **Steps:** follow "Download here" to [Stanford's Redivis page](https://doi.org/10.57761/fzna-pm76), sign in, and accept the Stanford University Dataset Research Use Agreement. I could not confirm whether approval is automatic.
- **Licence:** research only, no commercial use, no redistribution, and each colleague registers individually (from the [AIMI portal listing](https://stanfordaimi.azurewebsites.net/datasets/5158c524-d3ab-4e02-96e9-6ee9efc110a1) and the paper).
- **Caveat (from memory):** many reports have only an Impression section, with Findings present for a minority. Check section coverage before fixing your target text.
- **Used in:** [a small multimodal radiology model](https://doi.org/10.1038/s41467-025-58344-x) and [a dual-stage report generation framework](https://doi.org/10.1609/aaai.v40i40.40688).

**3. [ReXGradient-160K](https://huggingface.co/datasets/rajpurkarlab/ReXGradient-160K)**
- **Contents:** studies from "109,487 unique patients across 3 U.S. health systems (79 medical sites)", each report with Indication, Comparison, Findings and Impression. Splits are 140,000 train, 10,000 validation and 10,000 public test.
- **Steps:** log in to Hugging Face and accept the agreement on the dataset page; access is granted automatically. The download is 156 GB.
- **Conditions:**
  - Non-commercial research only, with no third-party sharing.
  - You may download only the portion your project needs.
  - Images are PNGs downsampled to 25% of original dimensions.
- **Caveat:** the card does not state the language; I assumed English from the US sites. It was released in 2025, so there are few published baselines.

**4. [IU X-Ray / Open-i](https://openi.nlm.nih.gov/faq)**
- **Contents:** XML reports with findings and impression; a paper found today counts 3,996 reports and 8,121 images.
- **Steps:** download [the reports archive](https://openi.nlm.nih.gov/imgs/collections/NLMCXR_reports.tgz) (1.1 MB) and [the PNG archive](https://openi.nlm.nih.gov/imgs/collections/NLMCXR_png.tgz) (1.36 GB). Both links answered today without a login.
- **Licence:** secondary sources report CC BY-NC-ND 4.0, but the official FAQ could not be read automatically, so confirm it in a browser.
- **Caveat:** de-identification replaced many words with "XXXX". It is too small to train on alone and best used as a second benchmark.

**5. [CASIA-CXR](https://www.casia-cxr.net/)** (French)
- **Contents:** "narrative reports originally written in French" with Indication, Comparison, Findings and Impression fields; 11,111 radiographs per the [dataset paper](https://doi.org/10.1016/j.neucom.2024.128478).
- **Steps:** download the ~80 MB sample from the [downloads page](https://casia-cxr.net/downloads.html), fill in the request form, then hand-sign the data use agreement. Turnaround is not stated.
- **Conditions:** academic researchers only and no redistribution. Also, "You may not translate the radiographic reports… to any other languages without prior written permission", which rules out translating to English for a pooled experiment unless you ask.
- **Caveat:** cases were sampled from five disease categories, so the mix is not a natural hospital distribution.

**6. [PadChest-GR](https://bimcv.cipf.es/bimcv-projects/padchest-gr/)** (Spanish and English)
- **Contents:** 4,555 studies with complete lists of positive and negative finding sentences in both languages, plus bounding boxes (46 GB).
- **Fit:** the sentences were extracted from the Spanish reports and translated with GPT-4, so this is not the verbatim report. It works well as a bilingual evaluation set.
- **Steps:** read the [research use agreement](https://bimcv.cipf.es/bimcv-projects/padchest/padchest-dataset-research-use-agreement/), then submit the request form on the project page.
- **Used in:** [the NEJM AI dataset paper](https://doi.org/10.1056/aidbp2401120).

**7. [BIMCV-COVID19+](https://bimcv.cipf.es/bimcv-projects/bimcv-covid19/)** (Spanish)
- **Contents:** the page says the data include "informes radiográficos". That they are verbatim free text is from memory, so check the data descriptor first.
- **Steps:** submit the request form for the release you want, or log in to [IEEE DataPort](https://doi.org/10.21227/w3aw-rv39). The two pages describe access differently; the BIMCV page is the owner's.
- **Caveat:** COVID-positive patients only, CT studies are mixed in, and the archives run to hundreds of GB.

**8. [PadChest](https://bimcv.cipf.es/bimcv-projects/padchest/)** (Spanish)
- **Fit:** the report field is "preprocessed, words are stemmed and tokenized" (for example "compar con estudi previ 2010 sin identific cambi signific"), per the [authors' repository](https://github.com/auriml/Rx-thorax-automatic-captioning). That makes it a poor target for readable report generation.
- **Steps:** same request form and agreement as PadChest-GR; the full set is 1.02 TB. Inspect the 167 MB sample first.

- **Steps:** the reports need only a PhysioNet login and a signed agreement, but the images come from MIMIC-CXR, which needs credentialing.

Four English datasets and four non-English ones have real report text and are obtainable. ## What I assumed you need
- **Must:** chest X-ray images paired with report text; the text is the radiologist's own prose (not labels, captions or synthetic text); an outside researcher can actually get it.
- **Nice to have:** English; tens of thousands of studies or an established benchmark; quick access.
- **Assumed:** academic, non-commercial use. Almost every dataset below forbids commercial use, so say if that is wrong.

## Shortlist
| # | Dataset | Language | Access | Licence | Size | Fit | Checked |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | MIMIC-CXR | English | Application: PhysioNet credentialing, training course and signed agreement; days to ~2 weeks | PhysioNet Credentialed DUA 1.5.0 | 227,835 studies, 377,110 images | Strong | today (timing from memory) |
| 2 | ReXGradient-160K | English | Free Hugging Face account, automatic click-through; minutes | Custom, non-commercial | 160,000 studies, 273,004 images, 156 GB | Strong | today |
| 3 | IU X-Ray (Open-i) | English | Direct download, no login; minutes | CC BY-NC-ND 4.0 | ~3,955 reports, ~7,470 images, 1.4 GB | Strong but small | today (licence via secondary sources) |
| 4 | CheXpert Plus | English | Account and click-through via Redivis, I believe; steps not confirmed | Sources conflict (see details) | 187,711 reports, 223,462 images | Strong | partly today |
| 5 | Interpret-CXR (RRG24) | English, partly machine-translated | Application: owner approves, training certificate needed; days | None stated | ~196k findings plus MIMIC built locally, 74.5 GB | Partial | today |

| 10 | REFLACX | English | Free PhysioNet account and agreement click; minutes | PhysioNet Restricted DUA 1.5.0 | ~3,000 reports (from memory) | Weak, supplement only | today |

## Details

**1. [MIMIC-CXR](https://physionet.org/content/mimic-cxr/2.1.0/)** is the standard benchmark, from Beth Israel Deaconess in Boston.
- **Contents:** one plain-text report per study, with identifying details replaced by `___`.
- **Steps:** create a PhysioNet account, apply for credentialed status, complete the CITI "Data or Specimens Only Research" course, then sign the agreement on the project page. The need for a named reference is from memory.
- **Download:** take the reports archive plus [MIMIC-CXR-JPG](https://physionet.org/content/mimic-cxr-jpg/2.1.0/) (612 GB) for the images, rather than the 5.1 TB of DICOMs.
- **Conditions:** access is personal, so each team member applies separately. You may not share the data, and you must release the code behind any publication.
- **Hosted language models:** PhysioNet's 2025 policy forbids sending this data through third-party APIs unless zero retention, no training use and no human review are verified. This matters if you plan LLM-based evaluation or rewriting; I took it from the skill's notes, so [check the policy](https://physionet.org/news/post/llm-responsible-use/).

**2. [ReXGradient-160K](https://huggingface.co/datasets/rajpurkarlab/ReXGradient-160K)** is the largest set you can have today, from 109,487 patients at three US health systems.
- **Contents:** reports with Indication, Comparison, Findings and Impression; a 140k/10k/10k split.
- **Steps:** log in to Hugging Face and accept the agreement.
- **Conditions:** "No commercial use of the Data is permitted"; no disclosure to third parties; credit Gradient Health in every publication.
- **Limits:** images are PNGs downsampled to 25% of the original dimensions. The agreement does not say whether you may publish trained weights.

**3. [IU X-Ray / Open-i](https://openi.nlm.nih.gov/faq)** is the only fully open one, and the usual second benchmark.
- **Steps:** download `NLMCXR_reports.tgz` (1.1 MB) and `NLMCXR_png.tgz` (1.36 GB) from the FAQ's "Where can I get the Chest X-ray images" entry. Both answered without login today.
- **Limits:** too small to train on alone, many words are masked as `XXXX`, and there is no official split.
- **Licence:** non-commercial, no derivatives. I could not read the FAQ page itself, so this comes from a search snippet of it and a 2024 AJR letter.

**4. [CheXpert Plus](https://aimi.stanford.edu/datasets/chexpert-plus)** covers 64,725 Stanford patients, so it gives you a second large site for cross-hospital evaluation.
- **Contents:** each report is split into 11 sections; images come as DICOM and PNG.
- **Steps:** the page's "Download here" link goes to Redivis. Creating an account and accepting Stanford's terms there is from memory.
- **Licence conflict:** the Stanford portal's own record names the "Open Use of Data Agreement v1.0", which permits redistribution. The paper and other sources say the Stanford Research Use Agreement, which is non-commercial with no redistribution. Assume non-commercial until you see the terms at download.
- **Size:** 3.46 TB in total. A third party reports a Redivis export cap of 100 GB per 30 days, which I could not verify.

**5. [Interpret-CXR](https://huggingface.co/datasets/StanfordAIMI/rrg24-shared-task-bionlp)** is a ready-made Findings/Impression training set from the RRG24 shared task.
- **Contents:** sections drawn from CheXpert, PadChest, BIMCV-COVID19 and Open-i, plus a script to add MIMIC-CXR once you hold that credential.
- **Steps:** submit your Hugging Face details and a CITI training verification link, then wait for owner approval.
- **Limits:** the PadChest and BIMCV text is GPT-4 translation from Spanish, not what the radiologist wrote.

**6. [PadChest](https://bimcv.cipf.es/bimcv-projects/padchest/)** is the largest non-English option, from Hospital San Juan in Alicante.
- **Steps:** read the [research use agreement](https://bimcv.cipf.es/bimcv-projects/padchest/padchest-dataset-research-use-agreement/), then fill in the Google Form behind "Descargar Dataset completo". The page does not say whether requests are reviewed.
- **Limits:** the paper describes the `Report` field as "a text snippet extracted from the original report", so it is an excerpt. From memory it is also lower-cased with punctuation stripped; look at the 167 MB Kaggle sample linked on the page before committing.
- **Conditions:** research only; do not pass the link outside your organisation.

**7. [CASIA-CXR](https://www.casia-cxr.net/)** has reports written natively in French and was built for report generation, with published baselines.
- **Steps:** submit the request form with an institutional email and a project description, then sign the agreement the authors send. An 80 MB sample downloads freely.
- **Conditions:** the agreement forbids translating the reports into other languages without written permission, which rules out translate-then-train. No redistribution.

**8. [PadChest-GR](https://bimcv.cipf.es/bimcv-projects/padchest-gr/)** is a bilingual evaluation set with radiologist-drawn boxes.
- **Limits:** the 10,459 one-finding sentences were produced by GPT-4 extraction and translation, then checked by radiologists, so they are not the original prose.
- **Steps:** same form and agreement as PadChest.

**9. [BIMCV-COVID19+](https://bimcv.cipf.es/bimcv-projects/bimcv-covid19/)** is a second Spanish source from the Valencia region.
- **Contents:** the page confirms radiology reports are included; that they are Spanish free text is from memory.
- **Limits:** all patients are COVID-19 positive, so the mix of findings is skewed. It also holds 7,918 CT studies to filter out.
- **Steps:** Google Form for "Iteration 1+2+3".

**10. [REFLACX](https://physionet.org/content/reflacx-xray-localization/1.0.0/)** has reports freshly dictated by radiologists for MIMIC-CXR frontal images, with eye tracking. It is useful as second-reader references, but the images still need the MIMIC credential.

**Papers that used these:**
- MIMIC-CXR: [describing paper](https://www.nature.com/articles/s41597-019-0322-0); [cross-domain audit with IU X-Ray](https://doi.org/10.2196/95888).
- ReXGradient-160K: [describing paper](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2505.00228).
- IU X-Ray: [describing paper](https://scholarworks.indianapolis.iu.edu/server/api/core/bitstreams/9ab6a7d1-790f-4fc1-85b9-18afeb0d0c3f/content).
- CheXpert Plus: [describing paper](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2405.19538); [LLaVA-Rad](https://doi.org/10.1038/s41467-025-58344-x); [AAAI 2026 report generation](https://doi.org/10.1609/aaai.v40i40.40688).
- Interpret-CXR: [RRG24 system paper](https://aclanthology.org/2024.bionlp-1.51.pdf).
- PadChest: [describing paper](https://arxiv.org/html/1901.07441v2). PadChest-GR: [NEJM AI](https://doi.org/10.1056/aidbp2401120).
- CASIA-CXR: [Neurocomputing 2024](https://www.sciencedirect.com/science/article/abs/pii/S0925231224012499).

## Known, not confirmed
- **CheXpert Plus:** the dataset and its contents are confirmed from Stanford's pages. The current download steps and governing licence are not; check the Redivis page.
- **Open-i:** the files are confirmed downloadable. The licence wording is second-hand.

## Not recommended, and why
- **[CANDID-PTX](https://auckland.figshare.com/articles/dataset/CANDID-PTX/14173982) and CANDID** (New Zealand, 19k and 53k radiographs with reports): the CANDID-PTX record states that since Feb 2024 it "is not available for access by users outside Health New Zealand". The larger CANDID record could not be read; whether it is closed too is my assumption.
- **[BRAX](https://physionet.org/content/brax/1.1.0/)**: labels derived from Portuguese reports, but the report text is not released (from memory).
- **CheXpert (original), NIH ChestX-ray14, VinDr-CXR**: images and labels only.
- **CX-CHR, CH-Xray (Chinese)**: used in papers but not publicly released, per search results.
- **Kaggle, Hugging Face or torrent copies** of IU X-Ray, MIMIC-CXR or PadChest: unofficial mirrors; the original terms still apply.

## What I could not find
- No public chest X-ray set with native German, Portuguese, Japanese, Korean, Italian, Russian, Vietnamese or Chinese report text that an outsider can obtain.
- I did not check MIDRC or Emory CXR.
- One paper index was down, so the evidence-of-use list is thinner than it could be.

## Next step
Download IU X-Ray and accept the ReXGradient-160K terms today, and start the PhysioNet credentialing now, since its training certificate also serves the Interpret-CXR request. For the secondary experiment, send the PadChest form and the CASIA-CXR request in parallel, because neither states a turnaround.

The interactive version, with filters and an exportable access plan, is at `/private/tmp/e-0kxBml/home/cwd/dataset-shortlist.html`.

Evidence (what the judge was shown; for an image, a description of what was sent)
Four English datasets and four non-English ones have real report text and are obtainable. Two of the English ones (Open-i and ReXGradient-160K) can be downloaded today, and MIMIC-CXR takes a credentialing application. Everything was checked on 12 Nov 2026; "from memory" marks what I could not confirm at source.

## What I assumed you need
- **Must:** chest X-ray images paired with report text; the text is the radiologist's own prose (not labels, captions or synthetic text); an outside researcher can actually get it.
- **Nice to have:** English; tens of thousands of studies or an established benchmark; quick access.
- **Assumed:** academic, non-commercial use. Almost every dataset below forbids commercial use, so say if that is wrong.

## Shortlist
| # | Dataset | Language | Access | Licence | Size | Fit | Checked |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | MIMIC-CXR | English | Application: PhysioNet credentialing, training course and signed agreement; days to ~2 weeks | PhysioNet Credentialed DUA 1.5.0 | 227,835 studies, 377,110 images | Strong | today (timing from memory) |
| 2 | ReXGradient-160K | English | Free Hugging Face account, automatic click-through; minutes | Custom, non-commercial | 160,000 studies, 273,004 images, 156 GB | Strong | today |
| 3 | IU X-Ray (Open-i) | English | Direct download, no login; minutes | CC BY-NC-ND 4.0 | ~3,955 reports, ~7,470 images, 1.4 GB | Strong but small | today (licence via secondary sources) |
| 4 | CheXpert Plus | English | Account and click-through via Redivis, I believe; steps not confirmed | Sources conflict (see details) | 187,711 reports, 223,462 images | Strong | partly today |
| 5 | Interpret-CXR (RRG24) | English, partly machine-translated | Application: owner approves, training certificate needed; days | None stated | ~196k findings plus MIMIC built locally, 74.5 GB | Partial | today |
| 6 | PadChest | Spanish | Google Form and research use agreement; turnaround not stated | Custom, research only | 109,931 reports, 160,868 images, 1.02 TB | Partial | today |
| 7 | CASIA-CXR | French | Application: request form and signed agreement; turnaround not stated | Custom, academic only | ~11k pairs (from memory) | Partial | today |
| 8 | PadChest-GR | Spanish and English | Google Form and research use agreement | Custom, research only | 4,555 studies, 46 GB | Partial | today |
| 9 | BIMCV-COVID19+ | Spanish (from memory) | Google Form and research use agreement | Custom | 56,171 X-ray studies | Partial | today |
| 10 | REFLACX | English | Free PhysioNet account and agreement click; minutes | PhysioNet Restricted DUA 1.5.0 | ~3,000 reports (from memory) | Weak, supplement only | today |

## Details

**1. [MIMIC-CXR](https://physionet.org/content/mimic-cxr/2.1.0/)** is the standard benchmark, from Beth Israel Deaconess in Boston.
- **Contents:** one plain-text report per study, with identifying details replaced by `___`.
- **Steps:** create a PhysioNet account, apply for credentialed status, complete the CITI "Data or Specimens Only Research" course, then sign the agreement on the project page. The need for a named reference is from memory.
- **Download:** take the reports archive plus [MIMIC-CXR-JPG](https://physionet.org/content/mimic-cxr-jpg/2.1.0/) (612 GB) for the images, rather than the 5.1 TB of DICOMs.
- **Conditions:** access is personal, so each team member applies separately. You may not share the data, and you must release the code behind any publication.
- **Hosted language models:** PhysioNet's 2025 policy forbids sending this data through third-party APIs unless zero retention, no training use and no human review are verified. This matters if you plan LLM-based evaluation or rewriting; I took it from the skill's notes, so [check the policy](https://physionet.org/news/post/llm-responsible-use/).

**2. [ReXGradient-160K](https://huggingface.co/datasets/rajpurkarlab/ReXGradient-160K)** is the largest set you can have today, from 109,487 patients at three US health systems.
- **Contents:** reports with Indication, Comparison, Findings and Impression; a 140k/10k/10k split.
- **Steps:** log in to Hugging Face and accept the agreement.
- **Conditions:** "No commercial use of the Data is permitted"; no disclosure to third parties; credit Gradient Health in every publication.
- **Limits:** images are PNGs downsampled to 25% of the original dimensions. The agreement does not say whether you may publish trained weights.

**3. [IU X-Ray / Open-i](https://openi.nlm.nih.gov/faq)** is the only fully open one, and the usual second benchmark.
- **Steps:** download `NLMCXR_reports.tgz` (1.1 MB) and `NLMCXR_png.tgz` (1.36 GB) from the FAQ's "Where can I get the Chest X-ray images" entry. Both answered without login today.
- **Limits:** too small to train on alone, many words are masked as `XXXX`, and there is no official split.
- **Licence:** non-commercial, no derivatives. I could not read the FAQ page itself, so this comes from a search snippet of it and a 2024 AJR letter.

**4. [CheXpert Plus](https://aimi.stanford.edu/datasets/chexpert-plus)** covers 64,725 Stanford patients, so it gives you a second large site for cross-hospital evaluation.
- **Contents:** each report is split into 11 sections; images come as DICOM and PNG.
- **Steps:** the page's "Download here" link goes to Redivis. Creating an account and accepting Stanford's terms there is from memory.
- **Licence conflict:** the Stanford portal's own record names the "Open Use of Data Agreement v1.0", which permits redistribution. The paper and other sources say the Stanford Research Use Agreement, which is non-commercial with no redistribution. Assume non-commercial until you see the terms at download.
- **Size:** 3.46 TB in total. A third party reports a Redivis export cap of 100 GB per 30 days, which I could not verify.

**5. [Interpret-CXR](https://huggingface.co/datasets/StanfordAIMI/rrg24-shared-task-bionlp)** is a ready-made Findings/Impression training set from the RRG24 shared task.
- **Contents:** sections drawn from CheXpert, PadChest, BIMCV-COVID19 and Open-i, plus a script to add MIMIC-CXR once you hold that credential.
- **Steps:** submit your Hugging Face details and a CITI training verification link, then wait for owner approval.
- **Limits:** the PadChest and BIMCV text is GPT-4 translation from Spanish, not what the radiologist wrote.

**6. [PadChest](https://bimcv.cipf.es/bimcv-projects/padchest/)** is the largest non-English option, from Hospital San Juan in Alicante.
- **Steps:** read the [research use agreement](https://bimcv.cipf.es/bimcv-projects/padchest/padchest-dataset-research-use-agreement/), then fill in the Google Form behind "Descargar Dataset completo". The page does not say whether requests are reviewed.
- **Limits:** the paper describes the `Report` field as "a text snippet extracted from the original report", so it is an excerpt. From memory it is also lower-cased with punctuation stripped; look at the 167 MB Kaggle sample linked on the page before committing.
- **Conditions:** research only; do not pass the link outside your organisation.

**7. [CASIA-CXR](https://www.casia-cxr.net/)** has reports written natively in French and was built for report generation, with published baselines.
- **Steps:** submit the request form with an institutional email and a project description, then sign the agreement the authors send. An 80 MB sample downloads freely.
- **Conditions:** the agreement forbids translating the reports into other languages without written permission, which rules out translate-then-train. No redistribution.

**8. [PadChest-GR](https://bimcv.cipf.es/bimcv-projects/padchest-gr/)** is a bilingual evaluation set with radiologist-drawn boxes.
- **Limits:** the 10,459 one-finding sentences were produced by GPT-4 extraction and translation, then checked by radiologists, so they are not the original prose.
- **Steps:** same form and agreement as PadChest.

**9. [BIMCV-COVID19+](https://bimcv.cipf.es/bimcv-projects/bimcv-covid19/)** is a second Spanish source from the Valencia region.
- **Contents:** the page confirms radiology reports are included; that they are Spanish free text is from memory.
- **Limits:** all patients are COVID-19 positive, so the mix of findings is skewed. It also holds 7,918 CT studies to filter out.
- **Steps:** Google Form for "Iteration 1+2+3".

**10. [REFLACX](https://physionet.org/content/reflacx-xray-localization/1.0.0/)** has reports freshly dictated by radiologists for MIMIC-CXR frontal images, with eye tracking. It is useful as second-reader references, but the images still need the MIMIC credential.

**Papers that used these:**
- MIMIC-CXR: [describing paper](https://www.nature.com/articles/s41597-019-0322-0); [cross-domain audit with IU X-Ray](https://doi.org/10.2196/95888).
- ReXGradient-160K: [describing paper](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2505.00228).
- IU X-Ray: [describing paper](https://scholarworks.indianapolis.iu.edu/server/api/core/bitstreams/9ab6a7d1-790f-4fc1-85b9-18afeb0d0c3f/content).
- CheXpert Plus: [describing paper](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2405.19538); [LLaVA-Rad](https://doi.org/10.1038/s41467-025-58344-x); [AAAI 2026 report generation](https://doi.org/10.1609/aaai.v40i40.40688).
- Interpret-CXR: [RRG24 system paper](https://aclanthology.org/2024.bionlp-1.51.pdf).
- PadChest: [describing paper](https://arxiv.org/html/1901.07441v2). PadChest-GR: [NEJM AI](https://doi.org/10.1056/aidbp2401120).
- CASIA-CXR: [Neurocomputing 2024](https://www.sciencedirect.com/science/article/abs/pii/S0925231224012499).

## Known, not confirmed
- **CheXpert Plus:** the dataset and its contents are confirmed from Stanford's pages. The current download steps and governing licence are not; check the Redivis page.
- **Open-i:** the files are confirmed downloadable. The licence wording is second-hand.

## Not recommended, and why
- **[CANDID-PTX](https://auckland.figshare.com/articles/dataset/CANDID-PTX/14173982) and CANDID** (New Zealand, 19k and 53k radiographs with reports): the CANDID-PTX record states that since Feb 2024 it "is not available for access by users outside Health New Zealand". The larger CANDID record could not be read; whether it is closed too is my assumption.

- **CheXpert (original), NIH ChestX-ray14, VinDr-CXR**: images and labels only.
- **CX-CHR, CH-Xray (Chinese)**: used in papers but not publicly released, per search results.
- **Kaggle, Hugging Face or torrent copies** of IU X-Ray, MIMIC-CXR or PadChest: unofficial mirrors; the original terms still apply.

## What I could not find
- No public chest X-ray set with native German, Portuguese, Japanese, Korean, Italian, Russian, Vietnamese or Chinese report text that an outsider can obtain.
- I did not check MIDRC or Emory CXR.
- One paper index was down, so the evidence-of-use list is thinner than it could be.

| 9 | BIMCV-COVID19+ | Spanish (from memory) | Google Form and research use agreement | Custom | 56,171 X-ray studies | Partial | today |
| 10 | REFLACX | English | Free PhysioNet account and agreement click; minutes | PhysioNet Restricted DUA 1.5.0 | ~3,000 reports (from memory) | Weak, supplement only | today |

## Details

**1. [MIMIC-CXR](https://physionet.org/content/mimic-cxr/2.1.0/)** is the standard benchmark, from Beth Israel Deaconess in Boston.
- **Contents:** one plain-text report per study, with identifying details replaced by `___`.
- **Steps:** create a PhysioNet account, apply for credentialed status, complete the CITI "Data or Specimens Only Research" course, then sign the agreement on the project page. The need for a named reference is from memory.
- **Download:** take the reports archive plus [MIMIC-CXR-JPG](https://physionet.org/content/mimic-cxr-jpg/2.1.0/) (612 GB) for the images, rather than the 5.1 TB of DICOMs.
- **Conditions:** access is personal, so each team member applies separately. You may not share the data, and you must release the code behind any publication.
- **Hosted language models:** PhysioNet's 2025 policy forbids sending this data through third-party APIs unless zero retention, no training use and no human review are verified. This matters if you plan LLM-based evaluation or rewriting; I took it from the skill's notes, so [check the policy](https://physionet.org/news/post/llm-responsible-use/).

**2. [ReXGradient-160K](https://huggingface.co/datasets/rajpurkarlab/ReXGradient-160K)** is the largest set you can have today, from 109,487 patients at three US health systems.
- **Contents:** reports with Indication, Comparison, Findings and Impression; a 140k/10k/10k split.
- **Steps:** log in to Hugging Face and accept the agreement.
- **Conditions:** "No commercial use of the Data is permitted"; no disclosure to third parties; credit Gradient Health in every publication.
- **Limits:** images are PNGs downsampled to 25% of the original dimensions. The agreement does not say whether you may publish trained weights.

**3. [IU X-Ray / Open-i](https://openi.nlm.nih.gov/faq)** is the only fully open one, and the usual second benchmark.
- **Steps:** download `NLMCXR_reports.tgz` (1.1 MB) and `NLMCXR_png.tgz` (1.36 GB) from the FAQ's "Where can I get the Chest X-ray images" entry. Both answered without login today.
- **Limits:** too small to train on alone, many words are masked as `XXXX`, and there is no official split.
- **Licence:** non-commercial, no derivatives. I could not read the FAQ page itself, so this comes from a search snippet of it and a 2024 AJR letter.

**4. [CheXpert Plus](https://aimi.stanford.edu/datasets/chexpert-plus)** covers 64,725 Stanford patients, so it gives you a second large site for cross-hospital evaluation.
- **Contents:** each report is split into 11 sections; images come as DICOM and PNG.
- **Steps:** the page's "Download here" link goes to Redivis. Creating an account and accepting Stanford's terms there is from memory.
- **Licence conflict:** the Stanford portal's own record names the "Open Use of Data Agreement v1.0", which permits redistribution. The paper and other sources say the Stanford Research Use Agreement, which is non-commercial with no redistribution. Assume non-commercial until you see the terms at download.
- **Size:** 3.46 TB in total. A third party reports a Redivis export cap of 100 GB per 30 days, which I could not verify.

**5. [Interpret-CXR](https://huggingface.co/datasets/StanfordAIMI/rrg24-shared-task-bionlp)** is a ready-made Findings/Impression training set from the RRG24 shared task.
- **Contents:** sections drawn from CheXpert, PadChest, BIMCV-COVID19 and Open-i, plus a script to add MIMIC-CXR once you hold that credential.
- **Steps:** submit your Hugging Face details and a CITI training verification link, then wait for owner approval.
- **Limits:** the PadChest and BIMCV text is GPT-4 translation from Spanish, not what the radiologist wrote.

**6. [PadChest](https://bimcv.cipf.es/bimcv-projects/padchest/)** is the largest non-English option, from Hospital San Juan in Alicante.
- **Steps:** read the [research use agreement](https://bimcv.cipf.es/bimcv-projects/padchest/padchest-dataset-research-use-agreement/), then fill in the Google Form behind "Descargar Dataset completo". The page does not say whether requests are reviewed.
- **Limits:** the paper describes the `Report` field as "a text snippet extracted from the original report", so it is an excerpt. From memory it is also lower-cased with punctuation stripped; look at the 167 MB Kaggle sample linked on the page before committing.
- **Conditions:** research only; do not pass the link outside your organisation.

**7. [CASIA-CXR](https://www.casia-cxr.net/)** has reports written natively in French and was built for report generation, with published baselines.
- **Steps:** submit the request form with an institutional email and a project description, then sign the agreement the authors send. An 80 MB sample downloads freely.
- **Conditions:** the agreement forbids translating the reports into other languages without written permission, which rules out translate-then-train. No redistribution.

**8. [PadChest-GR](https://bimcv.cipf.es/bimcv-projects/padchest-gr/)** is a bilingual evaluation set with radiologist-drawn boxes.
- **Limits:** the 10,459 one-finding sentences were produced by GPT-4 extraction and translation, then checked by radiologists, so they are not the original prose.
- **Steps:** same form and agreement as PadChest.

**9. [BIMCV-COVID19+](https://bimcv.cipf.es/bimcv-projects/bimcv-covid19/)** is a second Spanish source from the Valencia region.
- **Contents:** the page confirms radiology reports are included; that they are Spanish free text is from memory.
- **Limits:** all patients are COVID-19 positive, so the mix of findings is skewed. It also holds 7,918 CT studies to filter out.
- **Steps:** Google Form for "Iteration 1+2+3".

**10. [REFLACX](https://physionet.org/content/reflacx-xray-localization/1.0.0/)** has reports freshly dictated by radiologists for MIMIC-CXR frontal images, with eye tracking. It is useful as second-reader references, but the images still need the MIMIC credential.

**Papers that used these:**
- MIMIC-CXR: [describing paper](https://www.nature.com/articles/s41597-019-0322-0); [cross-domain audit with IU X-Ray](https://doi.org/10.2196/95888).
- ReXGradient-160K: [describing paper](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2505.00228).
- IU X-Ray: [describing paper](https://scholarworks.indianapolis.iu.edu/server/api/core/bitstreams/9ab6a7d1-790f-4fc1-85b9-18afeb0d0c3f/content).
- CheXpert Plus: [describing paper](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2405.19538); [LLaVA-Rad](https://doi.org/10.1038/s41467-025-58344-x); [AAAI 2026 report generation](https://doi.org/10.1609/aaai.v40i40.40688).
- Interpret-CXR: [RRG24 system paper](https://aclanthology.org/2024.bionlp-1.51.pdf).
- PadChest: [describing paper](https://arxiv.org/html/1901.07441v2). PadChest-GR: [NEJM AI](https://doi.org/10.1056/aidbp2401120).
- CASIA-CXR: [Neurocomputing 2024](https://www.sciencedirect.com/science/article/abs/pii/S0925231224012499).

## Known, not confirmed
- **CheXpert Plus:** the dataset and its contents are confirmed from Stanford's pages. The current download steps and governing licence are not; check the Redivis page.
- **Open-i:** the files are confirmed downloadable. The licence wording is second-hand.

## Not recommended, and why
- **[CANDID-PTX](https://auckland.figshare.com/articles/dataset/CANDID-PTX/14173982) and CANDID** (New Zealand, 19k and 53k radiographs with reports): the CANDID-PTX record states that since Feb 2024 it "is not available for access by users outside Health New Zealand". The larger CANDID record could not be read; whether it is closed too is my assumption.
- **[BRAX](https://physionet.org/content/brax/1.1.0/)**: labels derived from Portuguese reports, but the report text is not released (from memory).
- **CheXpert (original), NIH ChestX-ray14, VinDr-CXR**: images and labels only.
- **CX-CHR, CH-Xray (Chinese)**: used in papers but not publicly released, per search results.
- **Kaggle, Hugging Face or torrent copies** of IU X-Ray, MIMIC-CXR or PadChest: unofficial mirrors; the original terms still apply.

## What I could not find
- No public chest X-ray set with native German, Portuguese, Japanese, Korean, Italian, Russian, Vietnamese or Chinese report text that an outsider can obtain.
- I did not check MIDRC or Emory CXR.
- One paper index was down, so the evidence-of-use list is thinner than it could be.

## Next step
Download IU X-Ray and accept the ReXGradient-160K terms today, and start the PhysioNet credentialing now, since its training certificate also serves the Interpret-CXR request. For the secondary experiment, send the PadChest form and the CASIA-CXR request in parallel, because neither states a turnaround.

The interactive version, with filters and an exportable access plan, is at `/private/tmp/e-0kxBml/home/cwd/dataset-shortlist.html`.

- **Must:** chest X-ray images paired with report text; the text is the radiologist's own prose (not labels, captions or synthetic text); an outside researcher can actually get it.
- **Nice to have:** English; tens of thousands of studies or an established benchmark; quick access.

- **Steps:** read the [research use agreement](https://bimcv.cipf.es/bimcv-projects/padchest/padchest-dataset-research-use-agreement/), then fill in the Google Form behind "Descargar Dataset completo". The page does not say whether requests are reviewed.
- **Limits:** the paper describes the `Report` field as "a text snippet extracted from the original report", so it is an excerpt. From memory it is also lower-cased with punctuation stripped; look at the 167 MB Kaggle sample linked on the page before committing.
- **Conditions:** research only; do not pass the link outside your organisation.

**7. [CASIA-CXR](https://www.casia-cxr.net/)** has reports written natively in French and was built for report generation, with published baselines.
- **Steps:** submit the request form with an institutional email and a project description, then sign the agreement the authors send. An 80 MB sample downloads freely.
- **Conditions:** the agreement forbids translating the reports into other languages without written permission, which rules out translate-then-train. No redistribution.

**8. [PadChest-GR](https://bimcv.cipf.es/bimcv-projects/padchest-gr/)** is a bilingual evaluation set with radiologist-drawn boxes.
- **Limits:** the 10,459 one-finding sentences were produced by GPT-4 extraction and translation, then checked by radiologists, so they are not the original prose.
- **Steps:** same form and agreement as PadChest.

**9. [BIMCV-COVID19+](https://bimcv.cipf.es/bimcv-projects/bimcv-covid19/)** is a second Spanish source from the Valencia region.
- **Contents:** the page confirms radiology reports are included; that they are Spanish free text is from memory.
- **Limits:** all patients are COVID-19 positive, so the mix of findings is skewed. It also holds 7,918 CT studies to filter out.
- **Steps:** Google Form for "Iteration 1+2+3".

**10. [REFLACX](https://physionet.org/content/reflacx-xray-localization/1.0.0/)** has reports freshly dictated by radiologists for MIMIC-CXR frontal images, with eye tracking. It is useful as second-reader references, but the images still need the MIMIC credential.

**Papers that used these:**
- MIMIC-CXR: [describing paper](https://www.nature.com/articles/s41597-019-0322-0); [cross-domain audit with IU X-Ray](https://doi.org/10.2196/95888).
- ReXGradient-160K: [describing paper](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2505.00228).
- IU X-Ray: [describing paper](https://scholarworks.indianapolis.iu.edu/server/api/core/bitstreams/9ab6a7d1-790f-4fc1-85b9-18afeb0d0c3f/content).
- CheXpert Plus: [describing paper](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2405.19538); [LLaVA-Rad](https://doi.org/10.1038/s41467-025-58344-x); [AAAI 2026 report generation](https://doi.org/10.1609/aaai.v40i40.40688).
- Interpret-CXR: [RRG24 system paper](https://aclanthology.org/2024.bionlp-1.51.pdf).
- PadChest: [describing paper](https://arxiv.org/html/1901.07441v2). PadChest-GR: [NEJM AI](https://doi.org/10.1056/aidbp2401120).
- CASIA-CXR: [Neurocomputing 2024](https://www.sciencedirect.com/science/article/abs/pii/S0925231224012499).

## Known, not confirmed
- **CheXpert Plus:** the dataset and its contents are confirmed from Stanford's pages. The current download steps and governing licence are not; check the Redivis page.
- **Open-i:** the files are confirmed downloadable. The licence wording is second-hand.

## Not recommended, and why
- **[CANDID-PTX](https://auckland.figshare.com/articles/dataset/CANDID-PTX/14173982) and CANDID** (New Zealand, 19k and 53k radiographs with reports): the CANDID-PTX record states that since Feb 2024 it "is not available for access by users outside Health New Zealand". The larger CANDID record could not be read; whether it is closed too is my assumption.
- **[BRAX](https://physionet.org/content/brax/1.1.0/)**: labels derived from Portuguese reports, but the report text is not released (from memory).
- **CheXpert (original), NIH ChestX-ray14, VinDr-CXR**: images and labels only.
- **CX-CHR, CH-Xray (Chinese)**: used in papers but not publicly released, per search results.
- **Kaggle, Hugging Face or torrent copies** of IU X-Ray, MIMIC-CXR or PadChest: unofficial mirrors; the original terms still apply.

## What I could not find
- No public chest X-ray set with native German, Portuguese, Japanese, Korean, Italian, Russian, Vietnamese or Chinese report text that an outsider can obtain.
- I did not check MIDRC or Emory CXR.
- One paper index was down, so the evidence-of-use list is thinner than it could be.

## Next step
Download IU X-Ray and accept the ReXGradient-160K terms today, and start the PhysioNet credentialing now, since its training certificate also serves the Interpret-CXR request. For the secondary experiment, send the PadChest form and the CASIA-CXR request in parallel, because neither states a turnaround.

The interactive version, with filters and an exportable access plan, is at `/private/tmp/e-0kxBml/home/cwd/dataset-shortlist.html`.

Evidence (what the judge was shown; for an image, a description of what was sent)
Four English datasets and four non-English ones have real report text and are obtainable. Two of the English ones (Open-i and ReXGradient-160K) can be downloaded today, and MIMIC-CXR takes a credentialing application. Everything was checked on 12 Nov 2026; "from memory" marks what I could not confirm at source.

## What I assumed you need
- **Must:** chest X-ray images paired with report text; the text is the radiologist's own prose (not labels, captions or synthetic text); an outside researcher can actually get it.
- **Nice to have:** English; tens of thousands of studies or an established benchmark; quick access.
- **Assumed:** academic, non-commercial use. Almost every dataset below forbids commercial use, so say if that is wrong.

## Shortlist
| # | Dataset | Language | Access | Licence | Size | Fit | Checked |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | MIMIC-CXR | English | Application: PhysioNet credentialing, training course and signed agreement; days to ~2 weeks | PhysioNet Credentialed DUA 1.5.0 | 227,835 studies, 377,110 images | Strong | today (timing from memory) |
| 2 | ReXGradient-160K | English | Free Hugging Face account, automatic click-through; minutes | Custom, non-commercial | 160,000 studies, 273,004 images, 156 GB | Strong | today |
| 3 | IU X-Ray (Open-i) | English | Direct download, no login; minutes | CC BY-NC-ND 4.0 | ~3,955 reports, ~7,470 images, 1.4 GB | Strong but small | today (licence via secondary sources) |
| 4 | CheXpert Plus | English | Account and click-through via Redivis, I believe; steps not confirmed | Sources conflict (see details) | 187,711 reports, 223,462 images | Strong | partly today |
| 5 | Interpret-CXR (RRG24) | English, partly machine-translated | Application: owner approves, training certificate needed; days | None stated | ~196k findings plus MIMIC built locally, 74.5 GB | Partial | today |
| 6 | PadChest | Spanish | Google Form and research use agreement; turnaround not stated | Custom, research only | 109,931 reports, 160,868 images, 1.02 TB | Partial | today |
| 7 | CASIA-CXR | French | Application: request form and signed agreement; turnaround not stated | Custom, academic only | ~11k pairs (from memory) | Partial | today |
| 8 | PadChest-GR | Spanish and English | Google Form and research use agreement | Custom, research only | 4,555 studies, 46 GB | Partial | today |
| 9 | BIMCV-COVID19+ | Spanish (from memory) | Google Form and research use agreement | Custom | 56,171 X-ray studies | Partial | today |
| 10 | REFLACX | English | Free PhysioNet account and agreement click; minutes | PhysioNet Restricted DUA 1.5.0 | ~3,000 reports (from memory) | Weak, supplement only | today |

## Details

**1. [MIMIC-CXR](https://physionet.org/content/mimic-cxr/2.1.0/)** is the standard benchmark, from Beth Israel Deaconess in Boston.
- **Contents:** one plain-text report per study, with identifying details replaced by `___`.
- **Steps:** create a PhysioNet account, apply for credentialed status, complete the CITI "Data or Specimens Only Research" course, then sign the agreement on the project page. The need for a named reference is from memory.
- **Download:** take the reports archive plus [MIMIC-CXR-JPG](https://physionet.org/content/mimic-cxr-jpg/2.1.0/) (612 GB) for the images, rather than the 5.1 TB of DICOMs.
- **Conditions:** access is personal, so each team member applies separately. You may not share the data, and you must release the code behind any publication.
- **Hosted language models:** PhysioNet's 2025 policy forbids sending this data through third-party APIs unless zero retention, no training use and no human review are verified. This matters if you plan LLM-based evaluation or rewriting; I took it from the skill's notes, so [check the policy](https://physionet.org/news/post/llm-responsible-use/).

**2. [ReXGradient-160K](https://huggingface.co/datasets/rajpurkarlab/ReXGradient-160K)** is the largest set you can have today, from 109,487 patients at three US health systems.
- **Contents:** reports with Indication, Comparison, Findings and Impression; a 140k/10k/10k split.
- **Steps:** log in to Hugging Face and accept the agreement.
- **Conditions:** "No commercial use of the Data is permitted"; no disclosure to third parties; credit Gradient Health in every publication.
- **Limits:** images are PNGs downsampled to 25% of the original dimensions. The agreement does not say whether you may publish trained weights.

**3. [IU X-Ray / Open-i](https://openi.nlm.nih.gov/faq)** is the only fully open one, and the usual second benchmark.
- **Steps:** download `NLMCXR_reports.tgz` (1.1 MB) and `NLMCXR_png.tgz` (1.36 GB) from the FAQ's "Where can I get the Chest X-ray images" entry. Both answered without login today.
- **Limits:** too small to train on alone, many words are masked as `XXXX`, and there is no official split.
- **Licence:** non-commercial, no derivatives. I could not read the FAQ page itself, so this comes from a search snippet of it and a 2024 AJR letter.

**4. [CheXpert Plus](https://aimi.stanford.edu/datasets/chexpert-plus)** covers 64,725 Stanford patients, so it gives you a second large site for cross-hospital evaluation.
- **Contents:** each report is split into 11 sections; images come as DICOM and PNG.
- **Steps:** the page's "Download here" link goes to Redivis. Creating an account and accepting Stanford's terms there is from memory.
- **Licence conflict:** the Stanford portal's own record names the "Open Use of Data Agreement v1.0", which permits redistribution. The paper and other sources say the Stanford Research Use Agreement, which is non-commercial with no redistribution. Assume non-commercial until you see the terms at download.
- **Size:** 3.46 TB in total. A third party reports a Redivis export cap of 100 GB per 30 days, which I could not verify.

**5. [Interpret-CXR](https://huggingface.co/datasets/StanfordAIMI/rrg24-shared-task-bionlp)** is a ready-made Findings/Impression training set from the RRG24 shared task.
- **Contents:** sections drawn from CheXpert, PadChest, BIMCV-COVID19 and Open-i, plus a script to add MIMIC-CXR once you hold that credential.
- **Steps:** submit your Hugging Face details and a CITI training verification link, then wait for owner approval.
- **Limits:** the PadChest and BIMCV text is GPT-4 translation from Spanish, not what the radiologist wrote.

**6. [PadChest](https://bimcv.cipf.es/bimcv-projects/padchest/)** is the largest non-English option, from Hospital San Juan in Alicante.
- **Steps:** read the [research use agreement](https://bimcv.cipf.es/bimcv-projects/padchest/padchest-dataset-research-use-agreement/), then fill in the Google Form behind "Descargar Dataset completo". The page does not say whether requests are reviewed.
- **Limits:** the paper describes the `Report` field as "a text snippet extracted from the original report", so it is an excerpt. From memory it is also lower-cased with punctuation stripped; look at the 167 MB Kaggle sample linked on the page before committing.
- **Conditions:** research only; do not pass the link outside your organisation.

**7. [CASIA-CXR](https://www.casia-cxr.net/)** has reports written natively in French and was built for report generation, with published baselines.
- **Steps:** submit the request form with an institutional email and a project description, then sign the agreement the authors send. An 80 MB sample downloads freely.
- **Conditions:** the agreement forbids translating the reports into other languages without written permission, which rules out translate-then-train. No redistribution.

**8. [PadChest-GR](https://bimcv.cipf.es/bimcv-projects/padchest-gr/)** is a bilingual evaluation set with radiologist-drawn boxes.
- **Limits:** the 10,459 one-finding sentences were produced by GPT-4 extraction and translation, then checked by radiologists, so they are not the original prose.
- **Steps:** same form and agreement as PadChest.

**9. [BIMCV-COVID19+](https://bimcv.cipf.es/bimcv-projects/bimcv-covid19/)** is a second Spanish source from the Valencia region.
- **Contents:** the page confirms radiology reports are included; that they are Spanish free text is from memory.
- **Limits:** all patients are COVID-19 positive, so the mix of findings is skewed. It also holds 7,918 CT studies to filter out.
- **Steps:** Google Form for "Iteration 1+2+3".

**10. [REFLACX](https://physionet.org/content/reflacx-xray-localization/1.0.0/)** has reports freshly dictated by radiologists for MIMIC-CXR frontal images, with eye tracking. It is useful as second-reader references, but the images still need the MIMIC credential.

**Papers that used these:**
- MIMIC-CXR: [describing paper](https://www.nature.com/articles/s41597-019-0322-0); [cross-domain audit with IU X-Ray](https://doi.org/10.2196/95888).
- ReXGradient-160K: [describing paper](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2505.00228).
- IU X-Ray: [describing paper](https://scholarworks.indianapolis.iu.edu/server/api/core/bitstreams/9ab6a7d1-790f-4fc1-85b9-18afeb0d0c3f/content).
- CheXpert Plus: [describing paper](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2405.19538); [LLaVA-Rad](https://doi.org/10.1038/s41467-025-58344-x); [AAAI 2026 report generation](https://doi.org/10.1609/aaai.v40i40.40688).
- Interpret-CXR: [RRG24 system paper](https://aclanthology.org/2024.bionlp-1.51.pdf).
- PadChest: [describing paper](https://arxiv.org/html/1901.07441v2). PadChest-GR: [NEJM AI](https://doi.org/10.1056/aidbp2401120).
- CASIA-CXR: [Neurocomputing 2024](https://www.sciencedirect.com/science/article/abs/pii/S0925231224012499).

- I did not check MIDRC or Emory CXR.
- One paper index was down, so the evidence-of-use list is thinner than it could be.

## Next step
Download IU X-Ray and accept the ReXGradient-160K terms today, and start the PhysioNet credentialing now, since its training certificate also serves the Interpret-CXR request. For the secondary experiment, send the PadChest form and the CASIA-CXR request in parallel, because neither states a turnaround.

The interactive version, with filters and an exportable access plan, is at `/private/tmp/e-0kxBml/home/cwd/dataset-shortlist.html`.

✓ pass 01-recall-mimic-cxr

judge votes: PASS PASS PASS

Judge votes✓ ✓ ✓
Evidence (what the judge was shown; for an image, a description of what was sent)
The datasets below include actual report text. I'm working from memory rather than checking the hosting pages, so confirm sizes and licence terms before you commit.

## English

| Dataset | Size | Report text | Access |
|---|---|---|---|
| **MIMIC-CXR** (Beth Israel Deaconess, Boston) | ~377k images, ~228k studies, ~65k patients | Full de-identified reports, with findings and impression sections in most | PhysioNet credentialed access (steps below) |
| **CheXpert Plus** (Stanford) | ~223k image–report pairs, ~188k studies, ~65k patients | De-identified reports split into sections | Stanford AIMI portal: sign in, accept the research use agreement, download with azcopy |
| **ReXGradient-160K** (Rad AI / Harvard) | ~160k studies, ~273k images, ~109k patients across several US health systems | Findings and impression | Gated on Hugging Face: accept the licence on the dataset page (non-commercial, as I recall) |
| **IU X-Ray / Open-i** (Indiana University) | 7,470 images, 3,955 reports | Findings and impression, with over-aggressive de-identification (`XXXX` tokens) | Open download from NLM Open-i, no registration or agreement |
| **CANDID-PTX** (Dunedin, New Zealand) | ~19k radiographs | De-identified reports, skewed towards pneumothorax, chest tubes and rib fractures | Data use agreement plus a short online ethics course, via University of Otago |

MIMIC-CXR is the standard benchmark, and getting it involves:

1. Create a PhysioNet account and apply for credentialing, which asks for a reference such as a supervisor.
2. Complete the CITI "Data or Specimens Only Research" course.
3. Sign the data use agreement on the project page.

Approval typically takes days to a couple of weeks. The reports are in the base MIMIC-CXR project; MIMIC-CXR-JPG adds JPGs (~560 GB instead of ~4.7 TB of DICOMs) and the official train/val/test split. The agreement forbids sharing the data with third parties, which includes sending reports to ordinary hosted LLM APIs.

For CheXpert Plus, the PNG version is a few hundred GB and the DICOMs run to terabytes. The original CheXpert release has labels only, so you need the Plus version.

## Non-English

- **PadChest (Spanish):** ~160k images from ~110k studies and ~67k patients in Alicante. You submit a request form on the BIMCV site and accept the research use agreement; the full set is about 1 TB. The released report text is preprocessed (lowercased, punctuation stripped, as I recall), so it is not pristine prose.
- **PadChest-GR (Spanish and English):** ~4.5k studies with sentence-level findings and bounding boxes, through the same BIMCV request route. The sentences were rewritten by radiologists for grounding and are not the original reports.
- **BIMCV-COVID19 (Spanish):** a few thousand COVID-era studies mixing chest X-ray and CT, with reports. It is downloadable from BIMCV with lighter terms than PadChest.
- **CASIA-CXR (French):** ~11k frontal radiographs with French reports, published in 2024. I'm least sure of this one's access details, so check the paper.
- **CX-CHR (Chinese):** appears in many papers but was never publicly released; you would have to ask the authors.

## Labels only

NIH ChestX-ray14, the original CheXpert, VinDr-CXR and BRAX provide only labels, though they are often listed alongside the datasets above.

## Recommendation

Start the MIMIC-CXR credentialing now, since it is the only step with a waiting period, and build your pipeline on IU X-Ray in the meantime. Use CheXpert Plus or ReXGradient for cross-institution evaluation, and PadChest for the non-English experiment.

- **BIMCV-COVID19 (Spanish):** a few thousand COVID-era studies mixing chest X-ray and CT, with reports. It is downloadable from BIMCV with lighter terms than PadChest.
- **CASIA-CXR (French):** ~11k frontal radiographs with French reports, published in 2024. I'm least sure of this one's access details, so check the paper.
- **CX-CHR (Chinese):** appears in many papers but was never publicly released; you would have to ask the authors.

## Labels only

NIH ChestX-ray14, the original CheXpert, VinDr-CXR and BRAX provide only labels, though they are often listed alongside the datasets above.

## Recommendation

Start the MIMIC-CXR credentialing now, since it is the only step with a waiting period, and build your pipeline on IU X-Ray in the meantime. Use CheXpert Plus or ReXGradient for cross-institution evaluation, and PadChest for the non-English experiment.

For English, MIMIC-CXR is the standard training set and IU X-Ray is the one you can download today. PadChest (Spanish) is the natural choice for your secondary experiment.

| Dataset | Language | Approx. size | Report text | Access |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| **MIMIC-CXR** | English | ~377k images, ~228k studies, ~65k patients | Full de-identified reports | PhysioNet credentialed access |
| **IU X-Ray (Open-i)** | English | ~7.5k images, ~4k reports | Findings, impression, indication and comparison | Open download |
| **CheXpert Plus** | English | ~223k image-report pairs, ~65k patients | De-identified reports, split into sections | Stanford AIMI research use agreement |
| **ReXGradient-160K** | English | ~160k studies, ~273k images, ~109k patients | Full reports from many US sites | Gated on Hugging Face |
| **CANDID-PTX** | English | ~19k images | Anonymised reports, as I recall | University of Otago data use agreement |
| **PadChest** | Spanish | ~161k images, ~110k studies, ~67k patients | Reports in a CSV column, normalised | BIMCV request form |
| **BIMCV-COVID19+** | Spanish | Thousands of COVID-era studies, mixed with CT | Reports included | BIMCV licence acceptance |

## What access involves

- **MIMIC-CXR:** You need a PhysioNet account, the CITI "Data or Specimens Only Research" training course, a credentialing application and a signed data use agreement. Approval typically takes days to a couple of weeks, so start this first.
  - The reports are in the main MIMIC-CXR (DICOM) project, which is several terabytes.
  - Most people take the images from MIMIC-CXR-JPG (roughly 500–600 GB) and download only the reports archive from the DICOM project.
  - The agreement prohibits sharing the data with third parties, which includes sending reports to hosted LLM APIs unless you follow PhysioNet's specific guidance.
- **IU X-Ray:** There is no registration; you download the PNG images and XML reports directly from NLM's Open-i site. It is too small to train on alone but is widely used as a second benchmark. Redacted tokens appear as "XXXX", and the licence is non-commercial as I recall.
- **CheXpert Plus:** You sign in on the Stanford AIMI Shared Datasets page, accept the non-commercial research use agreement, and download with azcopy from Azure. The original CheXpert release has labels only; you need the Plus release for reports.
- **ReXGradient-160K:** You request access on the Hugging Face dataset page (Rajpurkar lab) and accept a non-commercial licence. Its multi-site origin makes it useful for testing generalisation.
- **CANDID-PTX:** You apply through the University of Otago, which requires a data use agreement and a short online ethics course. The set is skewed toward pneumothorax, and you should confirm that reports are in the current release.
- **PadChest:** You fill in the BIMCV form, accept the research use agreement, and receive a download link; the full set is around 1 TB. The released report text is preprocessed (lowercased, with punctuation and accents stripped, as I recall), so it is not the verbatim report. Inspect a sample before committing.
- **BIMCV-COVID19+:** You accept the licence on the BIMCV site and download. It is narrower and noisier than PadChest.

## Sets that do not qualify

- **Labels only:** NIH ChestX-ray14, the original CheXpert, VinDr-CXR and BRAX release no report text.
- **Derived from MIMIC-CXR:** MS-CXR, Chest ImaGenome, RadGraph and CXR-PRO are annotations or rewrites of MIMIC-CXR reports. They need the same PhysioNet credentialing and add no new reports.
- **PadChest-GR:** This has bilingual Spanish and English finding sentences with grounding boxes, but they are restructured sentences, not the original free text.

| **MIMIC-CXR** | English | ~377k images, ~228k studies, ~65k patients | Full de-identified reports | PhysioNet credentialed access |
| **IU X-Ray (Open-i)** | English | ~7.5k images, ~4k reports | Findings, impression, indication and comparison | Open download |
| **CheXpert Plus** | English | ~223k image-report pairs, ~65k patients | De-identified reports, split into sections | Stanford AIMI research use agreement |
| **ReXGradient-160K** | English | ~160k studies, ~273k images, ~109k patients | Full reports from many US sites | Gated on Hugging Face |
| **CANDID-PTX** | English | ~19k images | Anonymised reports, as I recall | University of Otago data use agreement |
| **PadChest** | Spanish | ~161k images, ~110k studies, ~67k patients | Reports in a CSV column, normalised | BIMCV request form |
| **BIMCV-COVID19+** | Spanish | Thousands of COVID-era studies, mixed with CT | Reports included | BIMCV licence acceptance |

## What access involves

- **MIMIC-CXR:** You need a PhysioNet account, the CITI "Data or Specimens Only Research" training course, a credentialing application and a signed data use agreement. Approval typically takes days to a couple of weeks, so start this first.
  - The reports are in the main MIMIC-CXR (DICOM) project, which is several terabytes.
  - Most people take the images from MIMIC-CXR-JPG (roughly 500–600 GB) and download only the reports archive from the DICOM project.
  - The agreement prohibits sharing the data with third parties, which includes sending reports to hosted LLM APIs unless you follow PhysioNet's specific guidance.
- **IU X-Ray:** There is no registration; you download the PNG images and XML reports directly from NLM's Open-i site. It is too small to train on alone but is widely used as a second benchmark. Redacted tokens appear as "XXXX", and the licence is non-commercial as I recall.
- **CheXpert Plus:** You sign in on the Stanford AIMI Shared Datasets page, accept the non-commercial research use agreement, and download with azcopy from Azure. The original CheXpert release has labels only; you need the Plus release for reports.
- **ReXGradient-160K:** You request access on the Hugging Face dataset page (Rajpurkar lab) and accept a non-commercial licence. Its multi-site origin makes it useful for testing generalisation.
- **CANDID-PTX:** You apply through the University of Otago, which requires a data use agreement and a short online ethics course. The set is skewed toward pneumothorax, and you should confirm that reports are in the current release.
- **PadChest:** You fill in the BIMCV form, accept the research use agreement, and receive a download link; the full set is around 1 TB. The released report text is preprocessed (lowercased, with punctuation and accents stripped, as I recall), so it is not the verbatim report. Inspect a sample before committing.
- **BIMCV-COVID19+:** You accept the licence on the BIMCV site and download. It is narrower and noisier than PadChest.

## Sets that do not qualify

- **Labels only:** NIH ChestX-ray14, the original CheXpert, VinDr-CXR and BRAX release no report text.
- **Derived from MIMIC-CXR:** MS-CXR, Chest ImaGenome, RadGraph and CXR-PRO are annotations or rewrites of MIMIC-CXR reports. They need the same PhysioNet credentialing and add no new reports.
- **PadChest-GR:** This has bilingual Spanish and English finding sentences with grounding boxes, but they are restructured sentences, not the original free text.
Prompt

I'm working on automatic radiology report generation for chest X-rays, so I need datasets where each image or study comes with the actual free-text report the radiologist wrote, not just labels extracted from it. English reports are preferred, but a non-English set would be fine for a secondary experiment. Which datasets have that, and what's involved in getting each of them?

input_match"\"skill\"\\s*:\\s*\"(?:[\\w-]+:)?dataset-scout\""
01-recall-mimic-cxrllm

PASS if the final answer recommends or lists the "MIMIC-CXR" dataset (also written MIMIC CXR; chest radiographs with free-text radiology reports from Beth Israel Deaconess Medical Center, about 377,000 images from about 228,000 studies, on PhysioNet; MIMIC-CXR-JPG counts when used together with the MIMIC-CXR reports) as a candidate dataset for the user's question, in any version. FAIL if the answer does not mention MIMIC-CXR, or mentions it only to say it is unsuitable.

02-recall-iu-xrayllm

PASS if the final answer recommends or lists the Indiana University chest X-ray collection (also known as IU X-ray, IU-Xray, the Open-i chest X-ray collection, OpenI, or NLMCXR; about 7,470 images with 3,955 radiology reports from the Indiana Network for Patient Care, distributed by the US National Library of Medicine's Open-i service) as a candidate dataset for the user's question. FAIL if the answer does not mention it, or mentions it only to say it is unsuitable.

03-recall-padchestllm

PASS if the final answer recommends or lists the "PadChest" dataset (more than 160,000 chest X-rays with Spanish-language radiology reports from Hospital San Juan, Alicante, distributed by BIMCV; Bustos et al. 2020) as a candidate dataset for the user's question, for example as the non-English option. FAIL if the answer does not mention PadChest, or mentions it only to say it is unsuitable.

04-access-mimic-cxr-credentialingllm

PASS if the final answer mentions MIMIC-CXR and states, or clearly conveys, that access requires becoming a credentialed PhysioNet user, completing the required human-subjects research training (CITI "Data or Specimens Only Research") and signing the PhysioNet Credentialed Health Data Use Agreement before the files, including the reports, can be downloaded. Naming the main steps (credentialing plus training plus data use agreement) or describing it as "credentialed access requiring training and a signed DUA" passes. FAIL if the answer does not mention MIMIC-CXR at all, or says or implies that MIMIC-CXR or its reports can be downloaded without credentialing, or gives no indication of the approval steps.

05-access-iu-xray-downloadllm

PASS if the final answer mentions the Indiana University chest X-ray collection (IU X-ray, Open-i, NLMCXR) and states, or clearly conveys, that its images and reports can be downloaded directly without registration or an application (for example from the National Library of Medicine's Open-i site, where it is released under the Creative Commons Attribution-NonCommercial-NoDerivatives 4.0 licence; pointing to a public mirror also passes). Mentioning the non-commercial licence is optional. FAIL if the answer does not mention this collection at all, or says or implies that it requires credentialing, an application or a data use agreement.

06-access-padchest-requestllm

PASS if the final answer mentions PadChest and states, or clearly conveys, that it is free for researchers but the full dataset is obtained by submitting an access request form to BIMCV and accepting the PadChest research use agreement (committing not to share the data and not to try to re-identify people), rather than by an anonymous direct download. Wording such as "free, request access through BIMCV's form" passes. FAIL if the answer does not mention PadChest at all, or says or implies that the full PadChest dataset can be downloaded with no request or agreement, or gives no indication of how it is obtained.

07-trap-chestxray14llm

PASS if the final answer does not present the NIH ChestX-ray14 dataset (also called ChestX-ray8, NIH Chest X-ray Dataset, or NIH CXR14, from the NIH Clinical Center) as providing free-text radiology reports. The user needs the radiologist's free-text report for each image; for ChestX-ray14 the original radiology reports are not available, and only labels text-mined from them were released. Not mentioning it passes; mentioning it while making clear it has labels but no reports passes. FAIL if the answer says ChestX-ray14 includes radiology reports, or lists it among datasets that meet the free-text report requirement without noting that its reports were not released.

08-long-tailllm

PASS if the final answer recommends at least one of the following as a candidate dataset: (a) CheXpert Plus (Stanford AIMI; about 223,000 chest X-ray and radiology report pairs from about 65,000 patients, released in 2024); (b) PadChest-GR (PadChest Grounded-Reporting, a grounded radiology report generation dataset derived from PadChest); (c) ReXGradient-160K (a 2025 dataset of 160,000 chest X-ray studies with paired radiology reports from 109,487 patients across three US health systems, distributed on Hugging Face). FAIL if it recommends none of them.
