Evidence (what the judge was shown; for an image, a description of what was sent)
Seven datasets meet your requirements well enough to use, and five of them you can download today (three with no account, two with a free IEEE account). Everything below was checked on 2026-11-12 unless marked "from memory".

## What I assumed you need
- **R1 (must):** a timestamped GPS point sequence along each trip, not just pickup and drop-off.
- **R2 (must):** at least a few hundred taxis or ride-hailing vehicles, any city.
- **R3 (nice):** trips identifiable by trip id or occupancy flag, so travel time has ground truth.
- **R4 (nice):** obtainable now for research, with known terms. I assumed academic, non-commercial use.

## Shortlist
| # | Dataset | Access | Licence | Size | Fit | Checked |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | Porto taxi (ECML/PKDD 2015) | Direct download, no account | CC BY 4.0 | 442 taxis, 1.71M trips, 15 s | Strong | today |
| 2 | San Francisco cabspotting (CRAWDAD epfl/mobility) | Free IEEE account, minutes | CC BY | ~500 taxis, 25 days | Strong | today |
| 3 | Rome taxi (CRAWDAD roma/taxi) | Free IEEE account, minutes | CC BY | ~320 taxis, 30 days, 7 s | Strong | today |
| 4 | Harbin taxi (DeepGTT release) | Direct download from Google Drive | None stated | ~13,000 taxis, 1M+ trips, 5 days | Strong | today (file not downloaded) |
| 5 | T-Drive sample (Beijing) | Direct download link; it refused my automated check | None stated, citation requested | 10,357 taxis, 1 week, ~177 s | Partial | page today, link not confirmed |
| 6 | Grab-Posisi (Singapore, Jakarta) | Email request, research only, likely weeks | Non-commercial | 84K trajectories, 1 s | Strong | terms today, mailbox not confirmed |
| 7 | Segmented Taxi Trajectories in Bangkok | Direct download | CC BY 4.0 | 33.5 MB, fleet size not stated | Partial | today |

## Details

**1. Porto taxi** — [UCI page](https://archive.ics.uci.edu/dataset/339/taxi+service+trajectory+prediction+challenge+ecml+pkdd+2015)
- **Contents:** one row per trip from all 442 Porto taxis, with a polyline of one coordinate pair every 15 seconds.
- **Missing:** there is no timestamp per point, only the trip start. You reconstruct times as start + 15 s × index, and should drop rows flagged `MISSING_DATA`.
- **Steps:** open the page and click Download (509 MB zip). The download URL answered today.
- **Used by:** [taxi destination prediction, PLOS ONE 2018](https://doi.org/10.1371/journal.pone.0194629); [road-network trajectory similarity, MDPI 2026](https://www.mdpi.com/2227-7390/14/22/4119).
- The collection period (July 2013 to June 2014) is from memory; the page does not state it.

**2. San Francisco cabspotting** — [IEEE DataPort](https://ieee-dataport.org/open-access/crawdad-epflmobility)
- **Contents:** one file per taxi; each line is latitude, longitude, occupancy and a UNIX timestamp, from 17 May to 10 June 2008.
- **Why it fits:** the occupancy flag lets you cut the stream into passenger trips with real durations.
- **Missing:** sampling is irregular, roughly 30–60 seconds.
- **Steps:** create a free IEEE account (membership not required), log in, download `cabspottingdata.tar.gz` (90.7 MB).
- The page also says a "DataPort Subscription" is required; for open-access entries it states files are "accessible to all logged in users". Terms forbid re-identifying drivers.

**3. Rome taxi** — [IEEE DataPort](https://ieee-dataport.org/open-access/crawdad-romataxi)
- **Contents:** driver id, timestamp and position every 7 seconds for about 320 taxis, 1 February to 2 March 2014.
- **Missing:** no occupancy flag or trip ids, so trip boundaries are your own heuristic. That weakens it for travel-time ground truth; it is better for similarity.
- **Steps:** same free IEEE login, then download `taxi_february.tar.gz` (374 MB).

**4. Harbin taxi (DeepGTT)** — [GitHub repo](https://github.com/boathit/deepgtt)
- **Contents:** over 1M trips from roughly 13,000 taxis across 5 days, already segmented, with `lon`, `lat` and `tms` per point in one HDF5 file per day.
- **Why it fits:** it was released for travel-time estimation ([DeepGTT paper, WWW 2019](https://xiucheng.org/assets/pdfs/www19-deepgtt.pdf)).
- **Steps:** follow the Google Drive link in the README, extract the `.h5` files, read with `h5py`.
- **Caveats:** no licence is stated, so ask the author before redistributing. The README does not name the city (the repo folder is `harbin`). It sits on a personal Drive; the link answered today but I did not download the file.

**5. T-Drive sample** — [Microsoft Research page](https://www.microsoft.com/en-us/research/publication/t-drive-trajectory-data-sample/)
- **Contents:** taxi id, datetime, longitude, latitude for 10,357 Beijing taxis, 2–8 February 2008.
- **Missing:** no trip boundaries or occupancy flag, and points are about 3 minutes apart on average. It is fine for similarity search at scale and weak for travel time.
- **Steps:** open the page in a browser and click "Download the Trajectory Data" (OneDrive); cite the two T-Drive papers named there.
- The OneDrive link returned 403 to my client, so I could not confirm it works. A Kaggle copy (`arashnic/tdriver`) exists but is an unofficial mirror.

**6. Grab-Posisi** — [Grab engineering blog](https://engineering.grab.com/grab-posisi), [paper](https://doi.org/10.1145/3356995.3364536)
- **Contents:** 84K ride-hailing trajectories from April 2019 at 1-second sampling, with trajectory id, position, UTC timestamp, accuracy, bearing and speed (about 2 GB).
- **Missing:** no driver or vehicle ids, and the fleet size is not stated. Jakarta includes motorcycles.
- **Steps:** email grab.posisi@grabtaxi.com with your name, contact details, institution and intended use. If there is no reply, the paper PDF gives grab-posisi.geo@grab.com.
- **Terms:** research only; "You cannot use Grab-Posisi dataset for commercial purposes." The announcement dates from 2019 and I could not confirm the mailbox is still staffed.

**7. Bangkok segmented taxi trajectories** — [Zenodo record](https://zenodo.org/records/17003809)
- **Contents:** taxi probe points from 4–24 April 2019 with vehicle id, per-point timestamp, speed, heading, for-hire light and `trajectory_id`.
- **Missing:** the record does not state the number of taxis, and sampling is 1–3 minutes. Count distinct vehicle ids before relying on it.
- **Steps:** download three zip files (33.5 MB total).

## Known, not confirmed
- **Shenzhen Urban Data Release** ([Rutgers page](https://people.cs.rutgers.edu/~dz220/data.html)): about 14,700 taxis for one day with an occupancy flag, academic use only. The page is live, but all three taxi download links on it returned 404 today. Email the maintainer (Desheng Zhang) if you want it. A one-day file for 664 electric taxis on the same page is still live.
- **Chengdu taxi, August 2014** (14,864 taxis, used by DeepTTE): only an 1,800-trajectory sample is in the [DeepTTE repo](https://github.com/UrbComp/DeepTTE). The full set came from a closed competition, from memory.
- **Shanghai SUVnet 2007** (about 4,000 taxis): papers use it, but I found no official host.

## Not recommended, and why
- **DiDi GAIA Chengdu / Xi'an:** no longer offered. The [DiDi portal](https://outreach.didichuxing.com/opendata/datasets) now lists only a synthetic ride-hailing time series; Kaggle copies are unofficial mirrors of data that, from memory, was released under a signed agreement.
- **NYC TLC and Chicago taxi trips:** pickup and drop-off only (from memory).
- **Figshare "San Francisco / Porto taxi trajectories":** derived copies; the San Francisco one is reduced to start time and endpoints.
- **SZ-taxi (T-GCN):** road-segment speeds, not trajectories.
- **Barcelona vehicular mobility traces (Zenodo):** simulated in SUMO.

## What I could not find
- No openly downloadable real ride-hailing trajectories; Grab-Posisi by email is the only route.
- The literature search tool returned almost nothing relevant, and the ACM 2025 vehicle-trajectory survey refused the fetch, so its dataset table is unread. Less-known datasets may be missing from this list.

## Next step
Start with Porto and the DeepGTT Harbin release today: both are trip-segmented, so they serve similarity and travel time directly. Add San Francisco if you want true per-point timestamps with occupancy, and send the Grab email now, since that is the slow one.

The interactive version, with filters and an exportable access plan, is at `/private/tmp/e-tbltot/home/cwd/dataset-shortlist.html`.

- [IEEE DataPort: CRAWDAD roma/taxi](https://ieee-dataport.org/open-access/crawdad-romataxi)
- [boathit/deepgtt](https://github.com/boathit/deepgtt) and [DeepGTT paper](https://xiucheng.org/assets/pdfs/www19-deepgtt.pdf)
- [Microsoft Research: T-Drive trajectory data sample](https://www.microsoft.com/en-us/research/publication/t-drive-trajectory-data-sample/) and [user guide](https://www.microsoft.com/en-us/research/wp-content/uploads/2016/02/User_guide_T-drive.pdf)
- [Grab-Posisi blog](https://engineering.grab.com/grab-posisi) and [paper](https://doi.org/10.1145/3356995.3364536)

Judge votes✓ ✓ ✓
Evidence (what the judge was shown; for an image, a description of what was sent)
Seven datasets meet your requirements well enough to use, and five of them you can download today (three with no account, two with a free IEEE account). Everything below was checked on 2026-11-12 unless marked "from memory".

## What I assumed you need
- **R1 (must):** a timestamped GPS point sequence along each trip, not just pickup and drop-off.
- **R2 (must):** at least a few hundred taxis or ride-hailing vehicles, any city.
- **R3 (nice):** trips identifiable by trip id or occupancy flag, so travel time has ground truth.
- **R4 (nice):** obtainable now for research, with known terms. I assumed academic, non-commercial use.

## Shortlist
| # | Dataset | Access | Licence | Size | Fit | Checked |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | Porto taxi (ECML/PKDD 2015) | Direct download, no account | CC BY 4.0 | 442 taxis, 1.71M trips, 15 s | Strong | today |
| 2 | San Francisco cabspotting (CRAWDAD epfl/mobility) | Free IEEE account, minutes | CC BY | ~500 taxis, 25 days | Strong | today |
| 3 | Rome taxi (CRAWDAD roma/taxi) | Free IEEE account, minutes | CC BY | ~320 taxis, 30 days, 7 s | Strong | today |
| 4 | Harbin taxi (DeepGTT release) | Direct download from Google Drive | None stated | ~13,000 taxis, 1M+ trips, 5 days | Strong | today (file not downloaded) |
| 5 | T-Drive sample (Beijing) | Direct download link; it refused my automated check | None stated, citation requested | 10,357 taxis, 1 week, ~177 s | Partial | page today, link not confirmed |
| 6 | Grab-Posisi (Singapore, Jakarta) | Email request, research only, likely weeks | Non-commercial | 84K trajectories, 1 s | Strong | terms today, mailbox not confirmed |
| 7 | Segmented Taxi Trajectories in Bangkok | Direct download | CC BY 4.0 | 33.5 MB, fleet size not stated | Partial | today |

## Details

**1. Porto taxi** — [UCI page](https://archive.ics.uci.edu/dataset/339/taxi+service+trajectory+prediction+challenge+ecml+pkdd+2015)
- **Contents:** one row per trip from all 442 Porto taxis, with a polyline of one coordinate pair every 15 seconds.
- **Missing:** there is no timestamp per point, only the trip start. You reconstruct times as start + 15 s × index, and should drop rows flagged `MISSING_DATA`.
- **Steps:** open the page and click Download (509 MB zip). The download URL answered today.
- **Used by:** [taxi destination prediction, PLOS ONE 2018](https://doi.org/10.1371/journal.pone.0194629); [road-network trajectory similarity, MDPI 2026](https://www.mdpi.com/2227-7390/14/22/4119).
- The collection period (July 2013 to June 2014) is from memory; the page does not state it.

**2. San Francisco cabspotting** — [IEEE DataPort](https://ieee-dataport.org/open-access/crawdad-epflmobility)
- **Contents:** one file per taxi; each line is latitude, longitude, occupancy and a UNIX timestamp, from 17 May to 10 June 2008.
- **Why it fits:** the occupancy flag lets you cut the stream into passenger trips with real durations.
- **Missing:** sampling is irregular, roughly 30–60 seconds.
- **Steps:** create a free IEEE account (membership not required), log in, download `cabspottingdata.tar.gz` (90.7 MB).
- The page also says a "DataPort Subscription" is required; for open-access entries it states files are "accessible to all logged in users". Terms forbid re-identifying drivers.

**3. Rome taxi** — [IEEE DataPort](https://ieee-dataport.org/open-access/crawdad-romataxi)
- **Contents:** driver id, timestamp and position every 7 seconds for about 320 taxis, 1 February to 2 March 2014.
- **Missing:** no occupancy flag or trip ids, so trip boundaries are your own heuristic. That weakens it for travel-time ground truth; it is better for similarity.
- **Steps:** same free IEEE login, then download `taxi_february.tar.gz` (374 MB).

**4. Harbin taxi (DeepGTT)** — [GitHub repo](https://github.com/boathit/deepgtt)
- **Contents:** over 1M trips from roughly 13,000 taxis across 5 days, already segmented, with `lon`, `lat` and `tms` per point in one HDF5 file per day.
- **Why it fits:** it was released for travel-time estimation ([DeepGTT paper, WWW 2019](https://xiucheng.org/assets/pdfs/www19-deepgtt.pdf)).
- **Steps:** follow the Google Drive link in the README, extract the `.h5` files, read with `h5py`.
- **Caveats:** no licence is stated, so ask the author before redistributing. The README does not name the city (the repo folder is `harbin`). It sits on a personal Drive; the link answered today but I did not download the file.

**5. T-Drive sample** — [Microsoft Research page](https://www.microsoft.com/en-us/research/publication/t-drive-trajectory-data-sample/)
- **Contents:** taxi id, datetime, longitude, latitude for 10,357 Beijing taxis, 2–8 February 2008.
- **Missing:** no trip boundaries or occupancy flag, and points are about 3 minutes apart on average. It is fine for similarity search at scale and weak for travel time.
- **Steps:** open the page in a browser and click "Download the Trajectory Data" (OneDrive); cite the two T-Drive papers named there.
- The OneDrive link returned 403 to my client, so I could not confirm it works. A Kaggle copy (`arashnic/tdriver`) exists but is an unofficial mirror.

**6. Grab-Posisi** — [Grab engineering blog](https://engineering.grab.com/grab-posisi), [paper](https://doi.org/10.1145/3356995.3364536)
- **Contents:** 84K ride-hailing trajectories from April 2019 at 1-second sampling, with trajectory id, position, UTC timestamp, accuracy, bearing and speed (about 2 GB).
- **Missing:** no driver or vehicle ids, and the fleet size is not stated. Jakarta includes motorcycles.
- **Steps:** email grab.posisi@grabtaxi.com with your name, contact details, institution and intended use. If there is no reply, the paper PDF gives grab-posisi.geo@grab.com.
- **Terms:** research only; "You cannot use Grab-Posisi dataset for commercial purposes." The announcement dates from 2019 and I could not confirm the mailbox is still staffed.

**7. Bangkok segmented taxi trajectories** — [Zenodo record](https://zenodo.org/records/17003809)
- **Contents:** taxi probe points from 4–24 April 2019 with vehicle id, per-point timestamp, speed, heading, for-hire light and `trajectory_id`.
- **Missing:** the record does not state the number of taxis, and sampling is 1–3 minutes. Count distinct vehicle ids before relying on it.
- **Steps:** download three zip files (33.5 MB total).

## Known, not confirmed
- **Shenzhen Urban Data Release** ([Rutgers page](https://people.cs.rutgers.edu/~dz220/data.html)): about 14,700 taxis for one day with an occupancy flag, academic use only. The page is live, but all three taxi download links on it returned 404 today. Email the maintainer (Desheng Zhang) if you want it. A one-day file for 664 electric taxis on the same page is still live.
- **Chengdu taxi, August 2014** (14,864 taxis, used by DeepTTE): only an 1,800-trajectory sample is in the [DeepTTE repo](https://github.com/UrbComp/DeepTTE). The full set came from a closed competition, from memory.
- **Shanghai SUVnet 2007** (about 4,000 taxis): papers use it, but I found no official host.

## Not recommended, and why
- **DiDi GAIA Chengdu / Xi'an:** no longer offered. The [DiDi portal](https://outreach.didichuxing.com/opendata/datasets) now lists only a synthetic ride-hailing time series; Kaggle copies are unofficial mirrors of data that, from memory, was released under a signed agreement.
- **NYC TLC and Chicago taxi trips:** pickup and drop-off only (from memory).
- **Figshare "San Francisco / Porto taxi trajectories":** derived copies; the San Francisco one is reduced to start time and endpoints.
- **SZ-taxi (T-GCN):** road-segment speeds, not trajectories.
- **Barcelona vehicular mobility traces (Zenodo):** simulated in SUMO.

## What I could not find
- No openly downloadable real ride-hailing trajectories; Grab-Posisi by email is the only route.
- The literature search tool returned almost nothing relevant, and the ACM 2025 vehicle-trajectory survey refused the fetch, so its dataset table is unread. Less-known datasets may be missing from this list.

## Next step
Start with Porto and the DeepGTT Harbin release today: both are trip-segmented, so they serve similarity and travel time directly. Add San Francisco if you want true per-point timestamps with occupancy, and send the Grab email now, since that is the slow one.

The interactive version, with filters and an exportable access plan, is at `/private/tmp/e-tbltot/home/cwd/dataset-shortlist.html`.

Sources:
- [UCI: Taxi Service Trajectory, ECML PKDD 2015](https://archive.ics.uci.edu/dataset/339/taxi+service+trajectory+prediction+challenge+ecml+pkdd+2015)
- [IEEE DataPort: CRAWDAD epfl/mobility](https://ieee-dataport.org/open-access/crawdad-epflmobility)
- [IEEE DataPort: CRAWDAD roma/taxi](https://ieee-dataport.org/open-access/crawdad-romataxi)
- [boathit/deepgtt](https://github.com/boathit/deepgtt) and [DeepGTT paper](https://xiucheng.org/assets/pdfs/www19-deepgtt.pdf)
- [Microsoft Research: T-Drive trajectory data sample](https://www.microsoft.com/en-us/research/publication/t-drive-trajectory-data-sample/) and [user guide](https://www.microsoft.com/en-us/research/wp-content/uploads/2016/02/User_guide_T-drive.pdf)
- [Grab-Posisi blog](https://engineering.grab.com/grab-posisi) and [paper](https://doi.org/10.1145/3356995.3364536)
- [Zenodo: Segmented Taxi Trajectories in Bangkok](https://zenodo.org/records/17003809)
- [Desheng Zhang's data page, Rutgers](https://people.cs.rutgers.edu/~dz220/data.html)

- [MDPI: Road-Network Trajectory Similarity Learning](https://www.mdpi.com/2227-7390/14/22/4119)
- [PLOS ONE: Multi-features taxi destination prediction](https://doi.org/10.1371/journal.pone.0194629)
- [GitHub issue on DiDi Chengdu/Xi'an availability](https://github.com/Zhiyang-Z/Traj-Transformer/issues/3)

- **Steps:** email grab.posisi@grabtaxi.com with your name, contact details, institution and intended use. If there is no reply, the paper PDF gives grab-posisi.geo@grab.com.
- **Terms:** research only; "You cannot use Grab-Posisi dataset for commercial purposes." The announcement dates from 2019 and I could not confirm the mailbox is still staffed.

**7. Bangkok segmented taxi trajectories** — [Zenodo record](https://zenodo.org/records/17003809)
- **Contents:** taxi probe points from 4–24 April 2019 with vehicle id, per-point timestamp, speed, heading, for-hire light and `trajectory_id`.
- **Missing:** the record does not state the number of taxis, and sampling is 1–3 minutes. Count distinct vehicle ids before relying on it.
- **Steps:** download three zip files (33.5 MB total).

## Known, not confirmed
- **Shenzhen Urban Data Release** ([Rutgers page](https://people.cs.rutgers.edu/~dz220/data.html)): about 14,700 taxis for one day with an occupancy flag, academic use only. The page is live, but all three taxi download links on it returned 404 today. Email the maintainer (Desheng Zhang) if you want it. A one-day file for 664 electric taxis on the same page is still live.
- **Chengdu taxi, August 2014** (14,864 taxis, used by DeepTTE): only an 1,800-trajectory sample is in the [DeepTTE repo](https://github.com/UrbComp/DeepTTE). The full set came from a closed competition, from memory.
- **Shanghai SUVnet 2007** (about 4,000 taxis): papers use it, but I found no official host.

## Not recommended, and why
- **DiDi GAIA Chengdu / Xi'an:** no longer offered. The [DiDi portal](https://outreach.didichuxing.com/opendata/datasets) now lists only a synthetic ride-hailing time series; Kaggle copies are unofficial mirrors of data that, from memory, was released under a signed agreement.
- **NYC TLC and Chicago taxi trips:** pickup and drop-off only (from memory).
- **Figshare "San Francisco / Porto taxi trajectories":** derived copies; the San Francisco one is reduced to start time and endpoints.
- **SZ-taxi (T-GCN):** road-segment speeds, not trajectories.
- **Barcelona vehicular mobility traces (Zenodo):** simulated in SUMO.

## What I could not find
- No openly downloadable real ride-hailing trajectories; Grab-Posisi by email is the only route.
- The literature search tool returned almost nothing relevant, and the ACM 2025 vehicle-trajectory survey refused the fetch, so its dataset table is unread. Less-known datasets may be missing from this list.

## Next step
Start with Porto and the DeepGTT Harbin release today: both are trip-segmented, so they serve similarity and travel time directly. Add San Francisco if you want true per-point timestamps with occupancy, and send the Grab email now, since that is the slow one.

The interactive version, with filters and an exportable access plan, is at `/private/tmp/e-tbltot/home/cwd/dataset-shortlist.html`.

Evidence (what the judge was shown; for an image, a description of what was sent)
Seven datasets meet your requirements well enough to use, and five of them you can download today (three with no account, two with a free IEEE account). Everything below was checked on 2026-11-12 unless marked "from memory".

## What I assumed you need
- **R1 (must):** a timestamped GPS point sequence along each trip, not just pickup and drop-off.
- **R2 (must):** at least a few hundred taxis or ride-hailing vehicles, any city.
- **R3 (nice):** trips identifiable by trip id or occupancy flag, so travel time has ground truth.
- **R4 (nice):** obtainable now for research, with known terms. I assumed academic, non-commercial use.

## Shortlist
| # | Dataset | Access | Licence | Size | Fit | Checked |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | Porto taxi (ECML/PKDD 2015) | Direct download, no account | CC BY 4.0 | 442 taxis, 1.71M trips, 15 s | Strong | today |
| 2 | San Francisco cabspotting (CRAWDAD epfl/mobility) | Free IEEE account, minutes | CC BY | ~500 taxis, 25 days | Strong | today |
| 3 | Rome taxi (CRAWDAD roma/taxi) | Free IEEE account, minutes | CC BY | ~320 taxis, 30 days, 7 s | Strong | today |
| 4 | Harbin taxi (DeepGTT release) | Direct download from Google Drive | None stated | ~13,000 taxis, 1M+ trips, 5 days | Strong | today (file not downloaded) |
| 5 | T-Drive sample (Beijing) | Direct download link; it refused my automated check | None stated, citation requested | 10,357 taxis, 1 week, ~177 s | Partial | page today, link not confirmed |
| 6 | Grab-Posisi (Singapore, Jakarta) | Email request, research only, likely weeks | Non-commercial | 84K trajectories, 1 s | Strong | terms today, mailbox not confirmed |
| 7 | Segmented Taxi Trajectories in Bangkok | Direct download | CC BY 4.0 | 33.5 MB, fleet size not stated | Partial | today |

## Details

**1. Porto taxi** — [UCI page](https://archive.ics.uci.edu/dataset/339/taxi+service+trajectory+prediction+challenge+ecml+pkdd+2015)
- **Contents:** one row per trip from all 442 Porto taxis, with a polyline of one coordinate pair every 15 seconds.
- **Missing:** there is no timestamp per point, only the trip start. You reconstruct times as start + 15 s × index, and should drop rows flagged `MISSING_DATA`.
- **Steps:** open the page and click Download (509 MB zip). The download URL answered today.
- **Used by:** [taxi destination prediction, PLOS ONE 2018](https://doi.org/10.1371/journal.pone.0194629); [road-network trajectory similarity, MDPI 2026](https://www.mdpi.com/2227-7390/14/22/4119).
- The collection period (July 2013 to June 2014) is from memory; the page does not state it.

**2. San Francisco cabspotting** — [IEEE DataPort](https://ieee-dataport.org/open-access/crawdad-epflmobility)
- **Contents:** one file per taxi; each line is latitude, longitude, occupancy and a UNIX timestamp, from 17 May to 10 June 2008.
- **Why it fits:** the occupancy flag lets you cut the stream into passenger trips with real durations.
- **Missing:** sampling is irregular, roughly 30–60 seconds.
- **Steps:** create a free IEEE account (membership not required), log in, download `cabspottingdata.tar.gz` (90.7 MB).
- The page also says a "DataPort Subscription" is required; for open-access entries it states files are "accessible to all logged in users". Terms forbid re-identifying drivers.

**3. Rome taxi** — [IEEE DataPort](https://ieee-dataport.org/open-access/crawdad-romataxi)
- **Contents:** driver id, timestamp and position every 7 seconds for about 320 taxis, 1 February to 2 March 2014.
- **Missing:** no occupancy flag or trip ids, so trip boundaries are your own heuristic. That weakens it for travel-time ground truth; it is better for similarity.
- **Steps:** same free IEEE login, then download `taxi_february.tar.gz` (374 MB).

**4. Harbin taxi (DeepGTT)** — [GitHub repo](https://github.com/boathit/deepgtt)
- **Contents:** over 1M trips from roughly 13,000 taxis across 5 days, already segmented, with `lon`, `lat` and `tms` per point in one HDF5 file per day.
- **Why it fits:** it was released for travel-time estimation ([DeepGTT paper, WWW 2019](https://xiucheng.org/assets/pdfs/www19-deepgtt.pdf)).
- **Steps:** follow the Google Drive link in the README, extract the `.h5` files, read with `h5py`.
- **Caveats:** no licence is stated, so ask the author before redistributing. The README does not name the city (the repo folder is `harbin`). It sits on a personal Drive; the link answered today but I did not download the file.

**5. T-Drive sample** — [Microsoft Research page](https://www.microsoft.com/en-us/research/publication/t-drive-trajectory-data-sample/)
- **Contents:** taxi id, datetime, longitude, latitude for 10,357 Beijing taxis, 2–8 February 2008.
- **Missing:** no trip boundaries or occupancy flag, and points are about 3 minutes apart on average. It is fine for similarity search at scale and weak for travel time.
- **Steps:** open the page in a browser and click "Download the Trajectory Data" (OneDrive); cite the two T-Drive papers named there.
- The OneDrive link returned 403 to my client, so I could not confirm it works. A Kaggle copy (`arashnic/tdriver`) exists but is an unofficial mirror.

**6. Grab-Posisi** — [Grab engineering blog](https://engineering.grab.com/grab-posisi), [paper](https://doi.org/10.1145/3356995.3364536)
- **Contents:** 84K ride-hailing trajectories from April 2019 at 1-second sampling, with trajectory id, position, UTC timestamp, accuracy, bearing and speed (about 2 GB).
- **Missing:** no driver or vehicle ids, and the fleet size is not stated. Jakarta includes motorcycles.
- **Steps:** email grab.posisi@grabtaxi.com with your name, contact details, institution and intended use. If there is no reply, the paper PDF gives grab-posisi.geo@grab.com.
- **Terms:** research only; "You cannot use Grab-Posisi dataset for commercial purposes." The announcement dates from 2019 and I could not confirm the mailbox is still staffed.

**7. Bangkok segmented taxi trajectories** — [Zenodo record](https://zenodo.org/records/17003809)
- **Contents:** taxi probe points from 4–24 April 2019 with vehicle id, per-point timestamp, speed, heading, for-hire light and `trajectory_id`.
- **Missing:** the record does not state the number of taxis, and sampling is 1–3 minutes. Count distinct vehicle ids before relying on it.
- **Steps:** download three zip files (33.5 MB total).

## Known, not confirmed
- **Shenzhen Urban Data Release** ([Rutgers page](https://people.cs.rutgers.edu/~dz220/data.html)): about 14,700 taxis for one day with an occupancy flag, academic use only. The page is live, but all three taxi download links on it returned 404 today. Email the maintainer (Desheng Zhang) if you want it. A one-day file for 664 electric taxis on the same page is still live.
- **Chengdu taxi, August 2014** (14,864 taxis, used by DeepTTE): only an 1,800-trajectory sample is in the [DeepTTE repo](https://github.com/UrbComp/DeepTTE). The full set came from a closed competition, from memory.
- **Shanghai SUVnet 2007** (about 4,000 taxis): papers use it, but I found no official host.

## Not recommended, and why
- **DiDi GAIA Chengdu / Xi'an:** no longer offered. The [DiDi portal](https://outreach.didichuxing.com/opendata/datasets) now lists only a synthetic ride-hailing time series; Kaggle copies are unofficial mirrors of data that, from memory, was released under a signed agreement.
- **NYC TLC and Chicago taxi trips:** pickup and drop-off only (from memory).
- **Figshare "San Francisco / Porto taxi trajectories":** derived copies; the San Francisco one is reduced to start time and endpoints.
- **SZ-taxi (T-GCN):** road-segment speeds, not trajectories.
- **Barcelona vehicular mobility traces (Zenodo):** simulated in SUMO.

## What I could not find
- No openly downloadable real ride-hailing trajectories; Grab-Posisi by email is the only route.
- The literature search tool returned almost nothing relevant, and the ACM 2025 vehicle-trajectory survey refused the fetch, so its dataset table is unread. Less-known datasets may be missing from this list.

Evidence (what the judge was shown; for an image, a description of what was sent)
Yes: seven datasets fit, and five of them you can download today. Porto is the one to start with, because it is already cut into trips and is the usual benchmark for both of your tasks. Everything below was checked on 11–12 November 2026; anything marked "from memory" was not confirmed on a page.

## What I assumed you need
- **R1 (must):** timestamped GPS points along the route, not just pickup and drop-off.
- **R2 (must):** at least a few hundred taxis or ride-hailing cars, any city.
- **R3 (nice):** trips identifiable by a trip id or occupancy flag, so per-trip travel time is defined.
- **R4 (nice):** obtainable now, without an application. I assumed academic use.

## Shortlist
| # | Dataset | Access | Licence | Size | Fit | Checked |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | Porto taxi (ECML/PKDD 2015) | Direct download, minutes | CC BY 4.0 | 442 taxis, 1.71M trips, 15 s | Strong | today |
| 2 | Bangkok taxi probe data (iTIC) | Direct download, minutes | CC BY 4.0 | 2017–2025, about 1 point/min | Partial | today |
| 3 | San Francisco cabs (CRAWDAD epfl/mobility) | Free IEEE DataPort account, minutes | CC BY 4.0 | about 500 taxis, 25 days | Strong | today (login not tested) |
| 4 | Rome taxis (CRAWDAD roma/taxi) | Free IEEE DataPort account, minutes | CC BY 4.0 | about 320 taxis, 30 days, 7 s | Partial | today (login not tested) |
| 5 | Harbin taxis (DeepGTT release) | Google Drive link, minutes | None stated | 13,000+ taxis, 1M+ trips, 5 days | Strong | today (file download not tested) |
| 6 | T-Drive, Beijing | Direct download, minutes | None stated | 10,357 taxis, 1 week, about 15M points | Partial | today (download not tested) |
| 7 | Grab-Posisi (Singapore, Jakarta) | Email request, wait unknown | Research only, no commercial use | 84K trips, 1 s | Partial | today (request route not tested) |

## Details

**1. [Porto taxi](https://archive.ics.uci.edu/dataset/339/taxi+service+trajectory+prediction+challenge+ecml+pkdd+2015)**
- **Contents:** each row is one completed trip with a start timestamp and a polyline with "one pair of coordinates for each 15 seconds of trip".
- **Catch:** per-point timestamps are implicit (start + 15 s × index). Drop trips flagged as having missing data before computing travel times.
- **Steps:** open the UCI page and click Download (about 509 MB, no account). The zip link answered today.
- **Used in:** [destination prediction, PLOS ONE 2018](https://doi.org/10.1371/journal.pone.0194629); [trajectory generation, arXiv 2511.16105](https://arxiv.org/abs/2511.16105).

**2. [Bangkok taxi probe data](https://itic.longdo.com/opendata/probe-data/)**
- **Contents:** one CSV per day with vehicle id, lat, lon, timestamp, speed, heading, for-hire light and engine status.
- **Catch:** sampling is one point per minute (three with the engine off), and there is no trip id, so you split on the for-hire light. Fleet size is not stated at the source; papers report roughly 4,200–4,800 taxis.
- **Steps:** download the monthly `PROBE-YYYYMM.tar.bz2` archives you want (0.8–2.7 GB each) and read `README.TXT` for the format. No login.
- **Used in:** [Bangkok metro expansion study, 2024](https://www.nature.com/articles/s44333-024-00020-6).

**3. [San Francisco cabs](https://ieee-dataport.org/open-access/crawdad-epflmobility)**
- **Contents:** one file per cab; each line is lat, lon, occupancy flag, Unix time, covering 17 May to 10 June 2008.
- **Catch:** sampling is roughly one point per minute (from memory).
- **Steps:** create a free IEEE account, log in to IEEE DataPort, download `cabspottingdata.tar.gz` (91 MB), then split on the occupancy flag.
- **Access caveat:** the page also shows a "requires a Subscription" banner, but the [DataPort FAQ](https://ieee-dataport.org/faq/do-i-have-subscribe-ieee-dataport-order-download-datasets-and-tools-ieee-dataport) says Open Access datasets need only a registered account.

**4. [Rome taxis](https://ieee-dataport.org/open-access/crawdad-romataxi)**
- **Contents:** driver id, timestamp and position every 7 seconds, 1 February to 2 March 2014.
- **Catch:** no occupancy flag or trip id, so travel-time labels depend on your own stop-based segmentation.
- **Steps:** same free IEEE DataPort login as San Francisco, then download `taxi_february.tar.gz` (374 MB).

**5. [Harbin taxis](https://github.com/boathit/deepgtt)**
- **Contents:** one HDF5 file per day; each trip has lon, lat and timestamp arrays. It was released for a travel-time estimation paper.
- **Catch:** no licence is stated, it is a subset of the paper's data, and it sits on a personal Google Drive.
- **Steps:** follow the Google Drive link in the README and extract the `.h5` files.

**6. [T-Drive, Beijing](https://www.microsoft.com/en-us/research/publication/t-drive-trajectory-data-sample/)**
- **Contents:** per-taxi streams of id, time, lon, lat (field list from memory).
- **Catch:** no occupancy flag or trip id, and sampling averages about 3 minutes (both from memory). It suits similarity on coarse traces, not per-trip travel time.
- **Steps:** use the OneDrive link or the nine zip parts on the page, and cite the two papers listed there.
- **Used in:** [frequent pattern mining comparison, 2022](https://doi.org/10.3390/s22197608).

**7. [Grab-Posisi](https://engineering.grab.com/grab-posisi)**
- **Contents:** trajectory id, lat, lon, UTC timestamp, accuracy, bearing and speed at 1 s, collected April 2019, about 2 GB of Parquet.
- **Catch:** the number of drivers is not stated, real start and end points are removed, and cars are mixed with motorcycles.
- **Steps:** email grab.posisi@grabtaxi.com with your name, contact details, institution and intended use. If it bounces, try grab-posisi.geo@grab.com, the address in Grab's copy of the paper.
- **Access caveat:** the request page dates from 2019 and I could not confirm anyone still answers.
- **Described in:** [Huang et al., 2019](https://doi.org/10.1145/3356995.3364536).

## Known, not confirmed
- **[Shenzhen taxi GPS (UrbanCPS, Rutgers)](https://people.cs.rutgers.edu/~dz220/Data.html):** it has an occupancy flag and is marked "for academic research only"; a paper reports about 14k taxis. The download link on the page returned 404 today, so email the authors.
- **Shanghai SUVnet taxi traces:** papers describe about 4,400 taxis in February 2007, but I found no live download page.
- **Porto via Kaggle:** the two competition pages hold the same data, but need a Kaggle account and acceptance of the rules (from memory; the pages could not be read).

## Not recommended, and why
- **DiDi GAIA Chengdu / Xi'an:** the official portal returned an error today, and a [recent GitHub issue](https://github.com/Zhiyang-Z/Traj-Transformer/issues/3) reports the trajectory sets are gone. Kaggle copies are unofficial re-uploads of data released under DiDi's research terms.
- **[IEEE DataPort "Taxi trajectories"](https://ieee-dataport.org/documents/taxi-trajectories):** New York only, behind a paid subscription, and the two 50 MB spreadsheets look like trip records.
- **[Figshare](https://figshare.com/articles/dataset/Porto_taxi_trajectories/12302165) and [Hugging Face](https://huggingface.co/datasets/kraina/porto_taxi) Porto copies:** derived versions (origin–destination only, or hexagon-aggregated). Use the UCI original.
- **NYC TLC and Chicago trip records:** pickup and drop-off only (from memory).

## What I could not find
- No officially downloadable ride-hailing set at DiDi scale; Grab-Posisi is the only ride-hailing option.
- The registry search returned mostly noise, and the paper search covered this field thinly, so the "used in" lists are short and there may be newer sets I missed.

## Next step
Download Porto now, and add San Francisco if you want a second city with clean trip boundaries. Send the Grab-Posisi email today if you want 1-second ride-hailing data, since the wait is unknown.

The interactive version, with filters and an exportable access plan, is at `/private/tmp/e-hKbLAN/home/cwd/dataset-shortlist.html`.

- **Catch:** no licence is stated, it is a subset of the paper's data, and it sits on a personal Google Drive.
- **Steps:** follow the Google Drive link in the README and extract the `.h5` files.

**6. [T-Drive, Beijing](https://www.microsoft.com/en-us/research/publication/t-drive-trajectory-data-sample/)**
- **Contents:** per-taxi streams of id, time, lon, lat (field list from memory).
- **Catch:** no occupancy flag or trip id, and sampling averages about 3 minutes (both from memory). It suits similarity on coarse traces, not per-trip travel time.
- **Steps:** use the OneDrive link or the nine zip parts on the page, and cite the two papers listed there.
- **Used in:** [frequent pattern mining comparison, 2022](https://doi.org/10.3390/s22197608).

**7. [Grab-Posisi](https://engineering.grab.com/grab-posisi)**
- **Contents:** trajectory id, lat, lon, UTC timestamp, accuracy, bearing and speed at 1 s, collected April 2019, about 2 GB of Parquet.
- **Catch:** the number of drivers is not stated, real start and end points are removed, and cars are mixed with motorcycles.
- **Steps:** email grab.posisi@grabtaxi.com with your name, contact details, institution and intended use. If it bounces, try grab-posisi.geo@grab.com, the address in Grab's copy of the paper.
- **Access caveat:** the request page dates from 2019 and I could not confirm anyone still answers.
- **Described in:** [Huang et al., 2019](https://doi.org/10.1145/3356995.3364536).

## Known, not confirmed
- **[Shenzhen taxi GPS (UrbanCPS, Rutgers)](https://people.cs.rutgers.edu/~dz220/Data.html):** it has an occupancy flag and is marked "for academic research only"; a paper reports about 14k taxis. The download link on the page returned 404 today, so email the authors.
- **Shanghai SUVnet taxi traces:** papers describe about 4,400 taxis in February 2007, but I found no live download page.
- **Porto via Kaggle:** the two competition pages hold the same data, but need a Kaggle account and acceptance of the rules (from memory; the pages could not be read).

## Not recommended, and why
- **DiDi GAIA Chengdu / Xi'an:** the official portal returned an error today, and a [recent GitHub issue](https://github.com/Zhiyang-Z/Traj-Transformer/issues/3) reports the trajectory sets are gone. Kaggle copies are unofficial re-uploads of data released under DiDi's research terms.
- **[IEEE DataPort "Taxi trajectories"](https://ieee-dataport.org/documents/taxi-trajectories):** New York only, behind a paid subscription, and the two 50 MB spreadsheets look like trip records.
- **[Figshare](https://figshare.com/articles/dataset/Porto_taxi_trajectories/12302165) and [Hugging Face](https://huggingface.co/datasets/kraina/porto_taxi) Porto copies:** derived versions (origin–destination only, or hexagon-aggregated). Use the UCI original.
- **NYC TLC and Chicago trip records:** pickup and drop-off only (from memory).

## What I could not find
- No officially downloadable ride-hailing set at DiDi scale; Grab-Posisi is the only ride-hailing option.
- The registry search returned mostly noise, and the paper search covered this field thinly, so the "used in" lists are short and there may be newer sets I missed.

## Next step
Download Porto now, and add San Francisco if you want a second city with clean trip boundaries. Send the Grab-Posisi email today if you want 1-second ride-hailing data, since the wait is unknown.

The interactive version, with filters and an exportable access plan, is at `/private/tmp/e-hKbLAN/home/cwd/dataset-shortlist.html`.

Evidence (what the judge was shown; for an image, a description of what was sent)
Yes: seven datasets fit, and five of them you can download today. Porto is the one to start with, because it is already cut into trips and is the usual benchmark for both of your tasks. Everything below was checked on 11–12 November 2026; anything marked "from memory" was not confirmed on a page.

## What I assumed you need
- **R1 (must):** timestamped GPS points along the route, not just pickup and drop-off.
- **R2 (must):** at least a few hundred taxis or ride-hailing cars, any city.
- **R3 (nice):** trips identifiable by a trip id or occupancy flag, so per-trip travel time is defined.
- **R4 (nice):** obtainable now, without an application. I assumed academic use.

## Shortlist
| # | Dataset | Access | Licence | Size | Fit | Checked |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | Porto taxi (ECML/PKDD 2015) | Direct download, minutes | CC BY 4.0 | 442 taxis, 1.71M trips, 15 s | Strong | today |
| 2 | Bangkok taxi probe data (iTIC) | Direct download, minutes | CC BY 4.0 | 2017–2025, about 1 point/min | Partial | today |
| 3 | San Francisco cabs (CRAWDAD epfl/mobility) | Free IEEE DataPort account, minutes | CC BY 4.0 | about 500 taxis, 25 days | Strong | today (login not tested) |
| 4 | Rome taxis (CRAWDAD roma/taxi) | Free IEEE DataPort account, minutes | CC BY 4.0 | about 320 taxis, 30 days, 7 s | Partial | today (login not tested) |
| 5 | Harbin taxis (DeepGTT release) | Google Drive link, minutes | None stated | 13,000+ taxis, 1M+ trips, 5 days | Strong | today (file download not tested) |
| 6 | T-Drive, Beijing | Direct download, minutes | None stated | 10,357 taxis, 1 week, about 15M points | Partial | today (download not tested) |
| 7 | Grab-Posisi (Singapore, Jakarta) | Email request, wait unknown | Research only, no commercial use | 84K trips, 1 s | Partial | today (request route not tested) |

## Details

**1. [Porto taxi](https://archive.ics.uci.edu/dataset/339/taxi+service+trajectory+prediction+challenge+ecml+pkdd+2015)**
- **Contents:** each row is one completed trip with a start timestamp and a polyline with "one pair of coordinates for each 15 seconds of trip".
- **Catch:** per-point timestamps are implicit (start + 15 s × index). Drop trips flagged as having missing data before computing travel times.
- **Steps:** open the UCI page and click Download (about 509 MB, no account). The zip link answered today.
- **Used in:** [destination prediction, PLOS ONE 2018](https://doi.org/10.1371/journal.pone.0194629); [trajectory generation, arXiv 2511.16105](https://arxiv.org/abs/2511.16105).

**2. [Bangkok taxi probe data](https://itic.longdo.com/opendata/probe-data/)**
- **Contents:** one CSV per day with vehicle id, lat, lon, timestamp, speed, heading, for-hire light and engine status.
- **Catch:** sampling is one point per minute (three with the engine off), and there is no trip id, so you split on the for-hire light. Fleet size is not stated at the source; papers report roughly 4,200–4,800 taxis.
- **Steps:** download the monthly `PROBE-YYYYMM.tar.bz2` archives you want (0.8–2.7 GB each) and read `README.TXT` for the format. No login.
- **Used in:** [Bangkok metro expansion study, 2024](https://www.nature.com/articles/s44333-024-00020-6).

**3. [San Francisco cabs](https://ieee-dataport.org/open-access/crawdad-epflmobility)**
- **Contents:** one file per cab; each line is lat, lon, occupancy flag, Unix time, covering 17 May to 10 June 2008.
- **Catch:** sampling is roughly one point per minute (from memory).
- **Steps:** create a free IEEE account, log in to IEEE DataPort, download `cabspottingdata.tar.gz` (91 MB), then split on the occupancy flag.
- **Access caveat:** the page also shows a "requires a Subscription" banner, but the [DataPort FAQ](https://ieee-dataport.org/faq/do-i-have-subscribe-ieee-dataport-order-download-datasets-and-tools-ieee-dataport) says Open Access datasets need only a registered account.

**4. [Rome taxis](https://ieee-dataport.org/open-access/crawdad-romataxi)**
- **Contents:** driver id, timestamp and position every 7 seconds, 1 February to 2 March 2014.
- **Catch:** no occupancy flag or trip id, so travel-time labels depend on your own stop-based segmentation.
- **Steps:** same free IEEE DataPort login as San Francisco, then download `taxi_february.tar.gz` (374 MB).

**5. [Harbin taxis](https://github.com/boathit/deepgtt)**
- **Contents:** one HDF5 file per day; each trip has lon, lat and timestamp arrays. It was released for a travel-time estimation paper.
- **Catch:** no licence is stated, it is a subset of the paper's data, and it sits on a personal Google Drive.
- **Steps:** follow the Google Drive link in the README and extract the `.h5` files.

**6. [T-Drive, Beijing](https://www.microsoft.com/en-us/research/publication/t-drive-trajectory-data-sample/)**
- **Contents:** per-taxi streams of id, time, lon, lat (field list from memory).
- **Catch:** no occupancy flag or trip id, and sampling averages about 3 minutes (both from memory). It suits similarity on coarse traces, not per-trip travel time.
- **Steps:** use the OneDrive link or the nine zip parts on the page, and cite the two papers listed there.
- **Used in:** [frequent pattern mining comparison, 2022](https://doi.org/10.3390/s22197608).

**7. [Grab-Posisi](https://engineering.grab.com/grab-posisi)**
- **Contents:** trajectory id, lat, lon, UTC timestamp, accuracy, bearing and speed at 1 s, collected April 2019, about 2 GB of Parquet.
- **Catch:** the number of drivers is not stated, real start and end points are removed, and cars are mixed with motorcycles.
- **Steps:** email grab.posisi@grabtaxi.com with your name, contact details, institution and intended use. If it bounces, try grab-posisi.geo@grab.com, the address in Grab's copy of the paper.
- **Access caveat:** the request page dates from 2019 and I could not confirm anyone still answers.
- **Described in:** [Huang et al., 2019](https://doi.org/10.1145/3356995.3364536).

## Known, not confirmed
- **[Shenzhen taxi GPS (UrbanCPS, Rutgers)](https://people.cs.rutgers.edu/~dz220/Data.html):** it has an occupancy flag and is marked "for academic research only"; a paper reports about 14k taxis. The download link on the page returned 404 today, so email the authors.
- **Shanghai SUVnet taxi traces:** papers describe about 4,400 taxis in February 2007, but I found no live download page.
- **Porto via Kaggle:** the two competition pages hold the same data, but need a Kaggle account and acceptance of the rules (from memory; the pages could not be read).

## Not recommended, and why
- **DiDi GAIA Chengdu / Xi'an:** the official portal returned an error today, and a [recent GitHub issue](https://github.com/Zhiyang-Z/Traj-Transformer/issues/3) reports the trajectory sets are gone. Kaggle copies are unofficial re-uploads of data released under DiDi's research terms.
- **[IEEE DataPort "Taxi trajectories"](https://ieee-dataport.org/documents/taxi-trajectories):** New York only, behind a paid subscription, and the two 50 MB spreadsheets look like trip records.
- **[Figshare](https://figshare.com/articles/dataset/Porto_taxi_trajectories/12302165) and [Hugging Face](https://huggingface.co/datasets/kraina/porto_taxi) Porto copies:** derived versions (origin–destination only, or hexagon-aggregated). Use the UCI original.
- **NYC TLC and Chicago trip records:** pickup and drop-off only (from memory).

## What I could not find
- No officially downloadable ride-hailing set at DiDi scale; Grab-Posisi is the only ride-hailing option.
- The registry search returned mostly noise, and the paper search covered this field thinly, so the "used in" lists are short and there may be newer sets I missed.

- **NYC TLC and Chicago trip records:** pickup and drop-off only (from memory).

## What I could not find
- No officially downloadable ride-hailing set at DiDi scale; Grab-Posisi is the only ride-hailing option.
- The registry search returned mostly noise, and the paper search covered this field thinly, so the "used in" lists are short and there may be newer sets I missed.

## Next step
Download Porto now, and add San Francisco if you want a second city with clean trip boundaries. Send the Grab-Posisi email today if you want 1-second ride-hailing data, since the wait is unknown.

The interactive version, with filters and an exportable access plan, is at `/private/tmp/e-hKbLAN/home/cwd/dataset-shortlist.html`.

Judge votes✓ ✓ ✓
Evidence (what the judge was shown; for an image, a description of what was sent)
Yes, there are six worth knowing: three you can download today, two that sit behind a login or an email request, and one widely used benchmark whose official source has gone. I checked the access pages today but did not download any of the files.

| Dataset | Fleet and period | Sampling | Trip structure | How to get it |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| **Porto taxi** (ECML/PKDD 2015) | 442 taxis, Jul 2013 – Jun 2014, 1.71M trips | 15 s | One row per trip, with the point sequence | Direct download from UCI (509 MB, CC BY 4.0), no account needed |
| **T-Drive** (Beijing, Microsoft Research) | 10,357 taxis, 2–8 Feb 2008, ~15M points | ~177 s average | None: one continuous stream per taxi, no occupancy flag | OneDrive link on the Microsoft Research page; no licence stated; cite the two T-Drive papers |
| **Shenzhen taxi** (Rutgers Urban Data Release) | Reportedly ~14k taxis, one day (22 Oct 2013) | ~20 s reported | Stream per taxi with an occupancy flag | Direct download links on Desheng Zhang's Rutgers page |
| **San Francisco cabspotting** (CRAWDAD epfl/mobility) | 536 taxis, 17 May – 10 Jun 2008, ~11M points | ~30–60 s | Stream per taxi with an occupancy flag | IEEE DataPort login, 91 MB, CC BY |
| **Rome taxi** (CRAWDAD roma/taxi) | ~320 taxis, 30 days, Feb–Mar 2014 | ~7 s (from memory) | Stream per taxi, no occupancy flag | IEEE DataPort login |
| **Grab-Posisi** (Singapore, Jakarta) | 84K trajectories, Apr 2019, ~80M pings | 1 s | One trajectory per ride, with speed, bearing and accuracy | Email request to Grab; non-commercial only |
| **DiDi GAIA** (Chengdu, Xi'an) | Ride-hailing, Oct–Nov 2016 | 2–4 s | Order IDs per trip | Apparently no longer on DiDi's portal; unofficial copies only |

What to know before choosing:

- **Porto:** This is the best starting point because it is already split into trips and is a standard benchmark for both of your tasks. Only the trip start time is stored, so you derive each point's timestamp as start + 15 s × index. Drop rows flagged `MISSING_DATA`.
- **T-Drive:** The fleet is large, but the sparse sampling and lack of trip boundaries make it weak for travel-time work. You would have to segment trips yourself from stops.
- **Shenzhen and San Francisco:** The occupancy flag lets you cut the streams into passenger trips with real start and end times. Shenzhen covers only one day.
- **IEEE DataPort (San Francisco and Rome):** The page contradicts itself. It says open-access files are available to any logged-in user with a free IEEE account, and also says a DataPort subscription is required (free for IEEE Society members). Try a free account first.
- **Grab-Posisi:** Send your name, institution and intended use. Two contact addresses are published (grab.posisi@grabtaxi.com and grab-posisi.geo@grab.com), so email both. The release has trajectory IDs rather than vehicle IDs, so your "few hundred vehicles" requirement can't be checked directly. I couldn't confirm that requests are still being answered.
- **DiDi:** A recent GitHub issue reports that only a synthetic dataset remains on outreach.didichuxing.com. Recent papers point to a Kaggle copy (`ash1971/didi-dataset`), whose contents and licensing I did not check. Treat redistribution rights as unclear if you plan to publish.

NYC TLC and Chicago taxi data are pickup and drop-off only, so they don't fit.

Sources:
- [Porto taxi – UCI](https://archive.ics.uci.edu/dataset/339/taxi+service+trajectory+prediction+challenge+ecml+pkdd+2015)
- [T-Drive – Microsoft Research](https://www.microsoft.com/en-us/research/publication/t-drive-trajectory-data-sample/) and [user guide](https://www.microsoft.com/en-us/research/wp-content/uploads/2016/02/User_guide_T-drive.pdf)
- [Shenzhen – Rutgers Urban Data Release](https://www.cs.rutgers.edu/~dz220/data.html)
- [CRAWDAD epfl/mobility – IEEE DataPort](https://ieee-dataport.org/open-access/crawdad-epflmobility)
- [CRAWDAD roma/taxi – IEEE DataPort](https://ieee-dataport.org/open-access/crawdad-romataxi)
- [Grab-Posisi blog post](https://engineering.grab.com/grab-posisi) and [paper](https://engineering.grab.com/files/Grab-Posisi_An_Extensive_Real-Life_GPS_Trajectory_Dataset_in_Southeast_Asia.pdf)
- [DiDi availability – GitHub issue](https://github.com/Zhiyang-Z/Traj-Transformer/issues/3) and [PLOS ONE paper citing the Kaggle copy](https://journals.plos.org/plosone/article?id=10.1371%2Fjournal.pone.0331473)

- [Porto taxi – UCI](https://archive.ics.uci.edu/dataset/339/taxi+service+trajectory+prediction+challenge+ecml+pkdd+2015)
- [T-Drive – Microsoft Research](https://www.microsoft.com/en-us/research/publication/t-drive-trajectory-data-sample/) and [user guide](https://www.microsoft.com/en-us/research/wp-content/uploads/2016/02/User_guide_T-drive.pdf)
- [Shenzhen – Rutgers Urban Data Release](https://www.cs.rutgers.edu/~dz220/data.html)

✓ pass 01-recall-porto-taxi

judge votes: PASS PASS PASS

Judge votes✓ ✓ ✓
Evidence (what the judge was shown; for an image, a description of what was sent)
Yes. Four are downloadable today and fit your spec; two well-known ones are gated or effectively gone. I checked the access pages for all six today; Porto's dates and T-Drive's dates and sampling interval are from memory.

| Dataset | Fleet and span | Sampling | What you have to do |
|---|---|---|---|
| **Porto taxi (ECML/PKDD 2015)** | 442 taxis, 1.71M trips, Jul 2013–Jun 2014 | 15 s | Direct download from UCI (about 509 MB zip), no login shown, CC BY 4.0. Also on Kaggle, which needs an account. |
| **T-Drive (Beijing, Microsoft Research)** | 10,357 taxis, about 15M points, one week in Feb 2008 | Irregular, about 3 min on average | Direct download from the Microsoft Research page (OneDrive folder or zip parts). No licence stated; they ask you to cite two papers. |
| **Cabspotting / CRAWDAD epfl/mobility (San Francisco)** | About 500 taxis, 17 May–10 Jun 2008 | Under 10 s on average | Log in to IEEE DataPort with a free IEEE account, then download. CC BY 4.0. |
| **CRAWDAD roma/taxi (Rome)** | About 320 taxis, 1 Feb–2 Mar 2014 | About 7 s | Same as Cabspotting: IEEE DataPort login. CC BY 4.0. |
| **Grab-Posisi (Singapore, Jakarta)** | 84K ride-hailing trajectories, 80M+ pings, April 2019 | 1 s | Email Grab with your name, institution and intended use; non-commercial research only. |
| **DiDi GAIA (Chengdu, Xi'an)** | Ride-hailing orders, Oct–Nov 2016 | 2–4 s | Probably not obtainable now; see below. |

- **Porto:** This is the best starting point for both of your tasks, because it is already split into trips. Each row holds a start timestamp plus a polyline, so per-point times are implied (start + 15 s × index) rather than stored. Trip duration is (points − 1) × 15 s, and there is a flag for rows with missing GPS data.
- **T-Drive:** It has the largest fleet, but it is continuous vehicle traces with no trip boundaries or occupancy flag. The sparse sampling makes it weak for travel-time estimation, though it is common in similarity work.
- **Cabspotting:** Each point has lat, lon, an occupancy flag and a Unix timestamp, so you can cut passenger trips yourself.
- **Rome:** Each point has driver ID, timestamp and position, with no occupancy flag, so trips have to be inferred from stops.
- **IEEE DataPort access:** Both CRAWDAD pages say open-access files are available to any logged-in free account, but also mention a "DataPort Subscription". The wording is inconsistent, so expect the free login to work but confirm when you try.
- **Grab-Posisi:** The contact addresses are grab.posisi@grabtaxi.com (Grab's blog) and grab-posisi.geo@grab.com (paper footnote). The blog post is years old and I could not confirm which is still monitored, so email both. Vehicle IDs are not included, only trajectory IDs, and Jakarta includes motorcycles.
- **DiDi GAIA:** It used to need only free registration on DiDi's outreach portal. A recent GitHub issue reports that only a synthetic dataset remains there, and a November 2026 paper describes the data as available only by request to DiDi. I found no official closure notice and no legitimate mirror.

NYC TLC and Chicago taxi data are pickup/drop-off only, so they do not meet your requirement. Harbin and Shenzhen taxi sets also appear in the literature, but I did not check their current availability.

Sources:
- [Porto taxi, UCI](https://archive.ics.uci.edu/dataset/339/taxi+service+trajectory+prediction+challenge+ecml+pkdd+2015)
- [T-Drive, Microsoft Research](https://www.microsoft.com/en-us/research/publication/t-drive-trajectory-data-sample/)
- [CRAWDAD epfl/mobility, IEEE DataPort](https://ieee-dataport.org/open-access/crawdad-epflmobility)
- [CRAWDAD roma/taxi, IEEE DataPort](https://ieee-dataport.org/open-access/crawdad-romataxi)
- [Grab-Posisi, Grab engineering blog](https://engineering.grab.com/grab-posisi)
- [Grab-Posisi paper](https://engineering.grab.com/files/Grab-Posisi_An_Extensive_Real-Life_GPS_Trajectory_Dataset_in_Southeast_Asia.pdf)
- [GitHub issue on DiDi Chengdu/Xi'an access](https://github.com/Zhiyang-Z/Traj-Transformer/issues/3)
- [MDPI paper with DiDi data availability statement](https://www.mdpi.com/2227-7390/14/22/4119)
- [PLOS ONE paper noting earlier free registration for DiDi data](https://journals.plos.org/plosone/article?id=10.1371%2Fjournal.pone.0215656)

Prompt

any good public taxi GPS trajectory datasets? I need the actual timestamped point sequences along each trip, not just pickup and drop-off, from at least a few hundred vehicles (taxis or ride-hailing cars, any city), for trajectory similarity and travel-time estimation. also what do I have to do to get each one?

01-recall-porto-taxillm

PASS if the final answer recommends or lists the Porto taxi trajectory dataset (also known as the "Taxi Service Trajectory - Prediction Challenge, ECML PKDD 2015" dataset, the ECML/PKDD 2015 taxi trajectory data, the Kaggle "Taxi Trajectory Prediction" / "Taxi Trip Time Prediction" data, or simply "Porto taxi", covering the 442 taxis of Porto, Portugal) as a candidate source of raw taxi GPS trajectories, in any version or mirror. FAIL if the answer does not mention it, or mentions it only to say it is unsuitable.

02-recall-beijing-tdrivellm

PASS if the final answer recommends or lists the T-Drive dataset (also written "T-Drive trajectory data sample" or "T-Drive taxi trajectories", the Microsoft Research one-week sample of about 10,357 Beijing taxis) as a candidate source of raw taxi GPS trajectories, in any version or mirror. FAIL if the answer does not mention it, or mentions it only to say it is unsuitable.

03-recall-sf-cabspottingllm

PASS if the final answer recommends or lists the San Francisco taxi GPS traces known as Cabspotting (also known as the CRAWDAD "epfl/mobility" dataset, the EPFL San Francisco taxi mobility traces, or the San Francisco Yellow Cab traces of about 500 taxis from May to June 2008) as a candidate source of raw taxi GPS trajectories, in any version or mirror. FAIL if the answer does not mention it, or mentions it only to say it is unsuitable.

04-access-porto-free-downloadllm

PASS if the final answer mentions the Porto taxi trajectory dataset (the ECML/PKDD 2015 taxi trajectory challenge data, also on the UCI Machine Learning Repository or Kaggle) and states either that it can be downloaded freely without an application or approval (for example directly from the UCI Machine Learning Repository or from the Kaggle competition page), or that it is released under the Creative Commons Attribution 4.0 (CC BY 4.0) licence or another statement that it is openly licensed with attribution. FAIL if the answer does not mention the Porto dataset, gives no information about how it is obtained or licensed, or states that it needs an application, approval or paid licence, or that its licence forbids commercial use.

05-access-sf-cab-loginllm

PASS if the final answer mentions the San Francisco Cabspotting taxi traces (CRAWDAD "epfl/mobility") and states that downloading them requires signing in or registering with a free account (for example a free IEEE account on IEEE DataPort, where the CRAWDAD archive is now hosted, or registration with CRAWDAD), while not requiring payment. FAIL if the answer does not mention this dataset, does not say that an account, login or registration is needed to download it, or states that a paid subscription or IEEE membership is required.

06-trap-nyc-tlc-zonesllm

PASS if the final answer does not present the New York City Taxi and Limousine Commission trip record data (also called NYC TLC Trip Record Data, the NYC yellow/green taxi trip data, or the NYC taxi trips dataset, including copies on BigQuery, Kaggle or AWS) as a source of GPS trajectories with points recorded along each trip. Not mentioning it passes; mentioning it while noting that it contains only pickup and drop-off information (times and locations or taxi zones) and no route or trajectory points also passes. FAIL if the answer recommends the NYC TLC trip records as raw GPS trajectory data or as meeting the requirement of timestamped points along each trip.

07-long-tailllm

PASS if the final answer recommends or lists at least one of these less known vehicle GPS trajectory datasets as a candidate: (a) the Rome taxi traces (CRAWDAD "roma/taxi", about 320 taxis in Rome, Italy, February 2014); (b) Grab-Posisi (the Grab GPS trajectory dataset from Singapore and Jakarta, collected in April 2019 at a 1-second sampling rate). FAIL if the answer mentions neither of them as a candidate.
